  - **Big rounded corners** (`rounded-[1.75rem]`/`[2rem]`, `rounded-full` pills)
    are a deliberate index.php-only deviation from `DESIGN.md`'s 12px card spec
    — the Floria look. Dark sections use **navy `dgt-blue-900`**, never
    near-black. **No gradient fills** (pinned rule kept — the hero scrim over a
    photo and the card gradient fallback are the only gradients, both
    pre-existing patterns).
  - **Motion (copied from Floria's framer-motion feel, later pass):**
    directional scroll-reveal variants — `.rv` (rise 38px) / `.rv-l` (in from
    left 52px) / `.rv-r` (in from right) / `.rv-s` (scale .94→1), plus the
    legacy `.reveal` alias — each eased `.8s cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1)`, once, via
    one IntersectionObserver; **per-item stagger** from inline
    `transition-delay` (hero children `.08/.16/.24s`, service cards by column
    `($i%3)*.09s`, process steps `$i*.1s`, MVW cols `$j*.1s`). The **nav is a
    1:1 port of Floria's `<Navbar>`** (spec pulled from its JS chunk): mount =
    framer spring `y:-100→0` (stiffness 100 / damping 20 → critically damped),
    sampled into the `@keyframes nav-drop` curve; then `transition-all
    duration-500` — past **`scrollY > 50`** the header padding goes `py-8`→`py-4`
    and `.nav-pill` goes from fully transparent (`px-2 py-2`, no bg/border/
    shadow/blur) to a frosted capsule (`px-6 py-3`, `bg rgba(16,47,81,.82)`,
    `backdrop-blur(12px)`, `border rgba(255,255,255,.12)`,
    `shadow 0 8px 30px rgba(16,47,81,.22)`). Palette-adapted only: navy frost,
    not Floria's `bg-white/70`, because the DGT nav text is white. JS just
    toggles `.scrolled`; the drop-in is pure CSS. The **hero visual** is a
    **moody dental still-life** — `assets/img/hero-a.jpg` was replaced with a
    dark, low-key photo of a dental mirror + probe catching side light on near
    black (Pexels #15613763, landscape crop), taking Floria's "one lit
    sculptural object on a dark ground" role (its dark-flower bg + bright bouquet
    accent). Full-bleed `.hero-bg` at `opacity-[0.62]` under a `100deg` navy
    scrim (heavy left `#102f51` → thin right `.25`, so the headline reads and the
    instruments show through). No bottom fade-to-white (removed 2026-09).
    `.hero-bg` holds a
    slight `brightness(.88) contrast(1.02)` at rest; **`#home:hover .hero-bg`
    lifts it to `brightness(1.08) contrast(1.09)` over `.8s`** — Floria's
    hero-accent `whileHover { filter:"brightness(.9) contrast(1.1)" }` moved onto
    the photo. `hero-kb` Ken Burns owns `transform` (so hover only touches
    `filter`). **The earlier tooth-SVG floating mark was removed** — the photo
    now carries the accent role and a second faint shape over it read as mud.
    Service-card image zooms to `scale(1.1)`
    over `.8s` on hover with the caption panel sliding up.
    The process section's backdrop image + heading slide in from the left
    (`.rv-l`); the image's "falls when scrolling" drift is **pure CSS** (sticky
    heading + tall absolutely-positioned image in an `overflow-x-clip` section,
    Floria's own technique — no JS, so it is not `prefers-reduced-motion` gated).
    The `.rv*` reveals, nav drop, Ken Burns etc. are all gated by
    `prefers-reduced-motion`. No scroll-linked
    wordmark, no `.about-stage` multi-layer reveal, no HUD brackets, no
    scroll-progress bar. A floating **`#toTop` "Back to Top" button** (fixed
    bottom-right, `z-60`) fades in past ~600px of scroll (JS toggles `.show`)
    and scrolls to top on click: a 50px navy (`dgt-blue-900`) rounded square
    with a white double border + up-arrow glyph that widens to a
    `dgt-teal-600` labelled pill on hover (arrow slides up and out, "Back to
    Top" rises in). Adapted from `uiverse.io/vinodjangid07`, recoloured to the
    DGT palette; `prefers-reduced-motion` keeps it a static icon square.
  - **Removed for good:** the clinic-facts strip (folded into the About `<dl>`
    + footer), the manifesto band, the standalone MVW band, the `// SERVICES`
    scroll-linked wordmark, the `.book-btn` Uiverse pill. (The services section
    churned through a staggered grid → a horizontal `#svcTrack` slider → its
    current static 2-column `.svc-card` grid + `#svcModal` detail popup,
    2026-09 — see the "Our Services" paragraph above.)
  - **The three section eyebrows above "Our services.", "How it works.", and
    "About us." — "What we do", "The visit", "Who we are" — were removed
    2026-09 at the user's request** (each was the `<p class="<?= $eyebrow ?>">`
    or equivalent one-off style directly above that section's `<h2>`); the
    heading is now the first element in each of those three header blocks,
    with its `mt-4`/`mt-6` top margin dropped since there's nothing above it to
    space away from any more. **The About Us copy was rewritten the same day**
    to the clinic's real founding/expansion history — DGT One was established
    by Dr. Maria Theresa G. Tamayo, DMD and Dr. Raffy D.G. Tamayo, DMD, grew
    from a single practice into a multi-branch network (Marikina, Cainta,
    Quezon City, Cavite, San Jose Del Monte Bulacan), and formally incorporated
    with the SEC in 2022 as **DGT ONE DENTAL CENTER CO.** — replacing the
    earlier generic "one of the best dental centers in the country" copy. Full
    text is in `index.php`'s `#about` section; treat it as a direct quote from
    the user, not marketing copy to further edit.
  - **"Meet the Team" carousel (`#team`, rebuilt 2026-09 from a literal
    build-spec the user pasted)** — a dark `bg-dgt-blue-900` section between
    About Us and the closing CTA. This exact section had already been built,
    debugged, and then fully removed once earlier in the same day (see the
    now-deleted history this replaced) after repeated "not working" reports on
    its wheel-scroll and infinite-loop mechanics; the user then pasted a
    fresh, generic build spec (dark theme, rounded cards, 4/2/1 responsive
    breakpoints, overlay arrows, pagination dots, click-drag + touch-swipe,
    CSS scroll-snap) asking for it rebuilt from scratch. The rebuild follows
    that spec's mechanics but **not** its literal colours — `bg-neutral-900`/
    `#1a1a1a` is a raw hex outside the DGT palette, so it was adapted to
    `dgt-blue-900` (dark sections are always navy, never near-black, same
    rule as every other dark section on this page) — and keeps two decisions
    already settled earlier the same day: the same **static PHP `$team`
    array** (not a `services`-style DB table — deliberately not re-litigated)
    with the same roster (Dr. Maria Theresa G. Tamayo, DMD and Dr. Raffy D.G.
    Tamayo, DMD, both "Founder & Dentist"; Catherine Ramirez, "Receptionist /
    Cashier"; Janette Ganolon, "Assistant Dentist"; photos still placeholder —
    a `photo` key expected under `assets/img/`, `null` renders the same navy-
    gradient + person-icon placeholder used for a `services` row with no
    image), and **first-name-only captions** (`$teamFirstName()`, unchanged).
    Simpler than the previous build in one respect: no infinite loop, no
    triple-rendered clone/real/clone track, no wheel-to-horizontal-scroll
    hijack — none of those were in the new spec, and the infinite-loop
    mechanic in particular had been the single biggest source of bugs in the
    earlier build, so it was deliberately not reintroduced.
    - **Layout:** `#teamTrack` is a `flex snap-x snap-mandatory overflow-x-auto`
      strip of `.team-card`s sized `w-full md:w-[calc(50%-10px)]
      lg:w-[calc(25%-15px)]` — 1 per view on mobile, 2 on tablet, 4 on desktop,
      matching the spec's breakpoints exactly. Each card is a `h-56
      rounded-lg` photo box (`object-cover`, or the navy-gradient placeholder)
      with the first name in bold centered below — no infinite-scroll padding
      trick this time, so `scrollLeft` starts at `0` (well, `4`, the track's
      own `px-1` — same scroll-snap padding quirk documented for the previous
      build) with genuine start/end edges.
    - **Navigation:** `#teamPrev`/`#teamNext` are circular ghost buttons
      (`bg-white/10`, blurred, matching the spec's "overlay arrow buttons"
      ask) disabled at the genuine start/end of the track (`scrollLeft <= 1` /
      `scrollLeft >= maxScrollLeft - 1`) — a real disabled state this time,
      not the previous build's "only disabled when nothing fits at all"
      infinite-loop logic. `#teamDots` are built/refreshed the same way as
      before (`goToPage()`/`refreshDots()`), and both arrow and dot clicks
      request `behavior:'smooth'` explicitly per call (`prefers-reduced-motion`
      swaps it to `'auto'`) rather than relying on a CSS `scroll-smooth` class
      on the track — deliberately, see the drag paragraph below.
    - **Touch swipe needs no JS at all** — `overflow-x-auto` + `snap-x` is
      natively swipeable on any touchscreen; the spec's "support touch/swipe
      gestures" requirement is satisfied by the CSS alone.
    - **Click-and-drag scrolling (the one genuinely new mechanic in this
      rebuild) applies the wheel-scroll lesson from earlier the same day
      *before* hitting the same bug, not after.** The earlier build's
      wheel-scroll bug was `snap-mandatory` synchronously reverting any small
      `scrollLeft` write back to the current card the instant it's applied —
      a per-frame mouse-drag delta is exactly as small as a wheel tick, so it
      would hit the identical bug immediately if built naively. The drag
      handler (`mousedown`/`mousemove`/`mouseup`/`mouseleave` on `#teamTrack`)
      sets `track.style.scrollSnapType = 'none'` for the duration of the drag
      and clears it again on release, the same suspend-and-restore pattern —
      built in from the start this time instead of discovered by a bug
      report. `cursor-grab` on the track / `cursor: grabbing` while dragging
      give the expected desktop affordance; a `moved` flag guards against a
      drag that actually moved the track also registering as a click on
      whatever's under the pointer when released (nothing clickable lives
      inside a card today, but it's cheap insurance).
    - **A real verification pitfall, caught and fixed before shipping:** the
      first headless-Chrome check of the drag handler showed `scrollLeft`
      not moving at all during a synthetic drag, with `window.scrollY`
      drifting upward by hundreds of pixels across the same sequence — at a
      glance this looked exactly like the earlier wheel-scroll bug reappearing
      in a new form. It wasn't the site: the test navigated straight to
      `...#team` and then called `scrollIntoView()` to bring the section into
      view, but `<html class="scroll-smooth">` (`scroll-behavior: smooth`)
      makes *that* call animate too, and jumping ~6,000px down the page
      doesn't finish inside a fixed 300ms wait — so the page was still
      gliding into position underneath the test's fixed pixel coordinates
      while it dispatched mouse events, and the track visibly drifted out
      from under them mid-drag. Fixed by polling `window.scrollY` in the test
      until it stopped changing before computing click coordinates, not by
      changing anything in the site's own code — confirmed by re-running the
      identical drag sequence once the scroll position was genuinely settled:
      `scrollLeft` moved by the correct amount, `window.scrollY` stayed
      perfectly flat throughout, and it glided to the nearest card on
      release. Worth remembering for any future test against a page with
      `scroll-smooth` on `<html>`: a `scrollIntoView()`/anchor-jump used only
      to stage the test can itself still be mid-flight when the test's next
      step assumes it has already landed.
    - **Immediately re-extended, same day, to add the infinite loop back in**
      — the user pasted a second, more specific spec explicitly asking for
      endless looping (wrap seamlessly past the last/first card) *combined*
      with the just-built click-and-drag. This is a deliberate re-merge of
      the two hardest-won pieces from this section's whole history: the
      triple-rendered clone/real/clone track + `normalize()` silent-wrap
      technique from the very first infinite-loop build (`setWidth()`,
      `padL()`, `currentPage()`'s modulo wrap, `goToPage()`/`stepBy()`'s
      absolute-vs-relative distinction — all restored verbatim, since that
      logic was already correct and had nothing to do with the wheel-scroll
      bug that got it removed) plus the click-and-drag handler from the
      no-loop rebuild just above (unchanged — `scrollSnapType` suspend/
      restore on `mousedown`/`mouseup`/`mouseleave`). The two combine cleanly
      with no new interaction bug: a drag's per-frame `scrollLeft` writes
      still land freely (snap suspended for the gesture), and once released,
      the existing 80ms scroll-debounce's `normalize()` call silently wraps
      the position back into the real-set range exactly as it already does
      after a native swipe or an arrow/dot click — dragging across a clone
      boundary needed no special-casing at all. Arrows are unconditionally
      enabled now (no `disabled` state) since there is always another
      direction to loop into. Verified with headless Chrome using the fixed
      settle-scroll-first test methodology from the paragraph above: stepping
      forward twice past the last real page lands back at page 0 (dot 0 goes
      active) instead of dead-ending, stepping backward three times from page
      0 wraps to the last real page and back correctly, and a drag on the
      tripled track moves the expected distance and settles cleanly with
      `scroll-snap-type` restored afterward.
    - **Rebuilt again, same day, off the native-scroll approach entirely** —
      the user reported the result "jumpy and misaligned" and pasted a third
      spec asking for a `transform: translateX()`-driven track instead of
      `scrollLeft`/CSS scroll-snap. This is the right call, not just a
      preference: every bug this section had (the wheel-scroll `pageCount()`
      gate, `snap-mandatory` reverting small `scrollLeft` writes, `scroll-
      smooth` racing programmatic writes) traced back to fighting the
      browser's own native-scroll physics; a transform-driven track has no
      native scroll state to fight at all. `#teamTrack` is no longer
      `overflow-x-auto`/`scrollLeft` — it now sits inside a plain
      `overflow-hidden` wrapper (`#teamViewport`) and is positioned purely by
      `track.style.transform = 'translateX(-pos)'`, where `pos` (a plain JS
      number) is the script's sole source of truth for horizontal position;
      `TRANSITION = 'transform .5s cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1)'` is toggled on
      the element's `transition` property per move (on for an animated
      arrow/dot/drag-commit step, `'none'` for an instant teleport or the
      post-loop correction), matching the spec's "smooth transition-transform
      duration-500 ease-out" ask. The triple-render clone/real/clone
      structure and the "animate into the clone, then instantly teleport back
      into the real range once the move visually finishes" infinite-loop
      technique both carry over conceptually unchanged from the scroll-based
      build, just re-expressed in transform terms (`setWidth()`/`offsetForPage()`
      compute px targets; `normalizeAfterMove()` is `normalize()`'s direct
      equivalent, run after a transition/instant-move instead of after a
      scroll-debounce). Visual details from this pass's spec: `aspect-video`
      (16:9) image boxes replacing the fixed `h-56`; each card carries its own
      `w-full sm:w-1/2 lg:w-1/4 px-3` (the spec's literal Tailwind classes,
      including its `sm:`/`lg:` breakpoints rather than this file's usual
      `md:`/`lg:` tablet pairing, since the user typed the class names
      directly) instead of the earlier `calc()`-based width/gap combination —
      the per-card `px-3` padding is what creates the visual gutter now, so
      the track carries no separate `gap` utility; the name is
      `mt-3 text-center font-semibold text-white` (dropped to `font-semibold`
      per this spec, was `font-bold`). Colours again adapted off the pasted
      spec's raw hex to the DGT palette (still `dgt-blue-900`, never a literal
      `#111111`).
      - **Drag is now transform-based too, with a commit/snap-back
        threshold** — `mousedown`/`mousemove`/`mouseup`/`mouseleave` (desktop)
        and `touchstart`/`touchmove`/`touchend`/`touchcancel` (mobile/tablet)
        share one drag engine: while dragging, `pos` tracks the pointer 1:1
        with `transition:'none'` (no animation lag while your finger/cursor is
        still moving); on release, a drag past ~15% of one page's width
        commits to the next/previous page (animated), anything shorter
        animates back to the page the drag started on — the classic swipe-
        carousel feel the spec asked for ("Drag Physics"). Because the track
        is `overflow-hidden` now (not `overflow-x-auto`), **touch swipe is no
        longer free from native scrolling and needed real touch handlers** —
        `[touch-action:pan-y]` on the track tells the browser to keep native
        *vertical* page scroll working while handing *horizontal* gestures to
        this code, so a visitor can still scroll the page normally with a
        vertical swipe that merely starts over the carousel.
      - **A real, serious bug found and fixed during verification — not a
        test artifact this time.** `animateTo()` completed a move by waiting
        for the track's `transitionend` event to clear an `animating` guard
        flag (itself there to stop overlapping animations from rapid
        clicks/drags). But a `transitionend` event only fires when the CSS
        property's value actually *changes* — clicking the dot for the page
        that's already active (a completely ordinary, expected user action)
        requests a target identical to the current position, so `transform`
        never changes, `transitionend` never fires, and `animating` stayed
        `true` forever — permanently freezing every later arrow click, dot
        click, drag, and touch swipe on the whole carousel. Caught during
        headless-Chrome verification when a routine "reset to page 0" dot
        click before a drag test silently broke every subsequent interaction
        in the same run. Fixed two ways: (1) `animateTo()` now checks whether
        the requested target is already within 0.5px of the current position
        and, if so, resolves immediately without waiting on any event at all;
        (2) a defensive fallback `setTimeout` (700ms, longer than the 500ms
        `TRANSITION` duration — same margin convention as the login page's own
        "launch" transition fallback) force-resolves the pending move if
        `transitionend` still doesn't fire for some *other* reason (e.g. a
        backgrounded tab throttling transitions). Both `clearPending()` calls
        cancel this timer too, so a fresh move started before an old one
        finished can't leave a stray timer firing late against a since-
        replaced move. Re-verified after the fix: the exact previously-
        broken sequence (dot click to the already-active page, immediately
        followed by a mouse drag) now works, and a second full pass confirmed
        drag tracks the pointer live, commits past the threshold, snaps back
        under it, and a simulated touch swipe advances a page correctly too.
      - **A second, unrelated test-script mistake surfaced during the same
        debugging pass, worth remembering for any future work on a
        `transform`-positioned element:** the first drag/touch test computed
        its on-screen click coordinates from `#teamTrack`'s own
        `getBoundingClientRect()` — but `#teamTrack` is the *whole* 12-card
        flex row (all three clone/real/clone repeats), continuously shifted
        left by `translateX(-pos)`, so its layout rect sits mostly *outside*
        the visible viewport (`left` came back strongly negative in the
        failing run). `#teamViewport`, the plain `overflow-hidden` wrapper
        around it, is what actually reflects the visible on-screen area,
        since it clips the oversized, transformed track to it. Once the test
        was pointed at `#teamViewport`'s rect instead, the drag/touch
        coordinates landed correctly and every interaction worked as
        expected. A real user's click/tap is never affected by this — they
        can only ever click within what's visibly on screen to begin with —
        but any future synthetic (CDP or otherwise) coordinate calculation
        against a `transform`-positioned scroll/carousel track should always
        measure the *clipping viewport* element, never the moving track
        itself.
    - **Rebuilt a third time onto per-CARD stepping (2026-09, replacing the
      per-PAGE version above) after the user reported it "broken when
      navigating right (it resets or locks up)"** while the opposite arrow
      "only works when sliding left." The per-page version nudged `pos` by a
      flat `perPage() * step()` per arrow click, which happened to be
      arithmetically consistent for this exact 4-member roster at every
      breakpoint (4/2/1 cards fit per row, always evenly dividing 4) — but it
      had a real, user-visible failure mode that arithmetic correctness
      didn't fix: whenever every card already fit in the viewport at once
      (`pageCount() === 1`, the common case on a normal desktop-width
      browser window with only 4 members), clicking Next/Prev animated a
      full lap all the way around the clone loop back to the *exact same*
      view — nothing about the visible order changed, which reads exactly
      like "it resets" or "it's stuck." Fixed by stepping exactly **one
      card** at a time instead of one "page" of however many cards fit:
      `realIndex` (always kept in `[0, REAL_COUNT)`) replaces `pageIndex`,
      `stepBy(dir)` moves `pos` by a single `step()` and `normalizeAfterMove()`
      only ever has to correct for one card's width in either direction.
      This always visibly rotates the row by one position regardless of how
      many cards fit on screen — even at full-desktop width, Next now
      visibly reorders Maria→Raffy→Catherine→Janette→Maria (a real,
      perceptible change each click) instead of a pointless round trip.
      Dots still represent pages (groups of `perPage` cards, clamped the
      same "avoid a short last page" way) purely as a position indicator;
      clicking one still jumps straight to that page's first card.
      Re-verified with headless Chrome across three widths (1440/800/500,
      giving 1/2/4 cards-per-view respectively) reading which real card sits
      leftmost in the viewport (not just the raw pixel offset) after each
      click: 6 consecutive Next clicks correctly cycle
      Maria→Raffy→Catherine→Janette→Maria→Raffy→Catherine at every width
      with no reset/misalignment, the same in reverse for Prev, and 8
      rapid-fire clicks with no wait between them (stress-testing the
      `animating`-flag stuck-forever class of bug from the previous fix)
      settle cleanly and stay responsive to a further click afterward.
    - **A second, independent, previously-unnoticed bug was caught by that
      same re-verification, in the drag/touch-swipe code, not the arrows**
      — inherited unchanged from the very first transform-based build (the
      per-page version had the identical formula, so this bug predates the
      per-card rewrite above and was not the thing the user had reported).
      `dragEnd()` computed `var dx = dragStartPos - pos;` to decide which
      way to commit a drag past the threshold, but since `dragMove()` sets
      `pos = dragStartPos - dx` (its own, local `dx = clientX - dragStartX`),
      algebraically `dragEnd`'s `dx` reduces to exactly
      `clientX - dragStartX` again — i.e. positive when the pointer moved
      *right*. Every other function in the carousel (`stepBy`,
      `normalizeAfterMove`, `animateTo`) uses the opposite convention,
      "increasing `pos` = forward = `dir === 1`" — so a drag to the *left*
      (which visibly increases `pos` during the drag itself, per
      `dragMove()`'s own formula, and is the standard "swipe left = next"
      gesture) was committing with `dir === -1` on release: the exact
      opposite of the direction the content had just visibly been moving
      throughout the drag. Caught by strengthening the test to read which
      named card actually ends up leftmost (not just that `tx` changed and
      "a commit happened") — a drag/swipe left from Maria landed on Janette
      (the *previous* card) instead of Raffy (the *next* one). Fixed by
      redefining `dragEnd`'s `dx` as `pos - dragStartPos` (positive when
      `pos` increased, matching every other function's convention) rather
      than re-deriving the pointer's raw screen-space delta under a
      different sign. Re-verified: a mouse drag left now correctly commits
      forward (Maria→Raffy), drag right commits backward (Raffy→Maria), and
      a simulated touch swipe left matches the mouse-drag result exactly.
    - **Replaced outright with Swiper.js (2026-09, final rebuild)** — the
      user reported the per-card fix above "still failing" for the specific
      case where the roster (4 members) exactly fills one desktop-width
      view, and asked to delete the whole hand-rolled script and rebuild on
      Swiper instead, pasting an exact `loop:true` / `navigation` /
      `pagination` / `breakpoints` config to use. The entire custom IIFE
      (transform math, drag/touch handlers, dot rendering, all of it) is
      gone; Swiper's own `loop` mode, `grabCursor`/touch handling and
      bundled A11y module now own everything that code used to hand-roll,
      and each of those was a source of its own bug across the three prior
      rebuilds. **Swiper is vendored locally, never loaded from the
      `jsdelivr` CDN the user's snippet used** — this project's hard rule
      (see "Styling: migrating to Tailwind" above) is that third-party JS is
      always vendored, the existing precedent being
      `assets/js/vendor/html2pdf.bundle.min.js`. Downloaded once and
      committed as `assets/js/vendor/swiper-bundle.min.js` +
      `assets/css/vendor/swiper-bundle.min.css` (a new `assets/css/vendor/`
      directory — there was no other vendored CSS in the app, since
      `assets/css/style.css` itself was deleted in the Tailwind migration).
      `index.php`'s `<head>` links the CSS after `tailwind.php`; the JS
      loads via a plain `<script src>` right before the page's own inline
      `<script>` block, which then does `new Swiper('#teamSwiper', {...})`
      (guarded on `window.Swiper` existing). The markup is now the plain
      Swiper shape — `#teamSwiper.swiper > .swiper-wrapper > .swiper-slide`
      per team member, rendered once (no manual clone/repeat markup — that
      was the old build's own infinite-loop technique, now entirely
      Swiper's job) — keeping the same photo/placeholder `aspect-video` box
      and `<h3>` name markup as before. `#teamPrev`/`#teamNext` and
      `#teamPagination` are still our own DGT-styled elements (circular
      `bg-white/10 backdrop-blur` buttons with the same inline SVG chevrons;
      a plain pagination `<div>`), wired in via the `navigation`/`pagination`
      `el` options — Swiper's own default button chrome is unused, only its
      bullet click/active-state behaviour. The pagination bullets are
      reskinned to the DGT dark-section dot look via the
      `--swiper-pagination-*` CSS custom properties (Swiper's supported
      theming path, since it emits no classes a Tailwind arbitrary variant
      could target) plus one `.swiper-pagination-bullet-active { width:
      20px }` override, scoped in a `<style>` block inside the section.
      Breakpoints match the user's spec (0/640/1024 → 1/2/4 slides per
      view, `spaceBetween` 16/20/24) and `speed` drops to `0` under
      `prefers-reduced-motion` (checked once, same `reduce` flag the rest
      of the page's motion already uses) rather than a separate code path.
      - **The "4 members exactly fill one desktop view" case is not
        actually fixable by making Next "do something" — it's the correct,
        standard state for a carousel to have nothing left to page to**,
        confirmed by inspecting Swiper's own instance state directly
        (`swiper.isBeginning`/`isEnd`/`allowSlideNext`/`allowSlidePrev` were
        all `true`/`false`/`false`/`false` at that breakpoint — Swiper
        itself agrees there is nothing to page to when `slidesPerView`
        equals the real slide count). Swiper's answer to this is to hide
        navigation/pagination entirely via its own `.swiper-button-lock` /
        `.swiper-pagination-lock` classes (a plain `display:none` in its
        vendored CSS) rather than leave the arrows visibly inert — which is
        the outcome that actually matches what "smooth… without visual
        jumps" should mean at that width: no dead-looking button, nothing to
        click, one clean row showing everyone at once.
      - **A real bug found during verification: that lock-hide didn't
        work.** `getComputedStyle` on `#teamNext` at desktop width showed
        `display: flex`, not `none`, so the button stayed visible (though
        with `pointer-events` still `auto` and `opacity` still `1` — visibly
        indistinguishable from "enabled but broken", exactly the "resets or
        locks up" complaint, just relocated one level down). Root cause:
        `#teamNext`/`#teamPagination` also carry Tailwind's own `flex`
        utility class, which has equal CSS specificity to Swiper's
        `.swiper-button-lock`/`.swiper-pagination-lock` rules — and the
        Tailwind Play CDN injects its generated `<style>` into the document
        *after* everything already parsed (it's a remote script that
        fetches, then scans the DOM and appends its own stylesheet), landing
        later than Swiper's `<link>` in the cascade and winning the tie on
        pure document order. Fixed with an explicit, `!important` override
        in the section's own `<style>` block —
        `#teamPrev.swiper-button-lock, #teamNext.swiper-button-lock,
        #teamPagination.swiper-pagination-lock { display: none !important;
        }` — verified afterward with `getComputedStyle`/`offsetParent` that
        the controls are now genuinely hidden (not just visually, but
        actually unreachable/non-tabbable) at a width where the whole
        roster fits, while remaining fully visible and functional at every
        narrower breakpoint. **Any future vendored CSS library in this app
        should assume the same risk** — a Tailwind utility class sharing a
        selector's specificity with a library's own state class can silently
        win, since Tailwind's CDN styles are injected late; give the
        library's state classes an explicit, scoped override rather than
        trusting its own bundled CSS to win the cascade unassisted.
      - Re-verified end-to-end with headless Chrome after the fix: arrow
        Next/Prev cycle correctly and symmetrically
        (Maria→Raffy→Catherine→Janette→Maria…) at 800px and 500px widths (2
        and 1 slides per view), survive 8 rapid-fire clicks with no wait
        between them and stay responsive afterward, and are confirmed fully
        hidden (`display:none`, `offsetParent === null`) at 1440px where all
        4 already fit; pagination dots jump straight to the clicked member;
        mouse drag and touch swipe both commit in the correct direction
        (drag/swipe left → forward, right → backward) when tested on **fresh
        page loads per gesture** — a same-session second synthetic drag
        immediately after a first one intermittently failed to register at
        all under CDP (confirmed via a temporary pointerdown/mousedown/
        touchstart listener dump showing zero events reached the element),
        which is a synthetic-input timing artifact of firing two full
        scripted gesture cycles back-to-back with no real hardware pacing
        between them, not a site defect — isolating each gesture to its own
        fresh load made every one pass. No console errors in any pass.
    - **Follow-up (2026-09, same day): forced the loop/nav to work on
      desktop too, where the 4-member roster exactly fills
      `slidesPerView:4`.** The user reported the Swiper build above "still
      failing" there and asked for `loop:true` + `loopAdditionalSlides: 4` +
      `watchOverflow: false` (plus `navigation.hideOnClick: false` and a
      script-path/404 sanity check) — reversing the intent of the lock-hide
      fix from the initial Swiper rebuild, which had deliberately hidden the
      controls in that exact case as "the correct behaviour, nothing to page
      to." The user's answer, via `AskUserQuestion`, was that they want
      genuine looping there instead, not a hidden control. Two real findings
      from testing this literally:
      - **`loopAdditionalSlides: 4` alone did not fix desktop and actively
        broke the tablet/mobile breakpoints that already worked.** Verified
        with headless Chrome: at 800px and 500px (previously a clean,
        symmetric Maria→Raffy→Catherine→Janette→Maria… cycle both
        directions), Next got stuck partway through — e.g. `next#3` onward
        repeating the same name instead of continuing the cycle — and Prev
        got stuck on the very first card. Root cause: `loopAdditionalSlides`
        only adds extra *cloned* buffer slides around the loop's rendering
        swath; it doesn't change what actually drives Swiper's
        `isBeginning`/`isEnd`/`allowSlideNext`/`allowSlidePrev` state, which
        is purely "does the real slide count exceed `slidesPerView`" — with
        only 4 real slides and `slidesPerView:4` at desktop, that's `true`/
        `true`/`false`/`false` regardless of any loop-related *option*.
        Reverted after confirming the regression.
      - **The only fix that actually works is having more real slides than
        the largest `slidesPerView` at every breakpoint** — so the markup
        now renders `$team` **twice** as real Swiper slides (8 total for 4
        members; only the first copy is exposed to assistive tech via
        `aria-hidden` on the second, the same convention this section's
        earlier hand-rolled clone/real/clone builds already used, so a
        screen reader still hears each person once). `watchOverflow: false`
        is kept too (belt-and-braces — with 8 real slides the lock state
        shouldn't arise anyway) alongside the `!important` CSS override from
        the initial Swiper rebuild, which now *forces* `.swiper-button-lock`/
        `.swiper-pagination-lock` to `display: flex` instead of `none`
        (flipped from that pass's original hide-it direction) as a second
        line of defence in case a future Swiper version re-locks under some
        other condition. `navigation.hideOnClick: false` was added too — a
        real, confirmed-in-the-vendored-bundle Swiper option, but it only
        controls an unrelated lightbox-style "tap the slide to hide the
        chrome" behaviour that already defaults to off, so it isn't actually
        what was hiding anything here; kept since it was explicitly asked
        for and is harmless. **Trade-off accepted by the user:** pagination
        now shows 8 dots (one per real slide, two full cycles through the
        roster) instead of 4 unique-member dots — raised directly via
        `AskUserQuestion` alongside two alternatives (capping desktop at 3
        slides per view instead of 4; or keeping the earlier hidden-controls
        behaviour), and this option ("duplicate roster to 8 real slides")
        is what the user picked. Re-verified end-to-end with headless
        Chrome after the fix: Next/Prev now cycle correctly and
        symmetrically at **all three** widths including 1440px (previously
        the one broken case), 8 rapid-fire clicks settle cleanly and stay
        responsive afterward, drag and touch swipe both commit the correct
        direction, pagination dot-click jumps correctly, and `assets/js/
        vendor/swiper-bundle.min.js` / `assets/css/vendor/swiper-bundle.min.css`
        both confirmed serving `200` (not `404`) via direct `curl`. No
        console errors.
    - **Keyboard arrow-key navigation (2026-09, same day)** — the user
      reported "the arrow keys is not working." Confirmed directly on the
      live `swiper` instance: `swiper.params.keyboard.enabled` was `false`
      — the keyboard module ships inside the vendored bundle but, like every
      Swiper module, does nothing until its config block turns it on, and
      the original init (ported from the user's own pasted config) never
      included one. Added `keyboard: { enabled: true, onlyInViewport: true }`
      — `onlyInViewport` (Swiper's default, kept explicit) means physical
      Left/Right presses only drive this carousel while it's actually
      scrolled into view, so they don't hijack arrow-key input used
      elsewhere on the page. Verified with a real `Input.dispatchKeyEvent`
      (not a JS-level `.slideNext()` call) that `ArrowRight` now advances
      Maria→Raffy. The user also reported the arrows "disappear" or fade in
      opacity on hover — inspected `#teamNext`'s computed `opacity`
      (`1`), `display` (`flex`) and `background-color` before vs. during a
      real dispatched hover, and it behaved exactly as designed (the
      `hover:bg-white/20` utility only brightens the translucent white fill
      from 10% to 20% alpha; nothing fades or hides). This could not be
      reproduced from `opacity`/`display`/`background-color` alone, so a
      follow-up question narrowed it to the Prev/Next buttons specifically
      — which pointed at the one hover-triggered property that check hadn't
      covered: `box-shadow`. **Confirmed real bug, found by checking that
      property too**: `tailwind.php`'s app-wide
      `button:not(:disabled):not(.tab):not(.nav-item):not(.modal-close):hover
      { box-shadow: 0 2px 10px rgba(15,28,46,.14) }` (a "lift" meant for
      buttons sitting on the white cards used everywhere else in the app)
      fires on these two plain `<button>` elements as well, since they don't
      carry any of that selector's exemption classes. On a **white** card a
      dark, low-opacity navy shadow reads as depth/lift; on **this**
      section's own dark `bg-dgt-blue-900` background, the exact same dark
      navy shadow around a translucent white circle instead blends into the
      backdrop, washing the button out right where the user's eye is — which
      is exactly what "disappears" / "opacity lower" on hover looks like,
      even though the button's own `opacity`/`background-color` never
      actually change (verified `box-shadow: none` at rest → `rgba(15, 28,
      46, 0.14) 0px 2px 10px 0px` on a real dispatched hover, before the
      fix). Fixed with a two-line scoped override in the section's own
      `<style>` block — `#teamPrev:hover, #teamNext:hover { box-shadow: none
      !important; }` — rather than touching the global rule, which is
      correct for every light-card button elsewhere in the app. Re-verified:
      `box-shadow` now reads `none` during a real hover, and the intended
      `hover:bg-white/20` brighten-only feedback is unaffected. **Any future
      circular/translucent control placed on a dark section should get the
      same treatment** — the global hover-lift rule assumes a light card
      underneath it by default.
  - **No testimonials / newsletter** — the user has no real quotes; fabricated
    ones are a slop tell (`taste-skill` §9 / §4.10). Skipped by design.
  - **SEO baseline** (kept from the prior pass): `<meta name="description">`,
    `<link rel="canonical">`, Open Graph + `twitter:card`, a `Dentist`
    **JSON-LD** block (real NAP + `Mo-Sa 10:00-17:00`, `json_encode`),
    `<title>` separator `|`.
  `login.php` / `register.php` / `reset_password.php` / `terms.php` still legacy.
- **Done — `modules/patient/appointments.php` (first per-role module page,
  2026-09):** a visual-polish-only pass (user-scoped explicitly, no
  layout/behaviour change) onto `dgt-*` tokens — the tab bar now follows the
  `tab` component spec and is a real `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"` with
  arrow-key nav; the primary CTA (Request Appointment / Continue to Payment /
  Submit Request) is `dgt-teal-600`; `includes/patient_booking_style.php`'s
  hand-written hex palette was remapped to the DGT equivalents (status colours
  — pending/approved/rescheduled/cancelled — deliberately left on the legacy
  `ok`/`warn`/`danger`/`info` hexes, matching `app.js`'s `BADGE_C` everywhere
  else until that file gets its own conversion pass). Full detail in
  `DESIGN.md`'s migration-status section.
- **Next:** finish shell → other admin pages → rest of the per-role module
  pages → finish public/auth → `app.js` shared components.

Keep `DESIGN.md` and this section in sync as the rollout proceeds.

### Styling: migrating to Tailwind

The user has decided to move the **whole app** to **Tailwind CSS via the Play CDN** (`includes/tailwind.php` — a `<script src="https://cdn.tailwindcss.com">` plus an inline `tailwind.config` with the palette tokens). No build step; it JIT-compiles in the browser. This reverses the old "hand-written CSS, never a CDN" rule — style new/edited pages with Tailwind utility classes and pull `includes/tailwind.php` into their `<head>`.

**Migration COMPLETE.** `assets/css/style.css` is deleted; nothing links it. Every page loads only `includes/tailwind.php` (+ the relevant scoped `<style>` partial where one applies). `tailwind.php` now also carries a small `<style>` block — the `:root` colour custom properties (kept in sync with the Tailwind tokens as a safety net; no `.php`/`.js` still references `var(--*)` after Group 4, so this block is currently dead but cheap to keep) plus a few surviving global rules: `.field-err` / `.field-err.ok` and `input/select/textarea.is-invalid` (the validation marker classes that `wireRefCheck()` / `wirePhoneField()` and the booking / walk-in / billing forms toggle app-wide), and a base interaction feel for `button, [role="button"], a` — a `.16s` `transition` (background / border / colour / shadow / brightness / transform), a soft `box-shadow` lift on `button:hover`, and a `translateY(1px) scale(.985)` press on `:active` (all with a `prefers-reduced-motion` off-switch; `.nav-item` / `.tab` / `.modal-close` opt out). This is so the row-action buttons — Edit / View / Details / Approve / Archive … — have visible, eased hover/press feedback, since their ghost style only shifts to a near-white fill on its own and none carry a `transition-*` utility.

Migration status:

- **Group 1 — public + auth pages (done):** `index.php`, `login.php`, `register.php`, `reset_password.php`, `terms.php`, `includes/terms_content.php`. No `style.css` link; they `require includes/tailwind.php`. Auth modals (forgot-password, T&C) toggle `hidden`/`flex` classes (not the `hidden` attribute); their JS sets Tailwind colour classes on status text. `includes/terms_content.php` is bare semantic HTML styled by the `TC_BODY_CLASSES` constant it defines (`[&_h3]:` / `[&_p]:` child selectors) — `terms.php` and the register modal each capture its output via `ob_start()` and apply that class string to the wrapper.
- **Group 2 — app shell (done):** `includes/layout.php` (`<head>` pulls only `tailwind.php`), `includes/sidebar.php`, `includes/header.php`. The sidebar keeps `id="sidebar"` + the `nav-item` / `active` marker classes (nav.js toggles `.active` and scopes its query as `#sidebar .nav-item[data-nav]`; sidebar.php styles it with `[&.active]:` Tailwind variants) and the mobile `.open` class (`max-nav:[&.open]:translate-x-0`). `nav:` is a custom 820px breakpoint in `tailwind.php`. Sidebar + header carry `print:hidden`, the content wrapper `print:ml-0`, replacing the old `@media print` rules that targeted `.sidebar` / `.app-main` / `.topbar`. The `.page` / `.page.page-wide` max-widths (1180 / 1560) are now emitted by `layout.php` as `mx-auto max-w-[1180px]` / `max-w-[1560px]` utilities on the `$pageClass` wrapper (the `page` / `page-wide` tokens stay as inert markers); a page that sets `$PAGE_FULL = true` instead gets `page page-wide w-full` — no `mx-auto`, no max-width — and **every `modules/**` page now does** (the user wanted the whole app to fill the viewport, not sit in a centred strip), so the 1180 / 1560 branches are currently only the fallback for a page that sets neither flag. `#modalRoot` / `#toastRoot` carry their layout utilities inline in `layout.php`; `#modalRoot` shows/hides via the `hidden` attribute (`openModal` sets `r.hidden = false`), and both keep their `modal-root` / `toast-root` marker class only so `invoice_style.php`'s `@media print { .modal-root, .toast-root { display:none } }` still hides them.
- **Group 3 — module pages (done):** all 24 `modules/**` pages converted to Tailwind utilities. Both the `$PAGE_CONTENT` markup **and** the `$PAGE_SCRIPT`-generated HTML were converted. Conventions established:
  - Reusable per-file PHP class-string vars (`$btnPrimary`, `$tableCls` with `[&_th]:` / `[&_td]:` / `[&_.amt]:` child selectors, `$compactCls`, `$tabCls`, …), mirrored as JS `const`s inside the nowdoc (`BTN`, `BTN_SM`, `PRIMARY`/`GHOST`/`OK`/`WARN`/`DANGER`/`NEUTRAL` colour suffixes, `FG`/`LBL`/`INP`/`HINT`, `BADGE` + soft-colour suffixes).
  - `tailwind.php` carries `-soft` variants of `ok` / `warn` / `danger` / `info` for badge + metric tints.
  - Marker classes kept where JS toggles/queries them, styled via arbitrary variants: `.tab` (`[&.active]:`), `.sortable`/`.sorted`, `.nav-item`.
  - `app.js` / `charts.js` component styling was converted in Group 4 (below); only `.field-err` / `.is-invalid` survive as global rules, in `tailwind.php`.
  - Three self-contained visual widgets were **relocated** (not utility-translated) into scoped `<style>` partials emitted inside `$PAGE_CONTENT` — applied on full render *and* the nav.js partial swap, all now **deleted from `style.css`**:
    - `includes/records_chart_style.php` — FDI tooth chart + `.rec-*` clinical panels (dentist + receptionist records). The dentist Patient Details modal is **full-width stacked**, not a side-by-side split: `.rec-clinical` is a 3-col (→2→1 responsive) grid of all the clinical text fields + DOB/Gender + the two full-width textareas, then `.rec-chart` (the tooth board + legend) sits on its own full-width row below — no tall skinny side column, no gap. `#modalRoot > [role="dialog"]:has(.rec-split)` widens that modal to `min(1180px, 100%-32px) !important` (beats openModal's `!w-[min(880px…)]` wide utility) so the board has room; `.tooth-board` flexes teeth to fill it (`.tooth-box` `max-width: 60px`, primary `74px`).
    - `includes/calendar_style.php` — `.cal-*` month grid (dentist + receptionist + cashier calendars). Markup change: the "Today" button is keyed `.cal-today-btn` (was `.cal-toolbar .btn`). Layout: `.cal-layout` is a 2-col grid — the month grid (`.cal-main`, col 1 / row 1) and the day-detail card (`#dayCard`, col 1 / row 2) stack in the left column with `#dayCard` now a grid child of `.cal-layout` (not a sibling after it), while `.cal-side` spans `grid-row: 1 / span 2` in col 2 so a tall "Upcoming Appointments" list never pushes `#dayCard` down or opens a gap under the calendar; `#calUpcoming` also caps at `max-height: 380px` and scrolls.
    - `includes/patient_booking_style.php` — the patient Appointments page's 3-step booking wizard (`.wiz-*`), reservation-fee step (`.pay-summary*` incl. `.pay-summary .row-between` layout, `.pm-*`, `.rcpt-drop*`), "Submitted" status card (`.submitted*` incl. `.submitted .kv` grid + `.submitted .alert-warn|-ok|-error`, `.spin` → renamed keyframe `dgt-spin`) and appointment-card grid (`.appt-c*`, `.tab-n`). The `.row-between` / `.kv` / `.alert*` primitives that `appointments.php`'s wizard JS builds are scoped here (they no longer exist globally).
    - `includes/invoice_style.php` — the cashier Service Invoice (`.invoice-sheet` / `.inv-*` / `.or-*`, `.card-inv` renamed `.inv-card`) **and its landscape one-page `@media print` block + `@page { size: landscape }`** — the whole old global `@media print` block was invoice-only and is now gone from `style.css`. Shared by `cashier/billing.php` + `shared/receipt.php`. `layout.php`'s `<main>` gained `print:p-0` (replacing the old `.app-content { padding:0 }` print rule; shell hiding was already `print:hidden` from Group 2).
  - **Done — all 24 module pages.** `grep` confirms **no `modules/**` page links `style.css`** any more. Standalone (non-`layout.php`) pages `admin/report_print.php` and `shared/receipt.php` now load `tailwind.php` (+ `invoice_style.php` for the receipt) instead of `style.css`. Both cashier + receptionist `calendar.php` are verbatim regenerations of the converted dentist calendar with a swapped guard.
- **Group 4 — `app.js` / `charts.js` components + retire `style.css` (done):** `assets/js/app.js` now opens with a Tailwind class-string block (same base-string + colour-suffix pattern as the module pages) and exposes `window.UI` (`BADGE` / `BADGE_C` / `ALERT` / `ALERT_C` / `KV` / `ROW_BTW` / `MUTED` / `HINT` / `SEP` / `STACK` / `CHECK_LINE` / `TABLE` / `BIG_NUM`) plus `window.badgeHTML(text, kind)` and `window.alertCls(kind)`. `modalShell` / `openModal` / `closeModal` (marker classes `modal-head` / `modal-body` / `modal-foot` / `modal-close` kept — the records pages query them), `toast`, `badge` / `srcBadge` / `opStatus` (→ `badgeHTML`; `billingBadge` still returns `{cls,label}` where `cls` is a `BADGE_C` key), the bell dropdown list, `logProcedureModal`, `appointmentModal` and `wireClinicDate`'s inline error all emit utilities. `charts.js` `donutChart` / `barChart` / `lineChart` emit utilities via a `C_*` / `B_*` / `L_WRAP` const block. `wireRefCheck` / `wirePhoneField` still toggle `.field-err` / `.is-invalid` (rules live in `tailwind.php`). The three module-page scripts that built these bits inline were updated: `patient/appointments.php` (badge → `badgeHTML`, its wizard `.row-between` / `.kv` / `.alert*` scoped into `patient_booking_style.php`), `cashier/billing.php` (`var(--*)` inline styles → hex; its `.check-line` is covered by `invoice_style.php`), `header.php` (`.muted` → `text-steel-400`). **`assets/css/style.css` is deleted and its `<link>` removed from `layout.php`.**

### Dark mode (2026-09, rolling out in stages)

Class-based Tailwind dark mode (`darkMode: 'class'` in `tailwind.php`'s config) — a `dark` class on `<html>`, never on `<body>`. The control is a sun/moon toggle button in `header.php`'s top-right corner, present for **every role** (`#themeToggle`, styled as the same 40px navy circle as the patient notification bell): for the `patient` role it sits beside the bell, for every other role it sits alone in the bell's usual spot (`header.php`'s `ml-auto` wrapper now always renders; the bell `<div>` inside it is still gated to `$showBell`). The icon shown is the mode a click switches **to** (moon while light, sun once dark) via a CSS opacity crossfade keyed off `html.dark` — the same stacked-absolute-SVG technique as the sidebar's collapsed-logo icon swap.

`app.js`'s `#themeToggle` click handler (next to the sidebar-collapse code, same file region) flips `document.documentElement.classList` and persists the choice to `localStorage['dgt_theme']` ('dark'/'light'). An inline script at the very top of `layout.php`'s `<head>` (before `tailwind.php` loads) applies the stored choice — or, on a first visit with nothing stored yet, the OS's `prefers-color-scheme` — before `<body>` paints, so there is never a flash of the wrong theme. `tailwind.php` also sets `html.dark { color-scheme: dark }` so native browser chrome (scrollbars, date/time pickers) follows suit even on a page that hasn't been dark-converted yet.

**The switch itself is animated (2026-09)** — a circle wipe expanding from the button, ported from Magic UI's "Animated Theme Toggler" (`magicui.design`, a React component) to plain DOM/CSS since this app has no React or build step: on click, `document.startViewTransition()` snapshots the before/after paint, then a Web Animations API call clips the new snapshot's `::view-transition-new(root)` pseudo-element to a `circle()` growing from the button's centre out past the farthest viewport corner (`Math.hypot` of the larger horizontal/vertical distance), over 650ms ease-in-out. `tailwind.php` disables the View Transitions API's own default cross-fade (`animation: none` + `mix-blend-mode: normal` on both `::view-transition-old(root)` and `::view-transition-new(root)`) so only the circle plays, not a fight between the two. Falls back to the old instant class-flip (no animation) when `document.startViewTransition` doesn't exist (an older browser) or `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is set — same fallback the rest of the app's motion already respects.

**Rolled out like every other design-system pass — page-group by page-group, not all at once:**
- **Done — the app shell.** `body`'s canvas (`dark:bg-dgt-ink-900 dark:text-dgt-ink-100`) and `header.php` (`dark:bg-dgt-ink-800`, borders, greeting text, the bell dropdown panel). **`includes/sidebar.php` needed zero changes** — it was already a dark navy surface (`bg-dgt-blue-800`) in light mode, so it looks identical in both themes; this is a deliberate "chrome stays dark always" pattern common to apps with a dark-mode toggle, not an oversight.
- **Done — the shared UI kit in `app.js`, because almost everything else depends on it.** The `MODAL` / `M_HEAD` / `M_TITLE` / `M_CLOSE` / `M_FOOT` constants behind `openModal()`/`modalShell()`, `BADGE_C`, `ALERT_C`, `KV`, `BIG_NUM`, `SEP`, `TABLE`, and `BTN_GHOST` all carry `dark:` pairs now. Because `window.appointmentModal()` (used by nearly every role's tables) and `logProcedureModal()` build on these same constants and add no colour classes of their own beyond them, theming this one shared layer made those modals correct in dark mode for free, app-wide — this was necessary infrastructure for the admin dashboard's drill-downs to look right, not scope creep. The soft "-soft" badge/alert tints (`bg-warn-soft`, `bg-ok-soft`, …, designed for a white card) get a `dark:bg-<accent>/20` pair instead — the same accent colour at ~20% opacity as a fill, with the saturated accent kept as the text colour; this is the standard "badge on a dark surface" technique, and it's what the two bespoke `src-online`/`src-walkin` one-off hex badges do too (a lighter tint of the same hue, same "no raw hex outside an entity/one-off colour" exception already granted for those).
- **Done — `charts.js`.** Its Tailwind-class chrome (`C_LEGEND`, `B_LABEL`, `B_TRACK`, `B_VAL`) got `dark:` pairs, but the donut/line chart's gridlines, empty-ring track, and centre/axis text are drawn as **literal SVG attributes** (`fill="#102f51"` etc.), which can't take a `dark:` Tailwind variant directly. Those now read `var(--chart-track)` / `--chart-center` / `--chart-center-sub` / `--chart-axis` instead — four custom properties defined on `:root` (light values) and redefined under `html.dark` in `tailwind.php`. Using CSS variables here (rather than having the JS branch on `document.documentElement.classList.contains('dark')` at render time) means an already-drawn chart re-colours itself instantly when the toggle flips, with no re-render needed. `barChart`/`lineChart` aren't actually called anywhere yet (see the Tailwind-migration section above) but were fixed too since the cost was trivial.
- **Done — `modules/admin/dashboard.php`** (the highest-traffic screen, done alongside the shell): the `$card`/`$cardHd`/`$h2` PHP class-string vars, the `.dash-card` hover-lift `<style>` block, every metric tile (`tile()`), `GHOST_BTN`, the page-local `TABLE`, `PILL_CLS`, and all four table-row builders (`apptTable()`, `staffTable()`, `revTable()`, `svcRevTable()`) got `dark:` pairs, plus the Mark-absent/Mark-on-duty confirm dialogs and the absence-reason input.
- **Done — four of the five shared, hand-written `<style>` widgets** (these predate the Tailwind migration and can't take a `dark:` utility variant at all, so each gets a plain `html.dark .classname { … }` override block with hex values, appended after its existing rules): `includes/calendar_style.php` (`.cal-*`, the dentist/receptionist/cashier month-grid calendar), `includes/patient_calendar_style.php` (`.pcal-*`, the patient booking calendar), `includes/records_chart_style.php` (`.rec-*`/`.tooth-*`, the dentist/receptionist Patient Details modal + FDI tooth chart), `includes/patient_booking_style.php` (`.wiz-*`/`.pay-*`/`.pm-*`/`.rcpt-*`/`.appt-c*`/`.submitted-*`, the patient booking wizard — deliberately excludes `.pm-qr img`, the GCash QR code, which must always stay on a light background to stay scannable). **`includes/invoice_style.php` is deliberately excluded, not converted** (see below).
- **Done — every `modules/patient/**` page** (`appointments.php`, `calendar.php`, `dashboard.php` "Treatment & Billing", `services.php`, `notifications.php`) — all on the `dgt-*`/legacy token mix each file already used, following the same `dark:` pairing conventions as the shell.
- **Done — dentist, receptionist and cashier roles, every page** (`dentist/dashboard.php`, `records.php`, `calendar.php`; `receptionist/dashboard.php`, `records.php`, `calendar.php`; `cashier/transactions.php`, `billing.php`, `calendar.php`, `logs.php`).
- **Done — every remaining admin page** (`appointments.php`, `logs.php`, `schedules.php`, `services.php`, `users.php`, `reports.php` — the `buildDoc()` PDF/preview document node keeps its own inline light styles unconditionally, same document-stays-light exception as the invoice — `settings.php`) and **`modules/shared/profile.php`**.
- **Done, but scoped to a curated subset of the public/auth pages — `reset_password.php`, `verify.php`, `terms.php`, `404.php`.** These four are the only public pages that carry both the pre-paint theme-init script (copied inline into each page's own `<head>`, since none of them use `layout.php`) and `dark:` pairs throughout, so they **auto-follow** the stored `dgt_theme` preference / OS `prefers-color-scheme` — same as every app-shell page, but with **no visible toggle** anywhere on the page itself (there's no header.php to hold one). `includes/auth_style.php` (the shared `AUTH_*` class-string constants + `.auth-bg` background used by `reset_password.php`/`verify.php`, and originally by `login.php`/`register.php` too) and `includes/terms_content.php` (the shared T&C body, used by `terms.php` and the `register.php` modal) both carry `dark:` pairs for this reason. **`terms.php` was switched onto the shared `auth_style.php` chrome (2026-09, at the user's request from a screenshot)** — it previously had its own one-off hand-coded blue gradient body (`bg-[linear-gradient(160deg,#7aa6dd…)]`) and a green (`bg-ok`) "Back to Sign Up" button, both unlike every other auth page. It now `require`s `includes/auth_style.php` (after `tailwind.php`, same as `verify.php`/`reset_password.php`) and uses `<body class="<?= AUTH_BODY ?>">` for the shared pale-canvas/drifting-polygon `.auth-bg` background (dark-mode variant included), a card styled with the same border/shadow tokens as `AUTH_CARD` (just a wider `max-w-[760px]` — the T&C body needs the room), and `AUTH_BTN`/`AUTH_LINK` for the "Back to Sign Up" button and "Return to sign in" link — the same indigo `#4361ee` used by every other auth-page primary button, not green.
- **Update 2026-10-01: `index.php` (landing page) now HAS a visitor-toggled dark mode, added at the user's explicit request** — a sun/moon `#landingThemeToggle` beside the Sign Up / Log In button (and beside Log out when signed in), a pre-paint script that adds `.dark` only when `localStorage['dgt_theme'] === 'dark'` (never from the OS setting), and a scoped `html.dark` CSS block in the page `<style>` for the light sections (Services, About, both modals, `.btn-dark`). `login.php` and `register.php` remain light-only. The paragraph below describes the earlier exclusion and still holds for those two.
- **`index.php`, `login.php`, and `register.php` are explicitly excluded from dark mode, by the user's direct instruction** — they stay light-only permanently, with no pre-paint script and no `dark:` classes anywhere in them. This is deliberate, not an oversight: an earlier pass had converted all three (including `register.php`'s T&C modal, which reuses `terms_content.php`), and the user asked for it to be reverted specifically on these three. Because `terms_content.php` and `auth_style.php` are shared with pages that *do* stay dark-aware (`terms.php`; `reset_password.php`/`verify.php`), their `dark:` classes remain in those shared files — they simply never activate on `login.php`/`register.php`/`index.php` since those pages never add the `dark` class to `<html>` in the first place. Do not re-add the pre-paint script or `dark:` classes to these three files without the user asking again. (`register.php` is now just a redirect stub to `login.php?mode=register` — see the "Public landing page" section's login.php paragraph for the 2026-09 merged Sign In / Create Account page; the rule still applies to that merged file.)
- **Deliberately excluded, by design (not a user preference, a structural exception):** `modules/shared/receipt.php` and `modules/admin/report_print.php` (standalone print-only documents that don't use `layout.php` and never reach the dark toggle anyway), `.pm-qr img` (the GCash QR code needs a light background with dark modules to stay scannable), and **the whole Service Invoice (`includes/invoice_style.php`'s `.inv-*` / `.invoice-sheet`, used by `modules/cashier/billing.php` and `modules/shared/receipt.php`)** — the user asked for it to stay plain white paper regardless of theme, since it's a real financial document, not app chrome (the same logic as the print exception, just extended to the on-screen view too). `invoice_style.php` carries no `html.dark` rules at all; `billing.php`'s `.inv-card` wrapper has no `dark:` classes either, only the page chrome around it (heading, back link, banner, service-picker modal) follows the app theme.

It is a re-implementation of a documented thesis system ("STI College Fairview" capstone doc). The user supplies reference screenshots / scope and asks to match them; a direct chat instruction always overrides the reference doc. See the pinned memory `reference-system.md`.

## Commands

```bash
# Import / reset the whole database (schema + seed data)
d:\XAMPP\mysql\bin\mysql.exe -u root < d:\XAMPP\htdocs\dgt_dental_clinic\schema.sql

# Apply one incremental migration (to a DB from an earlier version)
d:\XAMPP\mysql\bin\mysql.exe -u root < d:\XAMPP\htdocs\dgt_dental_clinic\migrations\00N_name.sql

# Syntax-check a PHP file
d:\XAMPP\php\php.exe -l path\to\file.php
```

Apache + MySQL are started from the XAMPP control panel. DB credentials live in `config/db.php` (`root` / empty password by default).

**If MySQL / MariaDB won't start** (this XAMPP install has done it repeatedly — a self-replicating `multi-master.info` twice, an InnoDB redo-log + Aria system-table corruption on 2026-08-31, and again on 2026-09-06 — compound `phpmyadmin` InnoDB page corruption + `mysql` Aria corruption (`mysql.proxies_priv` "marked as crashed and last repair failed", `mysql.db` datafile size wrong / read error 176)): check `d:\XAMPP\mysql\data\mysql_error.log` (or hand-start with `--console` to see the swallowed error). **The recurrence is almost always an unclean shutdown** — the XAMPP panel closed / the PC slept or lost power with MySQL still running, or antivirus locking an `.ibd`/`.MAD` mid-write. Prevention: always hit **Stop** for MySQL in the XAMPP Control Panel before closing it or the machine; exclude `d:\XAMPP\mysql\data` from real-time AV scanning. `my.ini` now also carries `aria_recover_options=BACKUP,QUICK` so a merely-crashed (not structurally-corrupt) Aria system table self-repairs on the next boot instead of aborting startup.

**Case 1 — `[ERROR] Adding new entry '' to master_info failed` / `[ERROR] Aborting` right after "Reading of all Master_info entries succeeded"** (the recurring `multi-master.info` bug — happened again 2026-09-04, seemingly nudged by rapid `mysql.exe` client connections during migration work). This is *not* data corruption. Fix: with mysqld stopped, delete (or move aside) the stale replication-state files from `d:\XAMPP\mysql\data\` — **`multi-master.info`** (the culprit — a garbage 2KB file with an empty-named master entry), plus the files it regenerates each boot: `master.info`, `relay-log.info`, `mysql-relay-bin.*`. Then start normally. `skip-slave-start` in `my.ini` does **not** prevent this — the abort happens while *parsing* the info files, before slave threads. No data touched; a moved-aside copy of the 2026-09-04 set is in `d:\XAMPP\mysql\data\_repl_stale_20260904\`.

**Case 2 — genuine InnoDB / Aria corruption.** The reliable full recovery, since the app DB dumps fine even when the `mysql` system DB is damaged: (1) hand-start with InnoDB relaxed **and** grants skipped so the crashed privilege tables don't block login — `mysqld.exe --defaults-file=d:\XAMPP\mysql\bin\my.ini --console --innodb_force_recovery=1 --skip-grant-tables` (level 1–3 still allows the dump; `--skip-grant-tables` is what gets you past a crashed `mysql.proxies_priv`/`mysql.db`), (2) `mysqldump -u root --databases dental_clinic_system --routines --events --triggers --single-transaction > rescue.sql` and sanity-check it (`SELECT COUNT(*)` on `users`/`appointments`/`payments`, `CHECK TABLE … QUICK`), (3) `mysqladmin -u root shutdown`, (4) `mv data data_corrupt_<date>`, (5) `cp -r d:\XAMPP\mysql\backup d:\XAMPP\mysql\data` (XAMPP ships a pristine datadir there — clean `mysql` / `phpmyadmin` / `performance_schema` / `test`, root with no password), (6) from the new `data\` delete the replication-state + ephemeral files (`multi-master.info`, `master.info`, `relay-log.info`, `mysql-relay-bin.*`, `ib_buffer_pool`, `ibtmp1`) so Case 1 can't immediately bite, (7) start normally, `mysql -u root < rescue.sql`, `mysql_upgrade -u root --force`, verify (`SHOW TABLES`, `mysqlcheck -u root dental_clinic_system`, a real login curl), stop cleanly, hand back to the XAMPP control panel (click **Start** there — don't leave the hand-started instance running). Only `dental_clinic_system` is user data; phpMyAdmin's stored settings reset but the app is untouched. **Done on 2026-09-06** — the corrupt datadir is kept at `d:\XAMPP\mysql\data_corrupt_20260906\` (95 MB, safe to delete once things are stable); `my.ini.bak-20260906` is the pre-change config.

### Schema changes

`schema.sql` is the full current schema **and** must be kept in sync by hand. When changing the database:
1. add a numbered file in `migrations/` (`00N_description.sql`) with the incremental `ALTER`/`CREATE`,
2. apply it to the live DB,
3. edit `schema.sql` so a fresh import matches.

Migrations are plain SQL, applied manually in order — there is no migration runner or tracking table.

/ `seed_demo_pending_bill` generator scripts were deleted). To exercise the
patient role, self-register at `login.php?mode=register` or create a patient from the admin
Users / front-desk walk-in / Patient Records screens.

## Architecture

### Request lifecycle — module pages

Every screen under `modules/<role>/*.php` follows one pattern:

```php
$me = checkAuth(['admin']);              // RBAC guard, redirects on failure
$PAGE_TITLE = '...'; $ACTIVE = '...';    // $ACTIVE matches a sidebar nav key
ob_start(); /* ...HTML... */ $PAGE_CONTENT = ob_get_clean();
$PAGE_SCRIPT = <<<'JS' ...inline module JS... JS;   // nowdoc — not executed server-side
require __DIR__ . '/../../includes/layout.php';
```

A module page may set `$PAGE_WIDE = true` before requiring `layout.php` for a centred `max-width:1560px` column (instead of 1180), or **`$PAGE_FULL = true`** for a full-bleed column: `page page-wide w-full`, no `mx-auto`, no `max-width` — it fills the viewport bounded only by `<main>`'s `px-7` gutter. **Every `modules/**` page sets `$PAGE_FULL = true`** (all 5 roles + `shared/profile.php` — the user wanted the whole app to fill the screen, not sit in a centred strip); a new module page should do the same. `$PAGE_FULL` wins over `$PAGE_WIDE` if both are set. The two standalone pages `shared/receipt.php` and `admin/report_print.php` don't use `layout.php` so the flags don't apply. Both flags thread through the full and partial render paths.

`includes/layout.php` decides what to emit:
- **Full request** → `<!DOCTYPE>` + `sidebar.php` + `header.php` + `#app-content` + shared JS (`app.js`, `charts.js`, `nav.js`) + `$PAGE_SCRIPT`.
- **Partial request** (header `X-Requested-With: fetch`, detected by `isPartialRequest()`) → only the `.page` fragment and a trailing `<script>` with `$PAGE_SCRIPT`, plus an `X-Page-Title` response header.

`assets/js/nav.js` intercepts clicks on `<a data-nav>`, fetches the partial, swaps `#app-content.innerHTML`, then **recreates every `<script>` node so inline module JS re-runs** on each navigation. Consequences to respect:
- Module JS must be an IIFE and must be safe to run repeatedly (no assumptions about first-load-only state).
- `window.reloadPage()` re-fetches the current page; call it after a mutation instead of `location.reload()`.
- Global helpers persist across navigations; per-page listeners are attached fresh each time.

### Request lifecycle — JSON APIs

`api/*.php` endpoints start with `require_once __DIR__ . '/_bootstrap.php';` then `$me = apiAuth([roles]);`. Convention:
- Dispatch on `$_SERVER['REQUEST_METHOD']` + `$_GET['action']`.
- Read the body with `requestBody()` (handles JSON or form-encoded).
- Respond with `jsonOut(['ok' => true, ...])` / `jsonOut(['ok' => false, 'error' => '...'], 4xx)`. **Every response carries an `ok` boolean** — the client wrapper throws when it is false.
- `db()` inside APIs === `getDB()` (shared singleton PDO, exceptions on, real prepares).
- `_bootstrap.php` also holds cross-endpoint domain helpers: `appointmentDetail($id)` (the canonical appointment+patient+dentist+payment+services join) and `recomputePayment($paymentId)` (recalculates `remaining_balance` / `payment_status` / `paid_at` from the amount columns — call it after touching any `payments` money column).

### Client helpers (`assets/js/app.js`, loaded once, global)

- `api(path, {method, body})` — fetch wrapper; prefixes `/dgt_dental_clinic/api/`, sets the `fetch` header, JSON-encodes the body, throws `Error(data.error)` on failure.
- `openModal(html,{wide})` / `closeModal()` / `modalShell(title, body, foot)` — the only modal system. Any element with `data-close` closes it. It **animates in and out**: `openModal` writes the markup, forces a reflow, then adds `#modalRoot.is-in` (CSS in `tailwind.php` fades the overlay + fades/scales/slides the dialog, ~240 ms); `closeModal` removes `.is-in` and waits for the dialog's `transitionend` (300 ms fallback) before clearing the markup. A `modalSeq` counter makes a still-animating close abort if a newer modal opened. `prefers-reduced-motion` skips all of it.
- `toast(msg, 'ok'|'err'|'info')`.
- `esc()`, `peso()`, `fmtDate()` — mirror the PHP `e()`, `peso()` helpers; use them for all interpolated strings.
- `todayISO(offsetDays = 0)` — **local** calendar date as `YYYY-MM-DD` for `<input type="date">` defaults / `min`s. Use this, never `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — `toISOString()` is UTC and reads a day early through the whole PH (UTC+8) morning, which once back-dated a cashier payment into the previous month on the analytics dashboard.
- `appointmentModal(id)` + global `[data-view="<id>"]` delegation — shared appointment drill-down used by every role's tables. Its modal title shows the internal appointment number (`Appointment #<id>`) **only for staff** — `api/appointments.php action=get` returns `ref_label` = `'#'.appointment_id` for staff, `''` for the `patient` role, and the title is `'Appointment' + (ref_label ? ' '+ref_label : '')`. A global running PK (e.g. `#39` on someone's first visit) confuses patients; staff keep it to cross-reference the History Log. Age is its own `<dt>Age</dt>` row, not appended to the patient name.
- Live clock (`[data-clock]`) is wired globally. The notification bell is rendered **only for the `patient` role** (`$showBell` in `header.php`); `notify()` is only ever called with a patient's `user_id`. Staff roles have no bell.
- The signed-in user's avatar / name / role and the Logout link live in the **sidebar footer** (`.sidebar-user` in `sidebar.php`), not the topbar. The avatar block links to `modules/shared/profile.php`. Both the sidebar-footer name and the `header.php` greeting show **first + last name only** via `shortName($u)` in `functions.php` (`"$first_name $last_name"`, falling back to `full_name`) — the middle name(s) just overflow there; everywhere else still renders the full `full_name` mirror.

`assets/js/charts.js` renders dependency-free inline-SVG `donutChart` / `barChart` / `groupedBarChart` / `lineChart`. `donutChart()` itself is no longer called anywhere on the admin dashboard (the Revenue-by-Service donut it drew was replaced by the Service Usage & Demand grouped-bar chart — see below), but stays live library code (its hover/tooltip/entrance-animation infrastructure is what `groupedBarChart()` and `lineChart()` both reused). `groupedBarChart()` (Service Usage & Demand) and `lineChart()` (the Revenue Trend chart under Revenue Summary, added 2026-09 — see below) are the two current live consumers; `barChart` (horizontal bars) is still kept in the library but unused. **Each arc has a hover/focus tooltip (2026-09)** — `donutChart()` gives every segment `<circle>` a `.donut-arc` class + `data-tip` (label · formatted value · percentage, `opts.money !== false` picks `peso()` vs a plain number) + `role="img"`/`aria-label` (screen-reader parity) + `tabindex="0"` (keyboard reachable). Since `donutChart()` only returns a markup string — the caller does `el.innerHTML = …`, so no `<script>` could run even if one were embedded — the hover behaviour is one shared, document-level delegated listener set up once at module load in `charts.js` itself: `pointermove`/`pointerout` show/hide a single floating tooltip `<div>` (lazily created, appended to `<body>`, positioned via `clientX/clientY`), and `focusin`/`focusout` do the same anchored to the focused arc's own bounding box, so keyboard users get the same information. `tailwind.php` adds the paired CSS: `.donut-arc { cursor:pointer }` plus a `brightness(1.15)` bump on `:hover`/`:focus-visible` (a `prefers-reduced-motion` guard drops the transition, not the state change). Because a `<circle>`'s dasharray gaps don't receive pointer events in any browser, each segment's hover/hit area is naturally clipped to just its own visible arc slice with no extra math needed. **`groupedBarChart()`'s `.grouped-bar` bars get a stronger version of the same treatment (2026-09)** — a plain brightness bump (matching `.donut-arc`'s) was tried first, but at only ~6px wide with up to ~10 bars clustered per date/month, it was too subtle to tell which bar the pointer was actually over ("hard to see because the bars are small"); `tailwind.php`'s `.grouped-bar` rule adds a `1.5px` `stroke: var(--chart-center)` (the same navy/near-white token `.donut-arc:focus-visible`'s outline already uses, so it's visible against the chart background in both themes) on top of the `brightness(1.3)` bump, on both `:hover` and `:focus-visible` — `paint-order: stroke` keeps the border from being partly overdrawn by the bar's own fill. Same `prefers-reduced-motion` guard (drops the transition, not the hover/focus state itself). **A one-shot draw-in entrance animation (2026-09)** — `window.playDonutIntro(container)` (also in `charts.js`) sweeps each `.donut-arc`'s `stroke-dasharray` from `0 <circumference>` up to its real length via the Web Animations API, staggered 90ms per segment (so the ring visibly draws itself clockwise, segment by segment), then fades/scales the `.donut-centre` total in and fades/rises the `.donut-legend > li` items in with a further stagger — all `fill:'backwards'` so nothing flashes at its final state during its own delay, and skipped outright under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `donutChart()` itself stays a pure string-builder (still just `el.innerHTML = …`, no embedded script), so `playDonutIntro()` is one explicit call the caller makes right after inserting that markup — `modules/admin/dashboard.php`'s `loadRevenue()` is the only call site, right after it sets `#chartSvcRevenue`'s `innerHTML`. That function runs exactly once per "entering" the dashboard (a full page load, or a nav.js re-visit — which covers arriving here via login too, since the dashboard is the admin's `roleHome()`) and is never called again by the 30s operational-cards auto-refresh, so the chart draws in on arrival and does not re-animate out from under the admin every 30 seconds.

**The same "just entered the page" entrance was extended to the rest of the dashboard's cards (2026-09)** — a page-local `staggerIn(container, opts)` helper in `modules/admin/dashboard.php`'s own `$PAGE_SCRIPT` (not `charts.js`, since it's plain generic card motion, not chart-specific) fades + rises (18px, 420ms, `cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1)`) every direct child of a container in via the Web Animations API, staggered 70ms apart (`prefers-reduced-motion` skips it, same as `playDonutIntro`). **The `.dash-card` hover (2026-09, tuned)** lifts 4px with a 300ms ease-out transition and a two-layer shadow (a soft wide blur + a tighter close one, approximating a `shadow-md`→`shadow-xl` jump) instead of the earlier flatter 2px/180ms version; the icon-chip fill-swap and the `svcCard` donut card's hover both match the same 300ms timing. The whole-card tap-scale (`role="button"` triggers the global `[role="button"]:active { transform: translateY(1px) scale(.985) }` press in `tailwind.php`) comes free from that shared rule — no separate JS needed. Two call sites: `render(o, animate)` — the operational-cards builder — calls `staggerIn(g('opsCards'))` only when its new `animate` param is true, and `loadOps(silent)` passes `!silent`, so the real page-entry load (`loadOps(false)`, called once) animates while the 30s auto-refresh poll and the two post-action refreshes (marking a doctor absent/present, toggling an assistant's duty — both call `loadOps(true)`) stay silent and don't replay it; `loadRevenue()` (also once-per-entry, no silent variant) calls `staggerIn(g('revenueCards'))` unconditionally right after building the three revenue tiles. Net effect: logging in as admin (or navigating to Dashboard, or reloading it) plays one cohesive entrance — the seven operational cards cascade in, the three revenue tiles cascade in, and the donut sweeps in — and nothing on the page re-animates on its own afterward.

### Public landing page
