`index.php` is a **public marketing landing page**, Tailwind utilities via `includes/tailwind.php` plus a scoped `<style>`. **Its full current form (the 2026-09 from-scratch Floria rebuild — Geist typeface, DGT cool palette, a horizontal Services card slider under a Floria header, dark navy Process + closing sections) is documented in the "Styling: the DGT design system" section above (the `index.php` bullet); read that for the layout.** Operational facts that persist regardless of styling passes:

- **`SELECT service_id, service_name, description, base_price, image FROM services WHERE is_active = 1 ORDER BY service_name`** into `$services`, wrapped in `try/catch` → empty list + "listed here shortly" fallback. Same rows the patient "Our Services" page and admin Services CRUD use; images from `uploads/services/` (migration `020_service_images.sql` seeded all 10).
- **Routing / nav by `$me`:** staff roles are redirected to `roleHome()`; **guests and signed-in patients both see the page.** A guest gets a "Sign Up / Log In" pill and the hero CTA points to `login.php`; a signed-in patient gets a "My Portal" nav link, a first-name chip, a "Log out" pill, and the hero CTA points to the patient `roleHome()`. `$bookUrl` holds that target. Nav labels: Home / Services / How It Works / About Us (+ My Portal). No notification bell.
- Because patient login/registration land on `index.php`, the **`includes/sidebar.php` brand block is a plain link to `index.php` for the `patient` role only** (their way back to the public site); every other role renders it as a non-linked `<div>`.
- Images used: `assets/img/dgt_logo.jpg` (logo + favicon), `assets/img/hero-a.jpg` (**a dark, low-key dental still-life** — mirror + probe on near-black, Pexels #15613763; navy gradient fallback behind it), `assets/img/dgtbulacanbranch.jpg` (About-section photo, renamed from `aboutus.png` 2026-09 — the real Bulacan branch storefront, not a stock team photo), `assets/img/process-bg.jpg` (**the faint "How it works" backdrop** — a blue-lit clinic scene, dentist over a patient, Pexels #15225509; `mix-blend-lighten`, `opacity-[0.55]`, ellipse mask, `.rv-l`), `assets/img/cta-clinic.jpg` (**the closing-CTA card background** — a clean modern operatory with a blue chair, Pexels #6812461; under a navy→ink darkening gradient), `assets/img/footer-flower.png` (**the footer flower** — the user-supplied blue hydrangea `for_footer.jpg` with its baked-checkerboard "transparency" keyed out to real alpha via a headless-canvas pass; sits blurred at `opacity-[0.4]` on the right of the footer, stem bleeding off). The footer's social links are now plain text (Facebook `facebook.com/dgtbulacan/` · TikTok `tiktok.com/@dgtonedentalcenter_` · Instagram `instagram.com/dgtonedentalcenter/`); the old `assets/icons/social-media.png` / `tik-tok.png` marks were deleted 2026-09.
- **SEO block** in `<head>`: `<meta name="description">`, `<link rel="canonical">`, Open Graph + `twitter:card`, a `Dentist` JSON-LD (real NAP + hours, via `json_encode`). `$origin` from `$_SERVER`.
- **Geist + Geist Mono** load from Google Fonts and are scoped to this page only via `body.floria { font-family: 'Geist', … }` — no `tailwind.php` change, the rest of the app keeps its system stack.

**`login.php` is now a combined Sign In / Create Account page — a "double slider" panel (2026-09, replacing two separate pages)**, built from a user-supplied reference (a classic sliding-panel sign-in/sign-up template). One `.auth2-container` holds both forms stacked in the same half (`.auth2-signin` / `.auth2-signup`, absolutely positioned, cross-faded via `opacity`/`transform`) plus an `.auth2-overlay-container` on the other half that slides the full width via `transform: translateX(-100%)` when a `.is-register` class is toggled on the container — the classic mechanic, reproduced as plain CSS transitions (no framework) since this app has none. **The whole thing is full-bleed (2026-09 revision)** — `.auth2-wrap`/`.auth2-container` fill `100vw`/`100vh` (no floating card, no border-radius/shadow, no visible page background around it — `body` has no padding and drops the shared `auth-bg` patterned background entirely since it would never show), per a reference screenshot the user pointed at that showed the split filling the whole browser window. The overlay panel is `assets/videos/clinic-loop.mp4` (a looping muted/autoplay/playsinline clinic video the user supplied; drop a replacement in `assets/videos/` and update **both** `<source src>`s — see the crossfade paragraph just below) under a **light** flat navy scrim (`rgba(15,28,46,.4)`, no gradient — the pinned no-gradient rule; deliberately kept low so the video reads clearly instead of going dark/moody, per the user's explicit "don't add opacity to the video too much") with two invite panels that slide with it ("Welcome Back!" / "New Here?", each a ghost pill button back to the other side). Because the scrim is light, legibility over a bright patch of footage comes from a **text-shadow on `.auth2-panel`** (`text-shadow: 0 2px 12px rgba(9,17,30,.6), 0 1px 3px rgba(9,17,30,.55)`) and a translucent navy backing on `.auth2-ghost-btn` (`rgba(15,28,46,.28)`, darkening on hover) — not from raising the overlay's opacity. Both forms still `POST` to `login.php` itself (a hidden `form=login`/`form=register` field tells the two PHP branches apart) — a full page reload re-renders the same merged file, and a server-computed `$activePanel` (from `$formSubmitted`, or `?mode=register` on a fresh GET) adds `.is-register` up front so a register error or the post-signup success card reloads showing the correct panel instead of flashing back to Sign In. The inactive panel gets the HTML `inert` attribute (kept in sync by the same JS that toggles `.is-register`) so it's unreachable by keyboard/screen reader while slid out of view. **Below an 860px breakpoint the slider becomes a plain stacked fallback** — the video banner (`order: -1` on `.auth2-overlay-container`, since it's after both form panels in DOM order) sits above whichever form is showing (no sliding, no overlay text panels; same toggle buttons just show/hide the two form blocks) since two 50%-width sliding panels don't work at phone width. `prefers-reduced-motion` drops all the panel/overlay transitions and pauses the video. **`register.php` is now just a redirect stub** to `login.php?mode=register`, kept only so an old bookmark/link still lands somewhere; every first-party link (`terms.php`'s "Back to Sign Up", the old `asset('register.php')` hrefs) was repointed straight at `login.php?mode=register` — hardcoded, not routed through `asset()`, for the same reason `verify.php?email=` links are hardcoded (`asset()`'s own `?v=` cache-bust query string would collide with the `?mode=` one). Every field, validation rule, and the OTP/forgot-password flow below are otherwise **unchanged** — only the page structure moved; nothing about the account-creation or sign-in logic itself changed. **Login is username-only** (`WHERE username = ? LIMIT 1`, then `password_verify`) — the `username` field is labelled "Username" / "Enter username"; **e-mail login was removed** (migration 028). On success a **patient is sent to `index.php`** (landing page, signed in); staff go to their `roleHome()` dashboard — the same split applies to the "already signed in" guard. **There IS a forced-password-change gate again (reinstated 2026-09, per instructor guidelines pasted verbatim), reversing the earlier "no gate" decision.** An account still on a system-generated temporary password (`must_change_password = 1` — set by the admin Add User flow, an admin Reset Password action, or a receptionist walk-in / Create Record booking) is redirected to a dedicated standalone page, **`change_password.php`** (repo root, styled like `reset_password.php`/`verify.php`), before it can reach anything else. Enforcement is a single shared function, **`requirePasswordChanged()`** in `includes/auth_middleware.php`, called from three places: inside `checkAuth()` itself (so every `modules/**` page and `shared/profile.php` picks it up for free with no per-page change), from `index.php` directly (the patient landing page doesn't go through `checkAuth()`), and — for a one-hop redirect straight there instead of bouncing through the dashboard first — `login.php`'s own post-login destination logic and its "already signed in" guard both check the flag directly. The function no-ops for a guest and for `change_password.php` itself (else that page could never be reached). `change_password.php` requires **Current (Temporary) Password / New Password / Confirm New Password** (current password verified server-side via `password_verify`, new password ≥ 8 characters, must differ from the current one, must match its confirmation — both a client-side `minlength`/match check and a full server re-check, same client+server pairing as every other form in this app), clears `must_change_password` on success, and redirects to `roleHome()` (or `index.php` for a patient) — the same destination `login.php` itself would have used. A **"Log out instead"** link is the one escape hatch (plain link to `logout.php`, which never called `checkAuth()` to begin with and is unaffected). `api/profile.php action=password` (the profile page's own self-service Change Password card, still there and still reachable *after* the gate clears) and its JS both had their minimum-length check bumped from 6 to 8 characters to match. (The earlier build removed a similar `force_password.php` interstitial at the user's request, in favour of the self-service Change Password form alone with just a soft reminder — that reminder banner on `modules/shared/profile.php` is untouched and still shows, it's just unreachable in practice now since the gate gets there first.) **A `role = 'patient'` account with `is_verified = 0` is blocked at login** ("Please verify your e-mail address before signing in.") with an inline **Enter verification code** link to `verify.php?email=<their email>` (that page owns the OTP entry + resend UX — see *E-mail verification* below). It also has the "Forgot your password?" modal (still keyed on e-mail — e-mail stays as a contact/recovery column, just not a login identifier). (The collapsed `<details>` demo-account list was removed 2026-09 at the user's request — the demo usernames/password are documented here in *Demo accounts* instead.) The **Create Account** panel self-serves a **patient** account: **First / Middle (optional) / Last name** (→ `first_name` / `middle_name` / `last_name` + the `full_name` mirror), Birthdate as three **Month / Day / Year `<select>`s** (`bd_month`/`bd_day`/`bd_year`, combined server-side into `patient_records.date_of_birth`, validated with `date_create_from_format` so e.g. Feb 30 is rejected), e-mail,
**the account holder must be 18 or older (2026-09)** — this restricts the self-registration *account*, not who can be a patient; a minor is still booked/treated normally (Pedodontics is a real service), just under a parent/guardian's own account via "booking for someone else", never their own login. Enforced two ways, both anchored to the DB clock (`SELECT CURDATE()`/`SELECT YEAR(CURDATE()) - 18`, not PHP's `date()`, the same convention as `dentalAssistantsOnDuty()`/`touchPresence()`/the online-booking lead time): the **Year `<select>` itself only offers `(this year − 18)` down to 100 years further back** — this year, last year, and every other too-recent year simply never appear as options, not just future ones; and the server re-checks the *exact* date (`$d > (clone $today)->modify('-18 years')`), which still catches the one boundary year the dropdown allows (e.g. picking the max year with a month/day whose birthday hasn't happened yet this cycle) with a **"You must be at least 18 years old to create an account."** error. This does **not** touch the dentist/receptionist **"+ Create Record"** modals or the front-desk walk-in flow — those have no birthdate field at all (a free-typed Age number instead) and legitimately register patients of any age; the 18+ gate is specific to this one self-service form. **Phone Number** (field `name="contact_number"`, required, PH-mobile `^09\d{9}$`, digits-only stripped client-side since `app.js` isn't loaded on public pages), password ≥ 8, Terms checkbox. The **username is auto-generated `first.last`** via `makeUsername()` and shown prominently on the success screen (a mono chip — "Use this to sign in — not your e-mail address"). It creates the row with **`is_verified = 0`** and **`must_change_password = 0`** (patients set their own password), **does not sign the user in**, sends a verification OTP via `sendVerificationOtp()` and renders an "Almost there — check your inbox" card (an **Enter Verification Code** button to `verify.php?email=<email>`, a **Resend it** button, and on localhost the raw code inline since there is no SMTP). Client-side JS keeps **Sign Up** disabled until every field is valid; the server re-validates and enforces e-mail uniqueness. A live hint under the password field (`#pwHint`) shows the 8-character rule status. **Both panel logos link back to `index.php`** (the public landing page) — plain `<a>` wraps, no JS.

**The overlay video loops seamlessly via a two-video opacity crossfade (2026-09)** — a single looping `<video>` (`loop` attribute) jumps visibly at the restart point, since the last and first frames rarely match. `login.php` now renders **two** identical `<video>` elements stacked in `.auth2-overlay` (`#authVideo`, `class="is-active"`, still `autoplay` for the very first play; `#authVideo2`, paused, no `loop`/`autoplay`), both picking up `.auth2-overlay video`'s existing `position:absolute; inset:0` rule so they sit pixel-for-pixel on top of each other for free — no extra positioning CSS needed. That rule gained `opacity:0; transition:opacity .6s linear` plus a `.is-active { opacity:1 }` override; a dedicated JS IIFE (right after the slide-toggle one) removes `#authVideo`'s `loop` attribute (looping is now driven by JS) and watches `timeupdate` on whichever video is currently playing — once it's within `CROSSFADE_SEC` (0.6s, clamped to at most a quarter of the clip's own duration for a short replacement video) of its own end, it seeks the standby video to `0`, calls `.play()` on it, swaps the `.is-active` class on the next animation frame (so both opacities animate across the same 0.6s CSS transition and visibly blend into each other), then after that duration elapses pauses the outgoing video and swaps which element is "active" vs. "standby" for the next cycle. Verified end-to-end with a headless-Chrome check that force-seeks the active video to `duration - 1.2s`: confirmed the standby video started playing and gained `.is-active` while the first paused at its own end, then continued advancing normally afterward — a real crossfade-and-swap, not just a class toggle. Skipped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion` — the JS IIFE removes `#authVideo2` from the DOM outright and leaves `#authVideo`'s original `loop` attribute in place, so a reduced-motion visitor gets the plain (un-faded) loop exactly as before, and a CSS-only `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .auth2-overlay video { transition: none } }` guard covers the case where JS never runs at all (a plain instant loop with no fade, since `loop` is untouched then too). Because `register.php` is just a redirect stub to `login.php?mode=register` (see above), this single fix covers both "login" and "register" — there is no separate video element to duplicate the change onto.

**"Launch" transition into the dashboard on a successful sign-in (2026-09).** This app has no client router — `login.php` POSTs, the server redirects with `header('Location: ...')` to whatever `roleHome()` resolves to, a genuinely different page per role. A literal shared-element DOM morph (the kind an SPA can do) isn't possible across that boundary, so this is a two-sided illusion built from two independent pieces, both `transform`/`opacity` only (GPU-accelerated) and both eased with `cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1)`: (1) **on `login.php`**, the `#loginForm` submit handler (in the page's own script, near the slide-toggle IIFE) calls `preventDefault()`, adds `.is-launching` to `#authContainer`, stashes a `sessionStorage['dgt_just_logged_in']='1'` flag, and calls the form's real (native) `.submit()` once the transition ends (or a 1000ms timeout fires, as a genuine fallback for a missed `transitionend` — **this must stay longer than the CSS duration below**, since a fallback shorter than the transition it's guarding always wins the race and silently truncates the animation on every single submit; an earlier version had this backwards, at 420ms against a 500ms transition, so the visible effect never actually finished playing before the page navigated away). **The exit itself is two-sided, not a zoom (2026-09, revised)** — on desktop (a real split screen) `.is-launching` pushes the two currently-visible halves further off in the direction they already face over **900ms** (slowed from an initial 500ms at the user's request): whichever of `.auth2-signin`/`.auth2-signup` is showing slides fully off its edge while fading out, and `.auth2-overlay-container` (the video) slides off the opposite edge, like doors opening — which physical element goes which way flips correctly with `.is-register` (Sign In: form left/video right; Create Account: video left/form right, since `.is-register` already swapped them, so the launch styles are scoped `:not(.is-register)` vs `.is-register` pairs). Below the 860px breakpoint, where the layout is a single stacked column with no literal "two sides" to speak of, `.is-launching` instead does the simpler whole-card `scale(1.06)` + `opacity:0`, same 900ms; (2) **in `assets/js/app.js`** (next to the theme-toggle code, same file region), a block that runs on every full page load checks and immediately clears that flag — a no-op on any ordinary page load or nav.js in-app navigation — and if it was set, fades+rises (`translateY(16px)→0`) `#sidebar`, `#topHeader` and each direct child of `#app-content` via the Web Animations API, staggered 60ms apart, mirroring the admin dashboard's own local `staggerIn()` pattern. **Deliberately optimistic, not success-gated**: gating the animation on the server's actual success/failure would mean turning the login form into an AJAX/JSON endpoint (a bigger, security-sensitive rework of the auth flow, including replaying `logActivity()`'s failed-attempt logging correctly) — out of scope for a cosmetic transition, so the exit animation plays on every submit attempt; a wrong password just reloads back to this same page shortly after, cutting the animation short rather than looking broken. The patient's post-login destination, `index.php`, doesn't load `app.js` (it's a public page with its own scroll-reveal/mount animations already) and doesn't consume the flag — harmless, since `sessionStorage` is tab-scoped and disappears with the tab. Both stages independently no-op under `prefers-reduced-motion` (checked in JS, not just CSS, so a reduced-motion user's submit is never delayed waiting on a transition that will never play) and degrade to a perfectly ordinary instant form submit if JS doesn't run at all.

**E-mail verification (OTP, migration `041`, 2026-09 — replaced the earlier click-a-link flow):** `includes/mailer.php` `sendVerificationOtp(PDO, userId, name, email)` generates a 6-digit numeric code, stores `sha256(code)` in `users.verify_otp_hash` with `verify_otp_expires_at = DATE_ADD(NOW(), INTERVAL 10 MINUTE)` (computed on the DB clock, same convention as password reset), and `sendAppMail()`s it via `verificationOtpEmailHtml()` — a `mailShellCode()` template (same branded card as `mailShell()`, but a large letter-spaced code block instead of a CTA button, since there's nothing to click). It returns a `dev_otp` only if the real SMTP send fails (and only on localhost), same convention as the reset flow's `dev_link` — SMTP is now live (see *Forgot password* above), so in normal operation the code is genuinely e-mailed and `dev_otp` stays null. **`verify.php`** (repo root, no auth) is a form page — not a link landing page — pre-filled from `?email=` when arriving from `login.php`'s Create Account success screen or its own unverified-block link; it POSTs `{email, otp}` via `fetch` to **`api/verify_otp.php`** (public), which checks `verify_otp_hash = sha256(otp)` AND `verify_otp_expires_at > NOW()` for an active, still-unverified patient, and on match sets `is_verified = 1` + nulls both OTP columns (single use) — one generic error ("Incorrect or expired code…") covers a wrong code, an expired one, or an unknown e-mail alike, so the endpoint never reveals which. On success the page swaps in a success card linking to `login.php`, matching the old link-flow's landing card. `api/request_verification.php` (public POST `{email}`, generic no-enumeration response + `dev_otp`) re-issues a fresh code for an **active, still-unverified patient** — wired to the "Resend it" buttons on `login.php`'s Create Account success screen and `verify.php` itself (the resend button that used to live inline on `login.php`'s blocked-login banner was replaced by a plain link to `verify.php?email=<email>`, since that page now owns the whole enter-code-and-resend UX). Schema: migration `041_email_verification_otp.sql` renamed `verify_token_hash` → `verify_otp_hash CHAR(64)` and added `verify_otp_expires_at DATETIME` (the original `is_verified TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` column from migration `024` is unchanged, as is its one-time grandfathering `UPDATE users SET is_verified = 1` for every pre-existing account).

**`verify.php`'s 6-box OTP input has `input-otp`-style motion (2026-09)** — the user asked to copy the feel of `kobra.systems/components/input-otp` (itself a wrapper around the `input-otp` React library with framer-motion). This app has no React/build step, so the same *behavior* was ported to plain CSS keyframes + vanilla JS rather than installing anything: each box still auto-advances/back-spaces/arrow-navigates as before, but now (1) a digit typed into a box plays a short spring-like pop (`anim-pop`, `otp-pop` keyframe — scale up then settle) and the box's background lifts from a flat tint to solid white/`otp-filled` once it holds a value; (2) pasting a full code staggers that same pop across the filled boxes 60ms apart (a `setTimeout` per index, not a real per-frame animation library) so it visibly "sweeps" left to right, and a paste that completes the code now auto-submits the same way finishing it by typing already did (paste previously did not auto-submit — the two entry paths are now consistent); (3) a failed verify (bad/expired code) adds `.otp-error` to `#otpBoxes` (a red ring on every box, `!important` so it beats the normal focus ring) and staggers an `anim-shake` across the boxes before clearing them, replacing the old plain "text error + silent clear"; (4) success staggers an `anim-success` sweep across all six boxes before swapping in the "E-mail verified" card, instead of an instant swap — this class does two things at once in one `otp-success` keyframe (a scale bounce **and** a momentary `ok`-green (`#1f9d63`) border/ring glow that fades in and back out), matching the reference's separate "bounce" + "trail sweep across all filled slots" effects as one combined pass rather than two independently-timed animations (renamed from an earlier `anim-bounce` that only did the scale, with no colour trail, after the user pointed at the reference again and said "there's an animation when u type the correct one" — a second WebFetch of the page confirmed its demo, which validates a hardcoded client-side code with no server round-trip, plays a `"otp-bounce"` class *plus* a separate `"otp-trail-success"` sweep element at 0.42s duration / 0.048s per-slot stagger); (5) **while the `fetch` to `api/verify_otp.php` is in flight (2026-09 follow-up — the user asked to "add the animation like its loading first"), every box pulses** — a staggered `anim-loading`/`otp-loading` keyframe (fade + shrink, 1s loop, 0.1s delay per box index, so it visibly waves left-to-right) plus `readOnly = true` on all six boxes so a re-submit or an edit can't race the in-flight request — started by `startLoading()` right after `btn.textContent = 'Verifying...'` and cleared by `stopLoading()` the instant a response comes back, before either the success bounce or `flashError()`'s shake plays, so the loading pulse and the outcome animation never overlap. **`api/verify_otp.php` on localhost answers in well under 100ms, which made the pulse flash by too fast to actually perceive** — the user pointed this out directly ("it will load first before seeing if its right"). The submit handler enforces a `MIN_LOADING_MS = 650` floor: it timestamps the request start and, once the real response lands, calls `afterMinLoading(fn)` which delays revealing the success/error outcome until at least that much time has elapsed since the request began (a no-op wait if the server itself was already slower than that) — verified against the real endpoint (not a mocked `fetch`) with a headless-Chrome poll loop confirming the pulse visibly holds before either outcome state appears, instead of both landing in the same frame. `MIN_LOADING_MS` is `0` under `reduceMotion`, so a reduced-motion user still gets the real, un-padded response time. **Two more timing/sequencing bugs surfaced when the user tried a *correct* code and said the whole thing felt "too fast" (2026-09, same follow-up):** (a) typing the final correct digit synchronously auto-submits the form in the same tick (`trySubmit()` → `form.requestSubmit()` → the submit handler runs immediately), so `startLoading()` used to stamp every box with `anim-loading` in that same tick too — which, since `.otp-box.anim-loading` and `.otp-box.anim-pop` both set the single `animation` shorthand property and `.anim-loading` is declared later in the stylesheet, instantly cut off the last-typed digit's own pop before it had a chance to visibly play. `startLoading()` now defers adding the pulse classes by a `setTimeout(…, 180)` grace period (readOnly-locking the boxes still happens immediately) so that last pop finishes first and the transition into "loading" reads as a handoff rather than a cut. (b) the success sweep staggered each box's `anim-bounce` start by a fixed per-index delay but then swapped to the success card on a **flat** `420ms` timer regardless — so every box after the first got its own bounce animation truncated mid-play, and the very last box (the one the eye is on right after typing it) barely animated at all before the screen changed. The swap timer is now computed as `(boxes.length - 1) * BOUNCE_STAGGER + BOUNCE_DURATION` (`BOUNCE_STAGGER = 55`, `BOUNCE_DURATION = 420`) — i.e. it waits for the *last* box's bounce to actually finish before swapping, so the full six-box sweep always completes. Re-verified end-to-end against a real disposable test account (a `users` row inserted directly with a known `verify_otp_hash`, deleted again after) typed digit-by-digit over the real network path: the loading pulse ran ~475ms→915ms, the bounce sweep ran ~915ms→1651ms (all six boxes visibly completing), then the success card appeared — a deliberate ~1.65s sequence instead of an instant jump-cut. All five are gated behind a single `reduceMotion` check (`matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')`) computed once at load — reduced-motion just clears/swaps instantly with no stagger, matching every other motion pattern in this app. `flashError(msg)` is the one shared helper both the bad-code branch and the network-error `catch` now call, so they get identical shake+ring treatment instead of the two branches drifting apart. Boxes also grew from `h-14 w-11 text-xl rounded-lg` to `h-14 w-12 text-2xl rounded-xl` and gained a muted `placeholder="0"` (shown only when empty), matching the reference's larger bold-monospace-numeral look. This is the only per-digit OTP UI in the codebase (`grep otp-box`) — if a second one is ever added, reuse this same CSS block rather than inventing another.

**Registration success screen has an inline 6-box OTP + auto-sign-in (2026-09) — replaced the earlier "Enter Verification Code" hand-off button to `verify.php`.** `login.php`'s Create Account success card now embeds the same OTP-box mechanics as `verify.php` (see the paragraph above) directly under the green confirmation banner (`#regOtpBoxes`, scoped CSS shared with the Forgot Password modal's boxes below), auto-focused on render, with a **"Reset Password & Sign In"**-style primary button — here **"Verify & Sign In"** — disabled until all 6 digits are entered and auto-submitting on the 6th. The banner's e-mail line carries an **"Edit Email"** toggle (`#regEditEmailBtn` → `#regEditEmailForm`, an inline `<input>` + Save/Cancel) for fixing a typo without re-registering, backed by **`api/update_pending_email.php`** — a public endpoint gated not by knowledge of the address but by `$_SESSION['pending_verify_user_id']`, a value `login.php`'s `form=register` handler stashes in the session the instant it creates the account (alongside `$_SESSION['pending_verify_email']`). Editing re-validates + uniqueness-checks the new address, updates `users.email`, and re-issues a fresh OTP to it. A 60-second resend countdown (`#regResendBtn`, plain `setInterval`, "Resend it (Ns)" → "Resend it") replaced the old always-enabled "Resend it" link. **`api/verify_otp.php` auto-signs the user in on a correct code** when — and only when — that same `$_SESSION['pending_verify_user_id']` matches the account just verified: it regenerates the session ID, sets the normal login session vars, `logActivity('auth', 'Signed in', …)`, clears the pending-verify session keys, and returns `{signed_in:true, redirect:'/dgt_dental_clinic/index.php'}` for the client to navigate to. Visiting the standalone `verify.php` page directly (no such session flag set) is unaffected — it still lands on the plain "E-mail verified — Sign In" card with no auto-login, since that request never proved it's the same browser that just registered the account.

**Forgot password is a two-step in-modal OTP state machine (2026-09, replaced the earlier e-mailed reset-link flow) — no navigation to a separate page.** The login page's "Forgot your password?" link opens `#fpOverlay`, which holds **two panels swapped in place, never both visible at once**: **Step 1** (`#fpForm`) is unchanged in shape — an e-mail field + **"Send Reset Code"** — and **Step 2** (`#fpStep2`, revealed the instant Step 1 succeeds) carries a green success banner naming the address with an inline **Edit Email** toggle (back to Step 1, address kept) exactly like the registration screen's own.

**Step 2 is itself two phases, shown one at a time (2026-09 revision — the user asked for "6 OTP first and new pass will pop up next" instead of showing the code and password fields together)**: **Phase A** (`#fpOtpPhase`) is just the 6-box OTP input (`#fpOtpBoxes`, CSS shared with `#regOtpBoxes` — see above), auto-focused on entry, plus the resend row below it. Typing the 6th digit auto-fires a check against the new **`api/check_reset_otp.php`** (public) — a read-only endpoint that validates `sha256(otp) = reset_token_hash` and `reset_token_expires_at > NOW()` **without consuming or mutating anything**, purely a UX gate so a wrong guess can be retried in place (shake + clear, same `flashOtpError()` treatment as elsewhere). Once it checks out, the boxes play the success bounce and, right after, **Phase A hides and Phase B (`#fpPasswordPhase`) reveals** — a "✓ Code verified" line, then **New Password** / **Confirm New Password** fields (`.pw-field` + `pwToggleBtn()`, the same show/hide-eye markup as `reset_password.php`) and the **"Reset Password & Sign In"** button, disabled until both passwords are ≥8 characters and match (a live mismatch message toggles as you type, mirroring `reset_password.php`'s own `check()`) — the OTP is no longer part of that button's gating, since it was already verified to get here. The resend row lives inside Phase A only (hidden once Phase B shows), since resending would silently invalidate the code just verified; the already-typed code is kept in a JS variable (`verifiedOtp`) rather than re-read from the (now hidden but not cleared) boxes. The actual mutation — setting the password and consuming the code — only happens on Phase B's submit, via **`api/reset_password_otp.php`**, which **re-validates the code from scratch** (so a code invalidated between Phase A's check and this submit — e.g. a resend fired from another tab — is still caught) rather than trusting Phase A's earlier check; on that rare failure the modal falls back to Phase A with an error instead of failing silently.

`api/request_password_reset.php` still always returns the same generic message (no account enumeration — the modal advances to Step 2 regardless of whether the address is real) but generates a **6-digit numeric code** instead of a 32-byte link token, storing `sha256(code)` in the same `users.reset_token_hash` / `reset_token_expires_at` columns as before (`DATE_ADD(NOW(), INTERVAL 15 MINUTE)`, DB clock) — only the value being hashed changed, so no schema migration was needed. It's e-mailed via `includes/mailer.php`'s `passwordResetOtpEmailHtml()` (a `mailShellCode()` template — the same big-letter-spaced-code layout as the verification OTP e-mail — which replaced the old link-CTA `passwordResetEmailHtml()`/`mailShell()` pairing for this flow); on localhost with SMTP disabled/failing it returns `dev_otp` in the JSON response (was `dev_link`). **`api/reset_password_otp.php`** validates the code (one generic error for wrong/expired/unknown-e-mail alike, same convention as `api/verify_otp.php`) and the new password (≥8 chars), then `password_hash()`es it, clears `must_change_password` and both reset-token columns (single use), and **signs the account straight in** the same way the registration flow's auto-login does (`session_regenerate_id`, session vars, `logActivity`, `redirect` to `roleHome()` or `index.php` for a patient) — no separate manual login step after resetting. **Real e-mail is live** — PHPMailer v7.1.1 is vendored at `includes/vendor/PHPMailer/src/` (no composer; `phpmailerAvailable()` in `mailer.php` requires its 3 files directly) and `config/mail.php` has `'enabled' => true` with the clinic's Gmail (`dgtonedental.bulacan@gmail.com`, `smtp.gmail.com:587` TLS, authenticated with a **Gmail App Password** — not the account's login password — generated at myaccount.google.com/apppasswords, which requires 2-Step Verification to be on). `sendAppMail()` returns `{sent:true, demo:false}` on a real SMTP accept; `dev_otp` (and the `logs/mail.log` fallback write) only fire if the SMTP send throws (caught in `sendAppMail()`), so the flow degrades gracefully instead of breaking. The branded e-mail's circular logo is a plain `<img src="{$logoUrl}">` built from `appBaseUrl() . '/assets/img/dgt_logo.jpg'` — **not** a `cid:`-embedded attachment (an embedded image is still technically a MIME attachment, and Gmail's inbox list shows a chip for it even when it's only used inline in the body; a real URL has no attachment part) — which only actually loads once the app is served from a real public domain, since on localhost Gmail can't reach it. `sendAppMail()` also auto-generates the PHPMailer `AltBody` plain-text alternative when a caller doesn't pass one (`verificationOtpEmailHtml()`/`passwordResetOtpEmailHtml()` don't) — it strips the whole `<head>` block first before `strip_tags()`, so the `<style>` block's CSS text can't leak into the plain-text body (which is what several mail clients, Gmail included, pull the inbox preview snippet from). **`config/mail.php` holds a live credential and must never be committed to a public remote** — see `.gitignore`. Schema unchanged: still migration `018_password_reset_tokens.sql`'s `reset_token_hash` / `reset_token_expires_at` columns, just holding a short code's hash instead of a long token's. **`reset_password.php?token=<raw>` (repo root) still exists as a dead-but-functional fallback** — nothing generates a long random link-token anymore, but the 6-digit code and the page's `?token=` check use the identical `sha256()` comparison against the identical column, so a manually-crafted `reset_password.php?token=123456` URL with a still-valid code would technically still work; it's simply no longer the primary (or advertised) path, since nothing links to it anymore.

**Custom 404 page (2026-09).** `404.php` (repo root) is a standalone DGT-styled
error page — logo + a tooth-icon chip (`bg-dgt-blue-50 text-dgt-blue-700`, the
same icon path as the admin dashboard's "Doctors on Duty" tile) + "Page not
found" + one line of copy + two buttons: a `dgt-teal-600` primary that goes to
`roleHome($me['role'])` for a signed-in user or `index.php` for a guest
(`currentUser()`, which doesn't redirect, unlike `checkAuth()`), and a plain
`history.back()` ghost button. It does **not** go through `layout.php` (a
broken URL may have no valid session/role context) and calls
`http_response_code(404)` itself. Wired via a root **`.htaccess`** —
`ErrorDocument 404 /dgt_dental_clinic/404.php`.

**Favicon set (2026-09).** `assets/img/favicon/` holds a real generated icon
set — `favicon.ico` (16/32/48 PNG-embedded frames, valid since Vista), 
`favicon-16x16.png` / `favicon-32x32.png` / `favicon-48x48.png`,
`apple-touch-icon.png` (180×180), `android-chrome-192x192.png` /
`-512x512.png`, and `site.webmanifest` (name/short_name, the two Android
icons, `theme_color #102f51`, `background_color #fff`). All generated from
`assets/img/dgt_logo.jpg` (2048×2048, mostly white padding around the mark) by
a one-off headless-Chrome canvas pass — same technique as the footer-flower
alpha-keying — that auto-detects the tight bounding box of the non-white logo
pixels, pads it ~7%, squares it, and re-draws it into each target size (avoids
guessing crop coordinates by eye). `favicon.ico` itself was hand-built (a
small Node script) as a standard ICONDIR + entries wrapping the three PNGs
directly — no GD/Imagick needed (GD isn't enabled on this PHP install).
Every page's `<head>` links the full set (`rel=icon` ico + 32/16 png,
`apple-touch-icon`, `rel=manifest`, `theme-color` meta) via `asset()`
(cache-busted `?v=<filemtime>`) — `includes/layout.php` (covers every
`modules/**` page), every public/auth page, and `404.php`; the two print-only
utility pages (`modules/shared/receipt.php`, `modules/admin/report_print.php`)
only get the tab-icon links (ico + 32/16 png), no manifest/apple-touch-icon,
since those aren't "add to home screen" surfaces. `.htaccess` also carries
`AddType application/manifest+json .webmanifest` — Apache doesn't know that
extension by default, and Chrome's manifest detection wants the real MIME
type.

**Create Account has an "I agree" checkbox again (2026-09, reinstated).** This churned twice: originally there was a required checkbox; then a small-print disclaimer line was added *alongside* it (a reference screenshot showed the OAuth-style "by continuing you agree to…" pattern), which duplicated the consent ask, so the user had the checkbox dropped in favor of the disclaimer alone; then the user asked for the checkbox back, so it now sits **above** the Sign Up button (the disclaimer line is gone, not both — the project's standing rule is still one consent pattern, not two): a `<label>` wrapping `<input type="checkbox" name="agree_terms" id="tcCheck" required>` plus "I agree to DGT One Dental Center's **Terms of Service** and **Privacy Policy**." Both are plain links (`#termsLink` / `#privacyLink`) that `preventDefault()` and open the same read-only T&C modal (`#tcOverlay`) rather than navigating away — Privacy Policy additionally scrolls the modal body to `#privacy` (an `id="privacy"` on `terms_content.php`'s §2 heading; there is no separate Privacy Policy page, since that section already covers the RA 10173 data-privacy consent). Opening/closing the modal to read it does **not** tick or untick the checkbox — they're independent; the modal's footer button stays labelled **Close**. The **Sign Up** button's disabled state (`refresh()` in the page's own script) now requires `tcCheck.checked` in addition to the existing field validations (name, birthdate, e-mail, phone, password length). The server re-checks it too — `login.php`'s `form=register` handler 422-equivalents with "Please agree to the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy to continue." when `$_POST['agree_terms'] !== '1'`, the same client+server pairing every other field on this form already gets. The modal's `#tcHeading` title text is still set dynamically per link (Terms link passes "Terms and Conditions", Privacy link passes "Privacy Policy") so the dialog reads correctly regardless of which link opened it, even though both draw from the one shared `terms_content.php` document. The standalone `terms.php` page (linked the same way from `includes/terms_content.php`) is unaffected. The agreement text itself still lives once in `includes/terms_content.php` (markup only — a `.terms-body` fragment; covers RA 10173 data-privacy consent, the **cancel-or-reschedule** rule (a Cancel forfeits the reservation fee; a reschedule made ≥ 24 h ahead keeps it), the **non-refundable-but-transferable reservation-fee** policy, slot-reassignment, GCash & bank reference-number and proof-of-payment requirements, and a "not for medical emergencies" disclaimer) and is `require`d by both the modal and the standalone `terms.php` page. T&C / modal styling is Tailwind utilities (the `TC_BODY_CLASSES` constant `terms_content.php` defines, applied to the wrapper by `terms.php` and the register modal via `ob_start()`).

Third-party JS is **vendored**, never loaded from a CDN: `assets/js/vendor/html2pdf.bundle.min.js`, `assets/js/vendor/swiper-bundle.min.js` (+ its stylesheet, `assets/css/vendor/swiper-bundle.min.css` — the one and only vendored CSS file, in a directory of its own since `assets/css/style.css` was deleted in the Tailwind migration), and `assets/js/vendor/tesseract/` (Tesseract.js 5.1.1 + its matching `tesseract.js-core` 5.1.1 — versions must match, the core package's `latest` tag is a breaking-change major ahead; `tesseract.min.js`, `worker.min.js`, the SIMD-LSTM-only core `tesseract-core-simd-lstm.wasm.js` + `.wasm` (the smaller LSTM-only build, not the combined legacy+LSTM core — plenty for reading printed receipt text), and `lang-data/eng.traineddata.gz` fetched from the same default source tesseract.js itself points at). Swiper powers `index.php`'s "Meet the Team" carousel — see the design-system section's `index.php` bullet for the full migration story and a Tailwind-CDN-vs-vendored-CSS specificity gotcha worth knowing before vendoring a second library. Revenue Reports (`modules/admin/reports.php`, guard `checkAuth(['admin','cashier'])` — same page for both roles) loads it via a `<script src>` in the page body. The **on-screen** "Patient Transactions" table (`#revTable` — # · Name · Specialist · Service · Date of Service · Status · **Cost**) carries an inline `style="table-layout:fixed !important;width:100%"` plus a `<colgroup>` (5 / 25 / 18 / 18 / 16 / 8 / 10 %; Date of Service is `whitespace-nowrap` left, Cost right-aligned via the `.amt` class) so its columns are evenly gapped regardless of data — this is separate from the PDF node below. One function — **`buildDoc(d)`** — builds the report document node (718px A4-portrait, all inline-styled: clinic header, "Revenue Report · range · N transactions · generated …" line, a `table-layout:fixed` itemized table with an explicit per-column % (# 4 / Name 24 / Specialist 18 / Service 22 / Date of Service 14 / Method 8 / Amount 10 = 100) so columns are strictly enforced and distribute evenly on any data; uniform 8px horizontal cell padding; `white-space:nowrap` + `font-size:8px` headers keep every header on one line (the Date of Service cells are `nowrap` too); all columns left-aligned except **Amount** (right, with `padding-right:16px` so a wide `₱30,000.00` stays inside the header block); the table itself is `overflow:hidden`. Columns: # · Name · Specialist · Service · Date of Service · **Method** (`payments.payment_mode` → Cash / GCash / **Bank** (short, the column is 8%) / Split / —) · Amount, plus a **Total Revenue** foot row. Both the toolbar **Generate PDF** button and the **Preview Report** modal's **Save as PDF** button call the same `generatePdf(btn)` — it renders `buildDoc()` in a brief off-screen overlay and runs **html2pdf** on it → a clean one-click download. **Neither uses `window.print()`** (removed 2026-09 along with the `body.rpt-print` / `@media print` isolation block) precisely so the PDF carries **no browser-injected date / title / URL / page-number header or footer** — the user asked for those gone. The **Preview Report** modal (`{wide:true}`) shows the same `buildDoc()` node for review, with **Save as PDF** + **Close**. **Official report metadata (2026-09, instructor feedback — churned through several passes, each one a direct instructor-feedback iteration)** — a `Prepared By: <name>` line (the logged-in staff/admin's `full_name`, falling back to `username`, passed into the client-side nowdoc via the same `__PLACEHOLDER__` + `str_replace()` technique `CLINIC` already used, since `$PAGE_SCRIPT` is a single-quoted nowdoc with no PHP interpolation) and a `Generated Date & Time: YYYY-MM-DD | HH:MM AM/PM` line (a dedicated `reportTimestamp()` JS helper — this exact format, distinct from the app's usual `toLocaleString` formatting) appear **once**, in a bordered footer block below the Total Revenue row — **not** in the document header (a header line showing the same two facts was tried, then removed as a plain duplicate of the footer, from a screenshot: "remove this one we already have at the bottom"). The header stays just "Revenue Report · range · N transactions", unchanged from before this feature existed. Both `buildDoc()`'s PDF/preview output and the standalone `report_print.php` view (`.rpt-footer`) carry the footer identically, so every export path (Generate PDF, Preview Report → Save as PDF, and the direct `report_print.php` URL's Print/Export PDF) has it. The live on-screen Revenue Reports page also shows a plain `Prepared By: <name>` line under the period label (untouched by the header-duplicate removal — that's a different context, not part of the printed document's own header). **Other shapes tried and discarded on the way here** — a header-only-"Report Generated" + footer-only-"Prepared By" split, and a literal signature-block footer (an underscore signature line + `Date Signed: ___` blank) — don't reintroduce either without the user asking again. (The `seed_demo_revenue` / `seed_demo_dashboard` demo-data generator scripts were deleted 2026-09 along with the seeded patient — there is no built-in sample revenue data any more; the reports populate from real bookings taken through the app.) `modules/admin/report_print.php` is a separate standalone printable view (its own Print / Export PDF buttons, `@media print` strips the shell) still reachable by direct URL. Both resolve their date window through `reportWindow()` in `includes/functions.php` (`period` = day/week/month anchored on `date`, or `range` with `from`/`to`). **The "Filter by:" tabs on `modules/admin/reports.php` read Day / Weekly / Monthly (2026-09)** — the user asked to relabel "Week"/"Month" to "Weekly"/"Monthly" ("make week and month to weekly n monthly"); an initial pass misread this as dropping the Day tab entirely and had to be reverted the same turn — Day is still there, unchanged, only the Week/Month button *text* changed (`data-p="week"`/`data-p="month"` values, and `reportWindow()`'s matching `period` values, are untouched — this was a label-only change). The custom **Date Range** inputs beside the tabs are unaffected. **A real, unrelated leftover was found and removed the same day**: 71 fake `completed` appointments named "DEMO SVC USAGE TEST" (ids 240–310, 71 `appointment_services` rows, 18 `payments` rows totalling ₱58,000) were still sitting in the live DB from an earlier session's Service Usage & Demand chart testing — a violation of the standing no-seeded-demo-data rule, silently inflating Revenue Reports, the dashboard's Revenue Trend and Service Usage charts, and the History Log across Oct 2025–Aug 2026. The user confirmed deletion via `AskUserQuestion` (initially scoped at just the 18 payment-linked rows before a fuller count turned up the other 53 payment-less ones); all 71 appointments + their `appointment_services` + `payments` rows were deleted, confirmed at zero afterward. The **Service** column in both the revenue rows and `historyLogRows()` is `COALESCE(a.service_id's name, GROUP_CONCAT of the appointment's availed procedures)` — so a row still names a service even when `a.service_id` is null (legacy walk-ins booked before the Service picker existed). **Revenue is recognised on `COALESCE(DATE(payments.paid_at), payments.payment_date)`** — i.e. by the settlement timestamp (`recomputePayment()` stamps `paid_at` when a bill flips to `fully_paid`), *not* the Payment Date field (no longer cashier-editable, 2026-09 — see the Billing screen paragraph below), which only backfills for a row with a null `paid_at`. The admin dashboard's **Revenue Summary** card used to show three fixed gross-revenue-to-date tiles (Today's/Weekly/This Month's Revenue, backed by `api/reports.php?action=analytics`) above its trend chart; both the tiles and that now-unused `analytics` action were removed 2026-09 at the user's request — see the Revenue Trend paragraph in the "Admin Dashboard" section below for the card's current, chart-only shape. Asset URLs go through the `asset()` helper (`includes/functions.php`) which appends `?v=<filemtime>` for cache-busting.

### Access control

`includes/auth_middleware.php` starts the session (`DENTALSESID` cookie) and exposes:
- `checkAuth($roles)` — page guard, `header('Location: ...')` + `exit` on failure (redirects to the user's own `roleHome()` when the role is wrong).
- `apiAuth($roles)` — API guard, emits `{ok:false}` + 401/403 and exits.
- `currentUser()` — request-cached `users` row; **auto-destroys the session if the account was archived mid-session** (`is_active = 0`).
- `touchPresence()` — called by both guards; a self-throttling (≤ once per 45 s, all on the DB clock) `UPDATE users SET last_seen_at = NOW()` for the logged-in user. Best-effort — wrapped in try/catch so a request never fails on it. `logout.php` nulls the column. Powers the admin dashboard's **Total Active Accounts** tile (staff whose `last_seen_at` is within the last 5 min). (**Assistant Dentists on Duty** no longer uses this — dental assistants are non-account staff and "on duty" is now an explicit `assistant_shifts` row for today, migration 038.)

Login roles: `admin`, `dentist`, `cashier`, `receptionist`, `patient`. `staffRoles()` in `functions.php` lists the non-patient **login** roles (admin / dentist / cashier / receptionist). Archiving a user is a soft delete via `users.is_active`.

**Dental assistants: a roster row, now with an optional real login (migration 038, extended by migration 058, 2026-09; display-renamed to "Assistant Dentist" 2026-09).** An assistant is still a `dental_assistants` roster row — `assistant_id`, `first_name` / `middle_name` (optional) / `last_name` + an auto-maintained `full_name` mirror (migration 039, same `personName()` convention as `users`), `contact_number`, `email`, `years_experience`, `about`, `avatar` (reserved), `is_active` — managed on the admin **Users → Assistant Dentists** tab (columns Avatar / Name / Email / Contact / Experience / Status / Actions, same shape as the receptionist/cashier table). What changed: migration 058 added a nullable `dental_assistants.user_id` FK → `users(user_id)` `ON DELETE SET NULL`, and the **Add modal now always creates a real login alongside the roster row** — First/Middle/Last Name, **Phone Number** and **Email Address** are both now **required** (the account needs an address for password reset), Years of Experience, About. `api/assistants.php action=add` runs the identical `verifyEmailExists()`/`makeUsername()`/`tempPassword()` pipeline every other staff-creation endpoint uses (`role = 'dental_assistant'`, `must_change_password = 1`, `is_verified = 1`), inserts both rows in one transaction, and returns `username`/`temp_password` for the same one-time "hand this over" success card every other role's Add flow shows. **A pre-058 roster row with no login (`user_id IS NULL`) gets one automatically the next time its profile is saved** — `action=update` detects the missing link, creates the account, and returns fresh credentials to show (the row's own **Edit Profile** button flags "No login yet. Edit the profile to create one." until then). `action=toggle_active` (Archive/Restore) cascades the same `is_active` flag onto the linked `users` row, so archiving an assistant deactivates their login too. `staffRoles()` now includes `dental_assistant`. **Permission scope, deliberately narrow and modelled on the receptionist's own non-clinical access** (per the job description: "track patient records to tell new vs. returning," "update simple records such as patient status and appointment details," never "clinical decisions or treatment plans"): `api/records.php` treats `dental_assistant` exactly like `receptionist` (the existing `$canClinical` gate already excludes anyone but dentist/admin, so no field-stripping logic needed to change) — same restricted Patient Details view (Personal Info + editable Contact Info + a bare Appointment History table, no tooth chart/medical history), but **`action=create` ("+ Create Record") stays receptionist/dentist/admin-only**, 403 for this role. `api/appointments.php action=check_in`/`undo_check_in` now also accepts `dental_assistant`, but a signed-in assistant can only ever tag **themselves** as the assisting staff (`assistantIdForUser($uid)` in `functions.php` resolves their own roster row and overwrites whatever `assistant_id` the client sent) — they cannot pick someone else, unlike the receptionist's own picker, which is unchanged. No other appointment action (`confirm`/`decline`/`reschedule`/`cancel`/`assign`) or `api/treatments.php` (procedure logging) is open to this role. **The new module, `modules/dental_assistant/`** — `dashboard.php` (sidebar label "Today's Patients"; a self on/off-duty toggle plus today's schedule with a New-patient/Returning badge per row, computed from a `prior_visits` subquery `api/appointments.php`'s list endpoint now returns, and a self-tagging **Check In** button) and `records.php` (a near-literal copy of the receptionist's own Patient Records page, minus "+ Create Record") — `includes/sidebar.php`'s `$navByRole['dental_assistant']` and `includes/auth_middleware.php`'s `roleHome()` both point here. `api/profile.php action=update` (the shared self-service profile editor) mirrors an assistant's own name/contact/email edit onto their `dental_assistants` roster row too, so the two never drift.

Everything from before migration 058 is unchanged: the **daily duty check-in** (the receptionist's "Assistant Dentists on Duty Today" widget, the admin dashboard's Assistant-Dentists-on-Duty drill-down, and now also the assistant's own dashboard toggle) all write the same `assistant_shifts` row (`UNIQUE(assistant_id, shift_date)`) for `CURDATE()` via `api/assistants.php action=set_shift` (`apiAuth(['receptionist','admin','dental_assistant'])` — a `dental_assistant` caller may only ever toggle their own `assistant_id`, resolved server-side, same self-only pattern as check-in); and **assist tags on a visit** — `appointments.checkin_assistant_id` (set by the front-desk check-in modal's optional "Assisting Staff" picker, or now by an assistant's own self-check-in) and `appointments.treatment_assistant_id` (set by the dentist's Log Procedure modal). Both pickers list only assistants on duty today (`dentalAssistantsOnDuty()` in `functions.php`); the APIs 422 an off-duty pick. **`dentalAssistantsOnDuty()`'s default date must come from the DB clock** (`SELECT CURDATE()`), not PHP's `date('Y-m-d')` — it was written the PHP-clock way until a 2026-09 bug fix, which on this box (PHP `Europe/Berlin`, MySQL on a different zone) meant every lookup disagreed with `set_shift`'s `CURDATE()`-based write for several hours a day, so a receptionist who had just marked someone on duty would see the Check-In modal's picker (and the check-in/treatment-log APIs' server-side validation) claim nobody was on duty. Same class of bug as `todayISO()` / `touchPresence()` / the treatment-log future-date guard — anything computing "today" for this app must use the DB clock, never PHP's. All of this is served by **`api/assistants.php`** (`list` / `on_duty` — GET, admin+receptionist; `me` — GET, the signed-in assistant's own profile + duty status; `add` / `update` / `toggle_active` — admin; `set_shift` — receptionist+admin+self). Physical duties (documented in `api/assistants.php` and the receptionist widget): room prep, instrument sterilization, four-handed assisting (instrument passing, suction), material prep. Verified end-to-end with a real throwaway account through the whole flow (admin-create → forced password change → self-duty-toggle → self-check-in → non-clinical records view → every disallowed action correctly 403'd and logged) before being deleted; a real, kept demo account (`assistant.demo`, see *Demo accounts*) was created the same way afterward for day-to-day testing.

**Pre-treatment "Ready for Treatment" verification (migration 060, 2026-09) — a refined Assistant Dentist workflow, translated from a pasted generic spec written in "routes/controllers/RBAC middleware" terms that don't exist in this codebase.** The spec asked for a pipeline "Scheduled → Checked In → Ready for Treatment → In Progress → Completed"; per this app's own standing rule against folding a pipeline stage into `appointments.status` (an earlier synthetic "Ongoing" status was built and reverted for exactly this reason — see the *Standardized status badge colours* paragraph below), "Ready for Treatment" is one more nullable `DATETIME`, `appointments.ready_for_treatment_at`, mirroring `checked_in_at` exactly, plus an optional `ready_notes VARCHAR(255)` free-text prep note. "In Progress" is never stored — this app has no "dentist started the procedure" moment (Log Procedure goes straight to `completed`) — it only ever renders as a small derived hint, never folded into the real status badge. `api/appointments.php action=mark_ready`/`undo_ready` (`apiAuth(['dental_assistant','admin'])`) require `checked_in_at` to already be set, reject a `completed`/`cancelled` visit, and for a `dental_assistant` caller enforce ownership **atomically in the UPDATE's own WHERE clause** (`checkin_assistant_id IS NULL OR checkin_assistant_id = <self>`, 409 on 0 `rowCount()`) rather than a read-then-write check, so two assistants racing on the same unassigned patient can't both pass a separate ownership read before either writes; `mark_ready` claims `checkin_assistant_id` for the caller via `COALESCE` if it was still unassigned (same convention `check_in` already uses) and is idempotent (`COALESCE(ready_for_treatment_at, NOW())`, so a double-click doesn't reset the "ready since" timestamp); `undo_ready` clears both `ready_for_treatment_at` and `ready_notes`, same ownership check. `modules/dental_assistant/dashboard.php` gained a new **"Pre-Treatment Verification Queue"** card above "Today's Schedule" — today's checked-in-but-not-yet-ready visits (derived client-side from the same `filter=today` fetch "Today's Schedule" already makes, no second API call), each with the existing New/Returning `visitBadge()` and a **Mark Ready for Treatment** button opening a small modal (modeled on the receptionist's own `checkInModal()`) with an optional prep-notes textarea. "Today's Schedule" itself gained a green "Ready for treatment" hint + an **Undo Ready** button once a row is ready — the existing self-check-in capability was deliberately left untouched, not removed, since the spec's "receptionist does check-in" reads as describing the normal path, not forbidding the assistant's existing fallback. `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s Queue table gained a small "Ready"/"Prepping…" hint next to the status badge for a checked-in row — purely informational, built as its own inline fragment next to `opStatus(a)`, never routed through `badge()`/`opStatus()`/`apptStatusBadge()` (which stay thin aliases over the real status only) — and **"Perform Procedure"'s enabled condition stays exactly `a.checked_in_at`, unchanged**, per the spec's explicit "the assistant's participation in the active procedure log remains optional/supportive" requirement: it must never gate the lead dentist. Nothing needed to change in `api/treatments.php` (already `apiAuth(['dentist','admin'])` only) or `api/records.php` (already treats `dental_assistant` exactly like `receptionist`) — both already satisfied the spec's "no treatment plans/diagnoses/billing" requirement; there is also no "prescription" endpoint anywhere in this app for the spec's access-block ask to apply to. Verified end-to-end against real accounts (not mocked sessions), on both local and live: a seeded checked-in test appointment correctly moved through mark_ready (idempotent on a repeat call) → a simulated race (`checkin_assistant_id` reassigned to a different assistant mid-flow) correctly 409'd both `mark_ready` and `undo_ready` → reassigning back to self and calling `undo_ready` correctly cleared both columns → `mark_ready` on a never-checked-in appointment correctly 422'd; the dentist-facing `filter=today` response was confirmed to surface `ready_for_treatment_at` once set. All test appointments were deleted afterward on both databases.

**Two same-day follow-up refinements to the above (2026-09), both against pasted "bug report" specs that turned out to be a mix of already-correct behavior and one real gap — each verified against the actual running app (real logins, real test appointments) before any code changed, not assumed from the report's wording.** (1) A report claiming receptionist check-in "automatically sets status to Ready for Treatment" did not reproduce — a live re-test of the real `check_in` action (with an assigned, on-duty assistant) confirmed `ready_for_treatment_at` stays `NULL` and `status` stays unchanged. The likely actual cause: "checked in" and "ready" only ever rendered as muted grey hint text, not a real badge, so the stage change could look like nothing happened. Fixed anyway, since it's a real presentation gap regardless of the report's own premise — `modules/dental_assistant/dashboard.php`'s Today's Schedule and Verification Queue tables, and `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s Queue table, now show a real **"Checked In"** (blue) / **"Ready for Treatment"** (green) badge pill next to the status, instead of small hint text — still purely derived/presentational, still never folded into `badge()`/`opStatus()` itself. (2) A follow-up report about "(None)" assisting-staff handling was two-thirds already correct — verified live: selecting "(None)" at check-in already saved `checkin_assistant_id = NULL` (via `check_in`'s existing `$asstId ?: null` handling), and `mark_ready` already auto-claimed an unassigned patient for the caller (`COALESCE(checkin_assistant_id, ?)`, from the original migration-060 pass above) — neither needed a change. The one real gap: the Pre-Treatment Verification Queue itself was **not** scoped to the caller at all — it showed every checked-in-not-ready patient clinic-wide, including ones already explicitly assigned to a *different* on-duty assistant. Fixed by adding `a.checkin_assistant_id` to `api/appointments.php`'s `list` SELECT (previously only the joined `checkin_assistant_name` was selected, not the raw id) and scoping the queue's client-side filter in `dashboard.php` to `!a.checkin_assistant_id || +a.checkin_assistant_id === myAssistantId` (`myAssistantId` captured from the existing `action=me` fetch `loadDuty()` already makes) — mirroring the exact same self-or-unassigned rule `action=mark_ready` already enforces server-side, just applied to what's *listed* too, not only what's editable. "Today's Schedule" (the full-day reference table) is deliberately left unscoped — it's meant to show everyone, unlike the action-oriented queue. Verified live with two simultaneously checked-in test rows (one assigned to a different assistant, one unassigned): the unassigned one correctly appeared in `assistant.demo`'s queue, the one assigned to someone else correctly did not. All test rows deleted afterward.

**The whole "Ready for Treatment" pre-treatment-verification feature above was removed again the same month (2026-09), per a follow-up pasted spec asking to simplify the workflow down to just two visible statuses, Approved and Completed.** The badge-visibility complaint that preceded it ("remove the checked in and ready for treatment on status its confusing" — trimming the derived badges out of the Status column, keeping the underlying mechanism) turned out to be a half-step; this pass removes the underlying mechanism itself, not just its display. Confirmed the exact scope via `AskUserQuestion` before touching anything, since a literal reading of the spec ("strictly use only two core statuses… remove Check In or Mark Ready buttons… Perform Procedure accessible directly for Approved rows") would also gut the receptionist's Check-In step and, with it, the admin dashboard's "Ongoing Patients" card (`checked_in_at`-keyed, and that dashboard's panel set is locked — never removed without being asked) — the user chose the narrower of the two options: **keep Check-In** (the receptionist/assistant "patient physically arrived" button, `checkin_assistant_id` assignment, and the Ongoing Patients card all stay exactly as they were), **drop Ready-for-Treatment entirely** (the assistant's separate verification queue/Mark Ready flow), and **stop gating Perform Procedure on check-in** — a dentist can now log a procedure on any `approved`/`rescheduled` row the moment it's in their Queue, whether or not the front desk has checked the patient in yet. Concretely: `api/appointments.php`'s `mark_ready`/`undo_ready` actions and their `ready_for_treatment_at`/`ready_notes` SELECT columns were deleted outright (confirmed via `curl` against both local and live that `action=mark_ready` now returns the generic `{"ok":false,"error":"Unknown action"}` 400, same as any other retired action); `api/treatments.php action=log` dropped its `empty($a['checked_in_at'])` 422 guard, leaving only the existing status/ownership/future-date checks (verified live: logging a procedure on a fresh `approved`, never-checked-in test appointment succeeded); `modules/dental_assistant/dashboard.php` lost the whole "Pre-Treatment Verification Queue" card, its `markReadyModal()`, and the `readyTable`/`readyCount` wiring — the page is back to just "My Duty Today" + "Today's Schedule" (Check-In/Undo check-in buttons unchanged); `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s Queue table lost its conditional "Awaiting check-in" placeholder — every queue row (already filtered to `approved`/`rescheduled`) now always shows a clickable **Perform Procedure** button next to **View**. The `ready_for_treatment_at`/`ready_notes` columns from migration 060 were **left in place on both databases, not dropped** — nothing reads or writes them any more, but dropping columns is a separate, more destructive step than removing the code paths that used them, and wasn't asked for. Deployed to live and re-verified there too (a live `mark_ready` POST against a real dentist session returns the same "Unknown action").

**Account creation (migration 028).** Every "Add …" modal on `modules/admin/users.php` collects **First / Middle (optional) / Last** name only — **no username field and no password field** for any role, admin included. `api/users.php action=add` builds `full_name` via `personName()`, the `first.last` username via `makeUsername()`, a **system-generated temporary password via `tempPassword()`** (10 chars, no-ambiguous alphabet), and sets **`must_change_password = 1`**. The response returns `username` + `temp_password`; the success modal shows both once (the temp password is not stored in plaintext or shown again) for the admin to hand over. There is **no forced change** — the holder signs in normally and `must_change_password = 1` just shows the soft reminder on their profile's Change Password card until they set their own (which clears it, as does `reset_password.php`). `action=reset_password` likewise generates a fresh `tempPassword()` and sets `must_change_password = 1`. Staff-created **patient** records (`api/records.php action=create`, used by the dentist's and receptionist's **+ Create Record** modals and the Front Desk walk-in "New patient" flow — First / Middle / Last, with a legacy single-`full_name` split fallback) are now **login-capable**: the endpoint issues a `tempPassword()`, sets `must_change_password = 1` **and `is_verified = 1`** (a staff member met them in person, so the e-mail-verification gate is skipped), **requires** an e-mail (for password reset) and rejects a duplicate one, and returns `username` + `temp_password`. The three patient-record modals mark First / Last / Age / Date / Time / **Email** with a red `*` (a per-file `REQ` const `<span class="text-danger"> *</span>`); the admin **Add User** modal (`modules/admin/users.php`) uses the same `REQ` const on its server-enforced required fields — First / Last **+ Email Address** (every role; `api/users.php action=add` requires a valid, unique e-mail for all roles), **Phone Number / Contact Number** (every role whose form has the field — patient / receptionist / cashier / assistant dentist; admin & dentist have no phone field and stay exempt), **Degree** + **PRC License No.** + **Specialties** (dentist). The walk-in "New patient" + both **+ Create Record** modals likewise mark **Phone Number / Contact No.** required (`_phoneOk(false)`, re-checked in `api/appointments.php action=walk_in` and `api/records.php action=create`). All three call sites show those once in an "Online login for &lt;name&gt;" modal for the front desk to hand over, so a walk-in patient can immediately book online under the same account (one record, one history). They still set their own password on first login via the soft reminder on their profile. `modules/shared/profile.php` edits First / Middle / Last (username stays read-only); `api/profile.php action=update` rewrites the `full_name` mirror and `$_SESSION['full_name']`.

**Editing an existing account from the admin Users table (2026-09).** Clicking anywhere on a row (not one of its action buttons) on `modules/admin/users.php` opens that person's editable profile — `editUserModal(u)` for the five login roles, `editAssistantModal(u)` (the pre-existing "Edit Profile" button's modal, just now also reachable by row click) for `dental_assistant`. Each `<tr>` carries `data-edituser="<id>"`; the shared `#userTable` click handler still lets Reset Password / Archive / Edit Profile's own buttons take priority and `return` first, so only a click elsewhere on the row falls through to the row-edit dispatch. `editUserModal()` mirrors the Add-form's per-role field set (admin: name + email; dentist: + degree/PRC license/doctor type/specialties/years/about; receptionist/cashier: + phone/years/about; patient: + phone) but pre-filled and with **no username or password field** — those never change here. It saves via the new **`api/users.php action=update`**, which re-validates everything the same way `action=add` does (required name, valid + unique e-mail excluding the row's own id, PH-mobile phone where that role requires one, a 7-digit PRC license + at least one specialty for a dentist) and never touches `username`/`password`/`must_change_password`; if the admin edits their own row, it also refreshes `$_SESSION['full_name']`, matching `profile.php`'s self-edit behavior.

**Real-time mailbox-existence check on the Add User modal, and the receptionist walk-in "New patient" modal (2026-09, generalized from Gmail-only to any domain by migration 051 the same month).** Originally scoped deliberately narrow to just `modules/admin/users.php`'s Add User modal's `#u_email` field (all 6 role tabs share it, since `openForm()` rebuilds it fresh per role), confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before building — the Edit User modal and patient self-registration (`login.php`) are still untouched. **Extended (2026-09) to the Front Desk Walk-in Booking modal's "New patient" `#w_email` field** (`modules/receptionist/dashboard.php`), confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`: they picked "walk-in only, client + server" over a client-only hint or extending to all three account-creation modals. Because the walk-in "New patient" flow shares its create endpoint (`api/records.php action=create`) with the dentist's and receptionist's own "+ Create Record" modals, the server-side gate added there (see below) also protects those two as an accepted side effect — but neither Create Record modal got the client-side inline check, so their `#*_email` field still shows no live red/green state; only the walk-in modal did. **The check was Gmail-only at first (only an `@gmail.com` address was ever looked up; every other domain got the pre-existing format+uniqueness check alone) and was generalized to any domain in a later 2026-09 pass**, at the user's request — the underlying multi-provider infrastructure needed no new provider or endpoint, only the Gmail-specific gate itself was removed, so `includes/gmail_verify.php` / `api/verify_gmail.php` / `config/gmail_verify.php` / the `gmail_verification_cache` table were all renamed to their domain-neutral `email_*` names (migration 051 does the table rename; `config/gmail_verify.php`'s real, already-live API keys were moved to `config/email_verify.php` byte-for-byte, no new keys needed) and `window.wireGmailCheck` in `app.js` became `window.wireEmailCheck`. On `blur` (not every keystroke — deliberately quota-conscious), `window.wireEmailCheck(input, opts)` in `app.js` (modeled on `window.wireRefCheck`, same `.is-invalid`/`.field-err`/`.field-err.ok` contract) calls **`api/verify_email.php`** (`apiAuth(['admin', 'receptionist'])`, POST `{email}`) and, on a confirmed-nonexistent mailbox, red-borders the field and shows **"This email address does not exist."** beneath it, disabling the modal's submit button (`#u_save` on Add User, `#w_go` on the walk-in modal). **On a confirmed-existent mailbox it shows the GCash-style green confirmation instead (2026-09, at the user's request, matching the patient booking wizard's own `wireRefCheck` green "✓ Reference number available" state)** — `.field-err.ok` + **"✓ Email address verified"** — but only when `api/verify_email.php`'s `source` is one of the real providers (`CONFIRMED_SOURCES` in `app.js`: `'abstract'`/`'emailverify'`/`'mailboxlayer'`), i.e. a provider genuinely confirmed it, whether just now or off an earlier cached real answer; a `fail_open` result also reports `valid:true` but renders as the plain no-message `clear` state, since "we couldn't reach a provider so we're not blocking" is not the same claim as "a provider confirmed this exists" and must not look like one. This is why `verifyEmailExists()`'s cache-hit branch returns the cached row's **real original `source`** rather than a generic `'cache'` label — the UI needs that distinction to survive a cache hit, not just a live lookup. A module-scope `Map` in `app.js` (`emailCheckCache`, storing `{valid, source}` per address rather than a bare boolean) means re-focusing/re-blurring the same value never re-checks it within the page's session, and still renders the correct green/red/clear state from the cached `source` on a repeat blur. **A neutral "Checking…" state (2026-09, at the user's request) shows while the network round trip to the real API is in flight** (`.field-err.checking`, muted grey, `tailwind.php`) — a cache miss can take a second or two against a real third-party API, and without this the field looked frozen/unresponsive during that window; a cache hit resolves instantly with no "Checking…" flash. A staleness guard (`isStale()`, comparing the input's current value against the value the pending request was for) discards a late-arriving response if the admin already changed the field before it resolved.

The real lookup lives in **`includes/email_verify.php`**'s `verifyEmailExists($email)` — a three-provider fallback chain: **Abstract API's Email Reputation product primary** (`emailreputation.abstractapi.com/v1/` — **not** the separate "Email Validation and Verification" product at `emailvalidation.abstractapi.com`, a different key/endpoint entirely; this account's key is only subscribed to Reputation, discovered by testing both live after the Validation endpoint rejected two independently rotated keys with a generic "Invalid API key" 401 while Abstract's own dashboard test button worked fine with the same key — **any spec that pastes the `emailvalidation.abstractapi.com` URL for this project is wrong for this account's key and must not be used**), **EmailVerify.io secondary** (`app.emailverify.io/api/v1/validate` — its own docs page pointed at a `/verify` path that 404s; the real endpoint was found via its PHP SDK's GitHub repo and confirmed live), **Mailboxlayer tertiary** (`apilayer.net/api/check`, `smtp_check` boolean — a key the user pasted while asking about MyEmailVerifier turned out, per its own dashboard screenshot, to actually belong to Mailboxlayer/APILayer instead; a claim read online that Mailboxlayer's free tier blocks HTTPS was tested directly and found false — both `http://` and `https://` worked identically with the real key) — that is cache-first against the **`email_verification_cache`** table (migration 050, renamed from `gmail_verification_cache` by migration 051 — `email` PK, `exists_flag`, `source`, `checked_at`; a real API answer is trusted for 30 days, a `fail_open` placeholder for only 1 hour so a transient outage isn't "confirmed valid" for a month but also isn't re-hit every request while it lasts). It **fails open** (treats the address as valid) whenever `config/email_verify.php` is absent/`enabled:false` (the default — this feature works with zero setup, always failing open with nothing configured), all three APIs are unreachable, or every one's answer is ambiguous (`unknown`/etc., **or a confirmed catch-all domain** — Abstract's `email_quality.is_catchall_email` / Mailboxlayer's `catch_all` boolean, checked since generalizing past Gmail-only: gmail.com is never catch-all, but plenty of ordinary business domains are, and on a catch-all domain a provider's "deliverable"/`smtp_check:true` signal can't actually distinguish this one mailbox from any other address at that domain, so it's treated as inconclusive rather than trusted) — only a clear `undeliverable`/`invalid`/`smtp_check:false` response from any provider, on a **non**-catch-all domain, ever sets `exists_flag = 0`.
