Response shapes differ per provider: Abstract is nested + lowercase (`data['email_deliverability']['status']`); EmailVerify.io is a flat top-level `data['status']` string; Mailboxlayer is a flat top-level `data['smtp_check']` **boolean** (checked with `===` against `true`/`false`, not a string comparison, so a missing/null value correctly falls through instead of matching either branch). `config/email_verify.example.php` is the committed, credential-free template (mirrors `config/mail.example.php`'s exact shape) — copy it to `config/email_verify.php` (added to `.gitignore` next to `config/mail.php`) with real `abstract_api_key`/`emailverify_api_key`/`mailboxlayer_api_key` values and `enabled:true` to turn on live checking. **All three providers live and independently verified in production with real keys (2026-09):** a real Gmail address returns `exists:true, source:'abstract'` (the normal path, since Abstract answers first for every address tested); a fabricated one returns `exists:false, source:'abstract'`; a repeat lookup of either hits the cache (still reporting the original real source) with no second API call. Each fallback branch was also verified in isolation by temporarily blanking the config down to just that one provider's key (then restoring the real file byte-for-byte, diffed to confirm) — under `emailverify`-only the same real/fake addresses resolved via `source:'emailverify'`; under `mailboxlayer`-only, via `source:'mailboxlayer'` — proving every branch works, not just the primary. **After generalizing past Gmail-only, a real Outlook address and a fabricated address on the clinic's own business domain were both tested live** — both returned `source:'abstract'` (the domain gate is genuinely gone, not just untested) — confirmed via the live cache table filling with non-Gmail rows. **ZeroBounce was tried as the secondary provider first, independently verified working, then removed at the user's request the same day in favor of EmailVerify.io** — same fallback role, different provider.

**This is enforced server-side too, not just as a UI hint** — `api/users.php action=add` **and `api/records.php action=create`** both call the identical `verifyEmailExists()` right after their existing format+uniqueness checks and 422 with the same `"This email address does not exist."` message on a confirmed-bad address, since a client-only check is trivially bypassed by disabling JS or calling the API directly; because the lookup is cache-first, the normal blur-then-submit flow only ever hits a real third-party API once (the client check populates the cache; the server's own check is a free cache hit). `action=update` (the Edit User modal) is deliberately **not** gated — out of the confirmed scope. Verified end-to-end, entirely off cache-seeded rows so no real third-party quota was spent confirming the wiring: a direct POST to `action=add` with a cache-seeded nonexistent address was rejected with the same 422; logged in as `reception.demo`, a POST to `api/verify_email.php` for that same cache-seeded address correctly returned `valid:false` (proving the widened `apiAuth(['admin','receptionist'])` guard works for the receptionist role, not just admin) and a POST to `api/records.php action=create` with it was rejected with the identical 422, while an ordinary address on the same endpoint succeeded normally and was cleaned up afterward.

**OTP fallback for an ambiguous address, Add User + Walk-in only (2026-09, migration 052).** `verifyEmailExists()` itself is unchanged and still always fails open on an ambiguous ("can't confirm either way") result, everywhere it's used — this matters because the dentist/receptionist "+ Create Record" modals share `api/records.php action=create` with the walk-in flow and have no OTP UI to satisfy a stronger gate, so that shared server-side enforcement had to stay exactly as it was. Instead, **`api/verify_email.php`** — the live client-side check, called only from the Add User and Walk-in "New patient" modals — now treats a `source:'fail_open'` result as a one-time-code challenge rather than silently accepting it: it calls `sendEmailOtpChallenge($email)` (`includes/email_verify.php`) to generate a 6-digit code, store its hash in the new **`email_otp_challenges`** table (10-minute expiry, 5 wrong-guess limit, one row per email, overwritten on every send/resend) and e-mail it via `sendAppMail()` + `mailShellCode()`/`emailOwnershipOtpEmailHtml()` (`includes/mailer.php`), then responds `{valid:false, source:'otp_required', otp_required:true}` instead of the usual `valid:true`. **`window.wireEmailCheck()`** (`app.js`) reacts to `otp_required` by revealing a small inline widget right under the field — a code input, **Verify Code**, and **Resend** (30s cooldown) — and keeps the modal's submit button disabled the whole time, exactly like a confirmed-bad address would. **`api/verify_email_otp.php`** (POST `{email, otp}`) checks the code against `email_otp_challenges`; on success it deletes the challenge row and writes `exists_flag=1, source='otp_verified'` straight into `email_verification_cache` — so `verifyEmailExists()` needs no code change to recognize it afterward, the field re-renders the same green "✓ Email address verified" state (`otp_verified` was added to `app.js`'s `CONFIRMED_SOURCES`), and the now-cached row also means the server-side `action=add`/`action=create` gate sees it as confirmed with zero extra plumbing. **`api/resend_email_otp.php`** (POST `{email}`) re-sends a fresh code without re-running the 3-provider chain (the address was already established as ambiguous by the original check). **This OTP requirement is a client-side UX gate only, not a new server-side hard block** — `verifyEmailExists()`'s own fail-open behavior is untouched, so a request that bypasses the client entirely (JS disabled, a direct API call) still succeeds exactly as before; the rationale, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`, is that the person being created an account for in these two specific flows is normally reachable on the spot to read the code back (a walk-in patient in person, or a new hire being onboarded by phone) — unlike, say, an admin adding an existing employee's account from their desk, which is why this was scoped to exactly these two modals and not extended to patient self-registration or the Create Record modals. Verified end-to-end with a real ambiguous address seeded into `email_verification_cache` (`source:'fail_open'`): `verify_email.php` correctly returned `otp_required:true` and populated `email_otp_challenges`; a wrong code was rejected and incremented `attempts`; 5 wrong codes in a row locked it out (a 6th attempt reports "too many attempts") and **Resend** issued a fresh, unlocked challenge; the correct code deleted the challenge row, wrote `otp_verified` into the cache, and a follow-up `verify_email.php` call for the same address returned `valid:true, source:'otp_verified'` straight from cache with no new e-mail sent. A real headless-Chrome pass against the actual Add New Doctor modal confirmed the same flow visually: the widget appears with **Add Doctor** disabled, a wrong code shows an inline error, and the correct code hides the widget, shows the green checkmark, and re-enables **Add Doctor**.

### Domain vocabulary (important)

The reference doc says "Secretary" and "Doctor"; **the UI of this app says "Receptionist" and "Dentist"**. `roleLabel()` in `includes/functions.php` is the source of truth for role display names. Exception: the per-role "Add …" buttons and "Add New Doctor" modal deliberately say "Doctor" to match reference screenshots.

`dentistSpecialties()` (in `functions.php`) maps specialty keys → labels and is shared by both service records and doctor accounts. `users.specialties` and `services.specialties` are **CSV of those keys**; unknown/custom keys are slugified on save and prettified for display (`specialtyLabels()` server-side, `prettyKey()` client-side). The admin **Add Doctor** modal now offers only the predefined specialty checkboxes — the free-text "Add a custom specialty" input was removed (the API's slugify fallback still handles any custom key that reaches it).

Dentists are tagged **owner** vs **roaming** (`users.doctor_type ENUM('owner','roaming') DEFAULT 'roaming'`, migration 011) but share the **exact same interface** — one set of dentist pages (My Schedule, Patient Records, Calendar), no per-type templates. Differentiation is purely `WHERE a.dentist_id = :logged_in_user_id` (already the default scope in `api/appointments.php` for role `dentist`). The Admin "Add Doctor" modal has a **Doctor Type** field, a **Degree** field and a **PRC License No.** field (`users.prc_license VARCHAR(50)`, migration 032 — **required** for a dentist, enforced client + server; shown as a column on the admin Users dentist table and returned by `api/users.php` `list`/`get`); `ownerDentistId()` in `api/appointments.php` is the default queue for new online bookings (`action=create` sets `dentist_id` on the pending row) and walk-ins. `action=dentists&type=owner` filters to owners for direct online booking; the receptionist reassigns any waiting patient live via `action=assign` (a "Reassign" button + all-dentists dropdown on the Front Desk **Approved Appointments** table). A roaming dentist's My Schedule / Calendar start empty ("No assigned patients for today." / "No upcoming appointments scheduled.") until the receptionist reassigns patients to them. Patient Records search stays universal for every dentist. The **receptionist** has the same Calendar page (`modules/receptionist/calendar.php`, an identical copy of `modules/dentist/calendar.php` guarded to `receptionist`) — for that role it shows every clinic appointment (no `dentist_id` scoping) and is read-only (View only). See pinned memory `dentist-roles.md`.

**Per-dentist working schedule (migration 031, `dentist_schedules`).** Each dentist has a **duty mode** (`users.doctor_type` — In-House Owner / Roaming Associate, migration 011) and a **recurring weekly grid** of `day_of_week` (0=Sun…6=Sat, PHP `date('w')`) × `time_slot` (`HH:MM`, a clinic slot) × `is_active` rows in `dentist_schedules`. Managed on the admin-only **Dentist Schedules** page (`modules/admin/schedules.php`, sidebar key `schedules`; **no visible `<h1>`** — the user had the page heading removed). Layout is a full-viewport (`min-h-[calc(100vh-150px)]` flex column) **weekly grid editor**: a toolbar row (name-only **Doctor** `<select>` — no duty-mode suffix/badge; quick-fill buttons **All / Monday-Friday / Mornings / Afternoons / Clear**; unsaved-state text; **Save Schedule**), then a `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_380px]` row — **Working Hours** (a `days (Mon–Sat) × time-slot` toggle matrix, `#matrix`, cells stretch to fill height; click a column/row header to toggle that whole day/slot) beside a right column stacking **Clinic Coverage** (`#coverage`, a compact fixed-size read-only same-shape grid tinting each cell by how many active dentists are on duty then — the selected doctor's cells track the live edit — plus a gap list / "every hour covered" line) over a **Doctors** roster (`#docRoster`, `flex-1`, scrolls) — every active dentist with an In-House/Roaming tag and their working days+times spelled out (days sharing a slot-set collapse to one line, e.g. "Mon–Sat 10:00 AM – 5:00 PM" / "Tue, Thu, Sat 1:00 PM – 4:00 PM"); click a row to switch the editor to that doctor. **Duty mode is NOT edited here** (the user had the Duty Mode card removed) — `users.doctor_type` is set on the **Users** page's *Add Doctor* modal; `api/schedules.php action=set_type` still exists but has no UI. Backed by `api/schedules.php` (`apiAuth(['admin'])`): `GET action=list` (**In-House/Owner active dentists only, 2026-09** — see the roaming-exclusion paragraph just below — + `is_owner` + `schedule` array of `{day_of_week,time_slot}` + `days`/`slots` option lists), `GET action=mine` (`['dentist','admin']`, own grid — unfiltered by type; a roaming dentist calling this themselves just gets their empty grid back, harmless), `POST action=set_type` (`{dentist_id, doctor_type}` → writes `users.doctor_type`, no UI), `POST action=save_schedule` (`{dentist_id, cells:[{day,slot}]}` — explicit day/slot cells; legacy `{days:[1..6], slots:['HH:MM']}` cross-product still accepted as a fallback — deletes then re-inserts, returns `cells:[[day,slot]]`). The matrix editor allows **different hours on different days** (the old pill editor forced the same slots every day). Helpers in `functions.php`: `dentistBookableSlots($id, $date)` → ordered `HH:MM` list the dentist works that weekday, intersected with `generateSlots()`.

**Roaming (on-call) dentists are excluded from fixed-schedule management entirely (2026-09, per an explicit instructor directive the user pasted verbatim: "Filter Fixed Schedule Views… ONLY for In-House / Owner Dentists… Roaming Dentist Interface Adjustment… Role/Type Check").** A recurring weekly grid is an In-House concept — a roaming dentist has no fixed shift and is instead assigned to a specific appointment by the receptionist upon approval (see the auto-routing/on-call-notification paragraphs below, unchanged). `api/schedules.php`'s admin `GET action=list` query now filters to `doctor_type = 'owner'` server-side (`WHERE role='dentist' AND is_active=1 AND doctor_type='owner'`) — this one query is the sole gate for the whole `modules/admin/schedules.php` page, since the Doctor picker, the weekly matrix, the Clinic Coverage board and the "Doctors" roster all just render whatever `dentists` the API returns; a roaming dentist never appears anywhere on that page, and the roster's now-always-"In-House" duty-type tag was dropped as dead weight (`renderRoster()` in the page's own script). `POST action=save_schedule` also rejects server-side (422, "Fixed weekly schedules only apply to In-House dentists…") if the target `dentist_id` resolves to a roaming dentist — the same client+server enforcement pattern used everywhere else in this app, since the UI hiding a roaming dentist from the picker doesn't stop a direct API call. `$requireDentist()` (the shared validator in `api/schedules.php`) now also returns the dentist's `doctor_type` for this check. **Nothing about the dentist-facing "My Schedule" page changed** — `modules/dentist/dashboard.php` was already purely Queue-based (today's approved/rescheduled visits + Recently Completed) for every dentist regardless of type, with no fixed weekly grid ever rendered there, so it already satisfied the instructor's "Roaming Dentist Interface Adjustment" ask (their primary view is the dynamic Queue, and they only ever see appointments actually assigned to them) before this change — confirmed by reading the file, not assumed. **The deeper on-duty/auto-routing engine (`dentistWorksOn()`, `dentistBookableSlots()`, `assignAppointmentDentist()`, `dentistsOnDutyAt()`, the receptionist's overload guard) was deliberately left untouched** — those already work off whatever rows exist in `dentist_schedules` regardless of `doctor_type`, and a roaming dentist's grid was already empty by default (`seedDefaultOwnerSchedule()` only ever seeds owners) before this change; removing the *admin's ability to fill one in by hand* doesn't change that engine's behavior, since a hand-filled roaming schedule was always an edge case the admin could optionally set, never something the auto-routing logic required. Verified live: `action=list` returns only the one active owner (`Raffy D.G. Tamayo`) even though 2 active roaming dentists exist in the same `users` table; a direct `action=save_schedule` POST against an active roaming dentist's `user_id` was rejected 422 with the expected message, while the same call against the owner succeeded — that owner's real 42-cell schedule was incidentally overwritten by this test and was immediately restored byte-for-byte via direct SQL afterward, re-confirmed via a follow-up `action=list` call returning the same 42 cells. **The grid is the sole source of truth: an empty grid (or nothing active on that weekday) → `[]` → the dentist is off duty and not bookable then** (changed 2026-09 at the user's request — emptying a dentist's grid on the Dentist Schedules page now takes them off duty everywhere; it used to fall back to "works every clinic slot"). Only a genuine DB / missing-table error still falls back to every slot, so booking never hard-crashes on a broken install. `dentistOnDuty($id, $date, $slot)` and `dentistWorksOn($id, $date)` build on it. `assertDentistOnDuty($id, $date, $slot)` in `api/appointments.php` 422s when a dentist is off their grid and is called from **`confirm`**, **`assign`** and **`reschedule`** (not `walk_in` any more — a walk-in is created with the Owner as a placeholder `dentist_id` and the real dentist is chosen + duty-checked at Approve). Booking integration (superseded by auto-routing below, 2026-09 — kept here for the walk-in/reassign single-dentist path, which is unchanged): `action=slots?dentist_id=` / `action=availability?dentist_id=` check one specific dentist's own grid+absences (used by the walk-in modal, which passes the Owner explicitly, and the Reschedule modals, which pass that appointment's own dentist); `action=dentists?date=&slot=` annotates each dentist with `works_today` / `day_slots` / `on_duty` so the receptionist **confirm** / **assign** pickers disable off-duty dentists (they fetch the appointment's date+slot first). The walk-in form no longer has a dentist picker.

**Reversed the same day: a roaming dentist is now always "on duty," every clinic slot, unconditionally (2026-09) — this directly contradicts, and supersedes, the "deliberately left untouched" claim two sentences above.** That claim held only as long as a roaming dentist's `dentist_schedules` grid stayed genuinely empty (the pre-058… pattern: no default seed for roaming, so `dentistBookableSlots()` returned `[]` for them everywhere, same as an in-house dentist off their own grid). In practice this session, a roaming dentist (`markjoseph.delacruz`) had accumulated real schedule rows (Mon–Sat 1–5 PM) from earlier ad-hoc testing before the fixed-schedule-page exclusion above existed — and because the shared duty engine still read that grid literally, the online-auto-routing overflow (see below) silently failed to hand him a booking at any hour outside those rows (e.g. an 11 AM slot he had no row for), even though he was active and available. The user caught this directly, testing a real 5-booking overflow at 11 AM and asking why it never reached him, then stated plainly: roaming dentists don't have a schedule — their whole role is to absorb overflow whenever the Owner is full, any hour, any day. `dentistBookableSlots()` in `includes/functions.php` now checks the dentist's `doctor_type` first: a `roaming` dentist returns every clinic slot (`generateSlots()`) unconditionally, skipping the `dentist_schedules` lookup entirely — any schedule rows a roaming dentist happens to have (stale test data or not) are simply never consulted again. A **`dentist_absences`** row still excludes them for that one specific date (checked first, before the roaming shortcut) — a roaming dentist can still be marked absent for a real day off; they're just never gated by a weekly grid. Because `dentistOnDuty()`, `dentistWorksOn()`, `assertDentistOnDuty()`, and `dentistsOnDutyAt()` all build on this one function, the fix cascades correctly everywhere a roaming dentist's duty is checked — the online-booking auto-router, the receptionist's confirm/assign/reschedule duty gate, the walk-in and reschedule modals' per-dentist slot pickers, and the admin dashboard's Doctors-on-Duty card (a roaming dentist now correctly shows as on duty every day, matching their actual on-call role, rather than "off duty" every day the admin hadn't hand-filled a grid for them). Verified against real seeded rows on both databases at the exact 11 AM slot that had failed: `dentistsOnDutyAt('2026-10-01','11:00')` now returns both dentists (previously just the Owner), and 5 sequential `assignAppointmentDentist()` calls correctly split 4-on-Owner/1-on-Roaming — matching the cap-based waterfall above — once 5 leftover real test appointments from the original failing screenshot (still sitting on live at that exact slot, all pre-fix-assigned to the Owner) were identified and deleted first, since they were silently skewing a first re-test. Deployed via FTP (scrubbed of the `/dgt_dental_clinic` prefix, per the standing deployment rule — `includes/functions.php` builds real asset/avatar URLs with it) and spot-checked live with zero prefix matches remaining.

**Online-booking auto-routing across on-duty dentists (2026-09).** Previously every online booking defaulted to the Owner Dentist's queue (`ownerDentistId()`) and the day/slot picker only ever showed the Owner's own working grid — a roaming dentist's schedule had no effect on what a patient could book online at all, only on what the receptionist could reassign a *pending* request to after the fact. The user asked for this to become automatic (translating a pasted "owner-primary, roaming-overflow, dynamic capacity" build spec down to something that keeps this app's own confirmed rule intact — see below): **`slotCapacity()` stays a flat 5 per date+slot, shared clinic-wide, unchanged** — the clinic's dentist's 2026-09 rule that capacity never scales with how many dentists are on duty was explicitly kept, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before building anything. What changed is *which on-duty dentist* a booking within that shared 5 goes to, and *whether a day/slot is even offered* when the Owner personally doesn't work it:

- **`dentistsOnDutyAt($date, $slot)`** (`api/appointments.php`) — every active dentist (owner or roaming) actually on their working grid for that exact date+slot (their own `dentist_schedules` row, minus a `dentist_absences` override), keyed by `dentist_id`.
- **`assignAppointmentDentist($date, $slot)`** — **revised a third time, 2026-09, to a "dynamic per-dentist cap" waterfall (`$hourlyCap = max(1, slotCapacity() - 1)`), replacing a flat-1-booking-per-dentist version from earlier the same day.** The flat-1 version (immediately below, kept as history) reasoned that a dentist can only physically see one patient in one exact hour, and gated purely on that — but walking through a concrete example with the user (5 bookings in one hour, 1 Owner + 1 Roaming on duty) surfaced that this wasn't the intended split: flat-1 sends the very 2nd booking to Roaming (since the Owner is already "full" the instant he has 1), then bounces back and forth as each one alternates free/busy, landing 4-on-Owner but via a scattered 1-2-1-1-1 pattern rather than a clean block. The user confirmed the actual intent is that the Owner should absorb almost an entire hour's shared capacity before Roaming ever picks one up — worked through explicitly as "Owner needs 4 before handing to Mark" against the same 5-booking/1-Owner/1-Roaming example. `$hourlyCap` is now `max(1, slotCapacity() - 1)` — "every chair in the shared hourly pool except the last one" — reusing the existing shared-capacity concept (`slotCapacity()`, a flat 5) rather than inventing an unrelated fixed number; with the current capacity of 5 that's a cap of 4, which reproduces the confirmed example exactly, and it scales automatically if the clinic's shared slot-capacity setting is ever changed. The three-key sort order itself (free-vs-busy under `$hourlyCap` → in-house-before-roaming tier → least-loaded tiebreak) is otherwise **unchanged** from the immediately-prior version — only the cap value changed. Net effect for the live 1-Owner + 1-Roaming roster in one hour: bookings 1–4 → Owner (Raffy), booking 5 → Roaming (Mark Joseph) — a clean 4-then-1 split, not an alternating one. Verified against real seeded rows on **both** local and live DB (an isolated CLI script running the exact function body — copied verbatim, not reimplemented — against the real 1-owner + 1-roaming roster at 2026-10-01 14:00, where Mark Joseph is genuinely on duty per his own Thu 1–5 PM `dentist_schedules` grid): 5 sequential calls to `assignAppointmentDentist()` landed dentist_id 82 (raffy.tamayo) four times then 103 (markjoseph.delacruz) once, on both databases; all seeded rows were deleted afterward. Deployed to live via FTP (no `/dgt_dental_clinic` prefix found) and confirmed the live site responds 200.

  *(Historical — the flat-`$hourlyCap = 1` version this replaced, live for less than a day in 2026-09): reasoned purely from "a dentist can only physically see one patient in one exact hour" — correct as a physical constraint, but not what the clinic actually wanted operationally (see above). Sort order was otherwise identical to the current version — same three keys, same tier rule, same least-loaded final tiebreak — only `$hourlyCap`'s value differed.)*

  *(Historical, one layer further back — the least-loaded/round-robin version this in turn replaced, 2026-09): picked whichever on-duty dentist currently had the fewest live bookings at that exact date+slot; the Owner broke a tie (so an empty slot's first booking still defaulted to the Owner), and a further tie among roaming dentists broke on the lowest `dentist_id`. Because every pick went to whoever was currently least loaded, load spread round-robin-fashion across the on-duty roaming dentists as a slot filled, with no separate rotation counter or state to track — this was itself a translation of the very same "owner-primary, roaming-overflow" pasted spec mentioned in this section's own opening paragraph, deliberately built as least-loaded round-robin instead of a literal waterfall at the time.)*
- **`action=slots`/`action=availability` with no `?dentist_id=`** (the patient online-booking calendar's own call, `dentist_id=null` per `wireReschedCal(null, …)`) now open a day/slot whenever **any** active dentist covers it — the union of every active dentist's own working grid for that weekday, each one's own `dentist_absences` still excluding just that one dentist from the union rather than closing the whole day. Previously a day the Owner didn't work (or was marked absent on) showed as closed to online booking even if a roaming dentist was fully available; verified live by temporarily marking the Owner absent on a test date — `action=availability` correctly kept the day open (all 7 slots) via the two roaming dentists' own coverage, and reverted to `closed` once queried with `?dentist_id=<owner>` explicitly (the walk-in/reschedule single-dentist path, unaffected by this change). A day/slot's `?dentist_id=` **explicit** call (walk-in, reschedule) is completely unchanged — only the no-`dentist_id` (online booking) path was widened.
- **Walk-ins are deliberately untouched** — a walk-in is still created with the Owner as a placeholder `dentist_id`, with the real dentist chosen and duty-checked by the receptionist at Approve, same as before; the front desk has a patient physically present and its own judgment calls (e.g. splitting an overflowing hour across whoever's actually in the building), so auto-routing wasn't extended there.
- **A roaming dentist gets an active on-call notification too (2026-09 follow-up, at the user's instructor's explicit requirement)** — a roaming dentist doesn't actively watch a fixed daily grid the way the in-house Owner does, so silently auto-routing/assigning them a booking (above) isn't enough; they need to be told. This is a deliberate, narrow exception to the standing `notifications-patient-only` rule (the global header bell — `$showBell` — is still patient-only and was **not** touched): `notifyRoamingDentistAssigned($dentistId, $appt)` (`api/appointments.php`) calls the plain, already-generic `notify()` (it only ever cared about `user_id`, nothing patient-specific) whenever the dentist actually being assigned or reassigned is `doctor_type = 'roaming'` — wired into **`action=confirm`** (Approve — the point the user's instructor specified, "upon approval," not the earlier, possibly-still-declined `action=create`) and **`action=assign`** (the receptionist's live reassignment, gated on `$dentistId !== $prevDentistId` so re-assigning to the same dentist doesn't re-notify). The notification payload is exactly what was asked: patient name, scheduled date, scheduled time slot, and procedure/service name, in one message string (the generic `notifications` table has no separate structured-payload columns, so this follows the same "title + one message string" shape every other `notify()` call in the app already uses). `api/notifications.php` itself needed **zero changes** — it was already `apiAuth()` with no role restriction and scoped every query to the caller's own `user_id`, so it worked for a dentist the moment something started writing rows for one. **`modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s original dedicated "On-Call Assignments" card was removed again the same day** (see the "simplified" paragraph two below) — the header bell (next paragraph) plus the Queue table already showing the assigned patient turned out to be the whole feature; treat any reference elsewhere to that card as historical.

Verified: a direct-logic test against the live DB confirmed three sequential auto-assignments for the same empty date+slot landed on Owner → roaming #1 → roaming #2 (least-loaded spreads evenly, owner-first only on the initial tie); the live `action=availability`/`action=slots` endpoints were confirmed to open a day via roaming coverage alone with the Owner absent, and to correctly stay closed when the same date was queried with the Owner's `dentist_id` explicitly; an ordinary day and Sunday closure were re-checked unchanged as a regression guard. The on-call notification was verified fully end-to-end against a real roaming dentist account (a temp-password reset + real login, not a mocked session): approving a real pending test appointment assigned to that dentist inserted the exact expected notification row (*"You have been assigned an on-call patient: … on 2026-10-06 at 2:00 PM for Restorative Dentistry."*), a headless-Chrome screenshot confirmed the dashboard card rendering it with a "1" unread badge, and clicking **Mark all read** correctly cleared the badge and the row's own button while leaving the message visible. All test rows/notifications were deleted afterward.

**A header notification bell was added for the same roaming-dentist on-call case (2026-09, same-day follow-up).** The dashboard card alone only works while the dentist is actually on "My Schedule" — the instructor's ask extended to a bell reachable from any page, matching the patient bell's own reach. This is a **second, independent bell**, not a reuse of the patient one — deliberately, to keep the `notifications-patient-only` exception narrow and auditable in one place rather than branching the shared patient-facing bell code on role. `includes/header.php` computes a second flag, `$showOnCallBell = $me['role'] === 'dentist' && $me['doctor_type'] === 'roaming'`, alongside its own title-scoped unread count (`WHERE title = 'New On-Call Assignment'`, matching the dashboard card's own scoping — not the patient bell's raw `is_read=0` count, since a dentist could in principle get some other notification type later and this bell must stay "on-call assignments only"), and renders a second `#oncallBellBtn`/`#oncallBellPanel`/`#oncallBellList`/`#oncallBellCount` block (same navy-circle button + dropdown-panel markup as the patient bell, copy-pasted with new ids rather than parameterized, since the two differ enough in behavior — see below — that sharing the same DOM/JS would mean branching every call on role anyway). The one shared thing that *was* refactored: the bell-icon hover "ring" animation, previously scoped to the literal `#bellBtn` id, now targets a shared `.notif-bell` class both buttons carry, so both bells animate identically without duplicating the `@keyframes` rule.

`assets/js/app.js` gained a parallel `loadOnCallBell()` / `refreshOnCallBellCount()` pair (both filter the same generic `notifications.php?action=list` response down to `title === 'New On-Call Assignment'` client-side — `api/notifications.php` itself needed no changes, still just scoped to the caller's own `user_id`) and reuses the existing generic `wireDropdown()` helper (extended with one more `if (btnId === 'oncallBellBtn') loadOnCallBell();` branch, the same way it already special-cases `'bellBtn'`). **Clicking a dropdown item behaves differently from the patient bell's own item links (which just `data-nav` to a notifications page) because the ask was "scroll to the relevant spot on the dashboard," not "go to a list page":** each item is a plain `data-oc-goto` element (not a real `<a href>`) whose click handler marks that one notification read (`action=read_one`) and then either scrolls directly (already on `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`) or hands off to it — `sessionStorage.setItem('dgt_scroll_oncall', '1')` followed by synthesizing and clicking a real `<a data-nav href="…/dashboard.php">` element so nav.js's own existing document-level click listener does the real SPA navigation (nav.js's listener silently no-ops a `data-nav` click whose `href` pathname already equals `location.pathname`, so hand-navigating was not an option for the same-page case — confirmed by reading `assets/js/nav.js` directly rather than assuming). `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s own `$PAGE_SCRIPT` (which nav.js re-runs on every swap) checks and unconditionally clears that flag on load and, when set, scrolls to and briefly flashes it (`.oc-flash`, a `prefers-reduced-motion`-guarded `box-shadow` pulse keyframe, `prefers-reduced-motion` itself falling back to an instant, non-animated `scrollIntoView`) — **the scroll target moved from the now-removed on-call card to `id="queueSection"` (the Queue card) the same day, see below.** Verified end-to-end with headless Chrome: the bell (with its correct unread badge) renders on an *unrelated* dentist page (Patient Records, not just the dashboard); opening it shows the right patient/date/time/service message; clicking the item navigates via nav.js to "My Schedule" and lands on the now-read notification (no "Mark read" button, badge cleared to 0). All test data was cleaned up afterward.

**Simplified the same day, at the user's request, citing the instructor's own follow-up feedback: the dedicated "On-Call Assignments" card is gone again.** The header bell (above) already surfaces the alert from any page, and an approved overflow booking already shows up as an ordinary row in the dentist's own **Queue** table (that table was never on-call-specific — it's just `appointments.php?filter=today` scoped to `dentist_id`, which a roaming dentist's newly-assigned appointment satisfies exactly like an in-house Owner's always has), so the standalone card was pure duplication once the bell existed. `modules/dentist/dashboard.php` dropped the whole conditional block (and the now-unused `$isRoaming` variable — nothing else on the page needs it) — the Queue card itself gained `id="queueSection"` and kept the `.oc-flash` highlight CSS (retargeted from `#onCallSection`), so the header bell's "scroll to it" behavior still works, just pointed at the Queue table instead of a separate section; the dashboard's own on-call-specific JS (`refreshOnCall()`, the `#onCallList`/`#onCallBadge`/`#onCallMarkAllBtn` wiring, its own 30s poll timer) was deleted outright rather than left dead. `assets/js/app.js`'s `scrollToOnCallCard()` was retargeted to `#queueSection` to match. **Nothing about the notification itself changed** — `notifyRoamingDentistAssigned()`, the header bell, and its dropdown are all exactly as documented above; only the dashboard's own now-redundant card was removed. Re-verified end-to-end with headless Chrome after the simplification: the bell still renders correctly on an unrelated page with the right badge/dropdown content; clicking an item navigates to "My Schedule" with **no** `#onCallSection`/`#onCallList` in the DOM any more, lands on `#queueSection`, and the approved patient is confirmed present as a real row in the Queue table (time, name, age, Online badge, Approved badge, View button) — exactly the "one single place" the request asked for. All test data cleaned up afterward.

**A full "see all notifications" page was added for the roaming-dentist bell too (2026-09, same-day follow-up)** — the user asked for it to work "like patient side," where the bell dropdown's own **See all notifications** link opens a full table. `modules/dentist/notifications.php` is a near-literal copy of `modules/patient/notifications.php` (same layout, same Mark read / Delete / Mark all read / Clear all actions, same `api/notifications.php` endpoints — that API needed no changes, it was already generic and user-scoped) with three deliberate differences: (1) the guard is `checkAuth(['dentist'])` **plus** an explicit `$me['doctor_type'] !== 'roaming'` redirect to `roleHome('dentist')` — an in-house Owner has no on-call notifications and no bell to reach this page from, so a direct URL hit just bounces them home; (2) the page title and `<h1>` read **"On-Call Assignments"**, not "Notifications," matching the bell panel's own heading; (3) `load()` filters the generic `action=list&all=1` response down to `title === 'New On-Call Assignment'` client-side — the identical scoping `loadOnCallBell()`/`refreshOnCallBellCount()` already use — so the page stays "on-call assignments only" even if some other `notify()` is ever aimed at a dentist later, rather than becoming a generic inbox. Mutations (`Mark read`/`Delete`/`Mark all read`/`Clear all`) call `window.refreshOnCallBellCount()` afterward (not `window.refreshBellCount()`, which is the patient bell's own counterpart) so the header badge stays in sync. `header.php`'s `#oncallBellPanel` gained the same footer bar the patient `#bellPanel` already has — a `<a data-nav href="/dgt_dental_clinic/modules/dentist/notifications.php">See all notifications</a>` — no new sidebar entry, matching the patient notifications page's own precedent of being reachable only from the bell, not the nav. Verified end-to-end with headless Chrome against the real roaming dentist account: the bell's unread badge read "1", the dropdown's "See all notifications" link existed with the correct href, clicking it correctly SPA-navigated (via nav.js) to the new page showing the one seeded on-call row, and **Mark all read** flipped it from "Unread" to "Read" and would have cleared the header badge on a live page. All seeded test data was deleted afterward.

**Per-dentist overload guard on the receptionist's Approve/Reassign dentist picker (2026-09, same-day follow-up — a real gap the user caught testing this).** The auto-routing above (`assignAppointmentDentist()`) always spreads a *new* booking to whichever on-duty dentist is currently least loaded, but nothing stopped the receptionist's own **Approve** (`confirmModal()`) or **Reassign** (`assignModal()`) dropdown from manually concentrating far more than one dentist's realistic share onto them — the dropdown only ever disabled a dentist who was **off their weekly schedule**, never one who already personally held a full plate of patients at that exact hour. Reported directly against a concrete case (Raffy, the Owner, already fully booked at a given date+time, yet still shown as a normal, pickable option in Approve). Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before building — of three options (hard block once a dentist personally hits the clinic's shared per-slot cap of 5; show a live count with no blocking; or a smaller user-specified per-dentist number), the user picked the hard block at 5, **not** a smaller number — this does **not** change the shared capacity model itself (`slotCapacity()` is still one flat total across every on-duty dentist, unchanged); it only stops that shared total from landing entirely on one dentist when others with room are on duty.
- **`api/appointments.php`'s `action=dentists?date=&slot=`** gained `slot_count` (that dentist's own live `pending`/`approved`/`rescheduled` count at the exact date+slot) and `slot_full` (`slot_count >= slotCapacity()`) per dentist, plus an optional `&exclude_id=<appointment_id>` that leaves one specific appointment out of every dentist's own count — without it, re-picking a dentist for an appointment already assigned to them would count that very appointment against them and lock them out of their own patient.
- **`assertDentistNotOverloaded($dentistId, $date, $slot, $excludeId = 0)`** is the server-side backstop (the UI restriction alone is trivially bypassable via a direct API call, same reasoning as every other client+server pair in this app) — wired into **`action=confirm`** and **`action=assign`** right alongside the existing `assertDentistOnDuty()` call, both passing the appointment's own id as `$excludeId` for the same self-lock reason as above.
- **`modules/receptionist/dashboard.php`'s `dutyMapFor()`** now takes a third `excludeId` argument (passed as the appointment id from both `confirmModal()`/`assignModal()`) and folds `slot_full` into the same disabled-options map `on_duty` already fed, labelling the option **"— Full (N patients already booked this hour)"** (vs. the existing **"— Off duty"**/**"— Not working"** labels) — `dentistOptions()` itself needed no change, since it already just renders whatever reason string it's given.
- Verified end-to-end: seeded 5 live appointments directly onto the Owner at one date+slot (matching the reported scenario) plus one separate pending request on a roaming dentist for the same slot; `action=dentists` correctly reported the Owner `slot_count:5, slot_full:true` while a roaming dentist with no bookings there showed `slot_full:false`; `?exclude_id=<one of the Owner's own 5>` correctly dropped his count to 4 and `slot_full` back to `false`; a direct `action=confirm`/`action=assign` call trying to newly assign the *other* pending request to the already-full Owner was rejected 422 with the expected message, while re-confirming one of the Owner's own 5 back onto himself still succeeded (proving `exclude_id` genuinely prevents a self-lock, not just theoretically). A real headless-Chrome pass through the actual **Approve Appointment** modal (a real receptionist login, not a mocked session) confirmed the rendered `<select>`: the Owner showed disabled with **"In-House — Full (5 patients already booked this hour)"**, an off-duty roaming dentist showed disabled with **"— Off duty"**, and the one dentist with room stayed selectable and became the default. All seeded test data was deleted afterward.

**Default schedule on duty-mode change (2026-09).** `seedDefaultOwnerSchedule($dentistId)` in `functions.php` gives a dentist the **full Mon–Sat grid** (every clinic slot, all 6 days) the moment they become **In-House (owner)** — an in-house doctor is in the clinic full time, so that's the sensible starting point. A **Roaming** dentist gets **no default at all** and starts with a genuinely empty grid (off duty everywhere) until the admin fills one in by hand on Dentist Schedules. The helper is a no-op if the dentist already has *any* schedule rows, so it never clobbers an admin's existing customization — it only fires the first time a dentist becomes owner with a still-empty grid. Wired at every place `doctor_type` can be set to `owner`: the admin **Add Doctor** modal (`api/users.php action=add`), the admin **edit-profile** modal (`action=update`, e.g. promoting an existing Roaming dentist to In-House), and `api/schedules.php action=set_type` (kept in parity even though that action currently has no UI). `schema.sql`'s fresh-install seed matches this policy too — it now backfills the full grid only for `doctor_type = 'owner'` rows (`elaine.silva`), not every dentist; `migrations/seed_demo_schedules.sql` still separately sets `associate.dentist` (Roaming) to Tue/Thu/Sat afternoons only, which runs after the schema seed regardless. This only governs *new* defaults going forward — no existing live dentist's schedule was retroactively changed by this. Weekly time slots (the `HH:MM` list) and the flat slot capacity of 5 stay clinic-wide.

**One-off dentist absence (migration 040, `dentist_absences`).** The weekly grid above says which weekdays a dentist *normally* works — it has no way to say "they were scheduled today but called in sick." `dentist_absences` (`dentist_id` + `absence_date` + optional `reason` + `marked_by`, `UNIQUE(dentist_id, absence_date)`) is that single-date override: a row here means the dentist is OFF duty on that exact date regardless of what the grid says, and it never touches the grid itself — clearing it (deleting the row) just restores whatever the grid already said for that weekday. It is checked inside `dentistBookableSlots($id, $date)` itself (an optional `$ignoreAbsence` param lets a caller ask for the grid-only answer, used to tell "not scheduled today" apart from "scheduled but marked absent"), so `dentistOnDuty()`, `dentistWorksOn()`, `assertDentistOnDuty()` (confirm/assign/reschedule 422s), the admin dashboard's **Doctors on Duty** card/drill-down, and `action=slots`/`action=availability`'s owner-dentist-based booking window (a day the Owner is marked absent on now closes exactly like a day off their grid) all pick it up with no extra wiring. Managed **admin-only**, from the admin dashboard's **Doctors on Duty** drill-down (`staffTable()` in `modules/admin/dashboard.php`) — a per-row **Mark absent** / **Mark present** button next to every dentist who is scheduled to work today (a dentist who isn't on the grid today shows "Not scheduled" instead, since there's nothing to mark them absent from); marking absent takes an optional free-text reason. Backed by `api/schedules.php action=set_absence` (`{dentist_id, absent, date?, reason?}`, `apiAuth(['admin'])`, defaults `date` to today) — an upsert (`ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE`) when marking absent, a plain delete when clearing it; both branches call `logActivity('appointments', …)`. The drill-down table's **On Duty Today** column shows a red **Absent today** badge (a new `absent` `BADGE_C` entry in `app.js`, distinct from the neutral grey "Off" used for an ordinary day-off) instead of the usual On duty/Off badge whenever `absent_today` is set.

**Marking a dentist absent auto-notifies their affected patients and unlocks patient-side rescheduling (2026-09).** Before this, `action=set_absence` only wrote the `dentist_absences` row — nothing told an affected patient their dentist wouldn't be there. Now, the moment a dentist is marked absent (not when clearing it), the endpoint queries every `pending`/`approved`/`rescheduled` appointment under that `dentist_id` on that `absence_date` and calls `notify()` for each one: title **"Doctor unavailable"**, message *"Your doctor is unavailable on \<date\>. Please reschedule or cancel your appointment."* — **never** the staff-only `reason` column or any health/personal detail (that stays visible only in the admin drill-down and the History Log's own `logActivity()` line for the same action). Best-effort, wrapped in try/catch — a notify failure never undoes the absence itself, same convention as `expireLapsedPendingAppointments()`.

On the patient side, `api/appointments.php`'s shared `list` query (used by every role, but only rendered specially for `patient`) now LEFT JOINs `dentist_absences` on `(dentist_id, scheduled_date = absence_date)` and returns a plain boolean `doctor_absent` per row (presence only, never the reason). `modules/patient/appointments.php`'s `card()` shows an amber `.appt-c-notice` box (same shape as the existing red `.appt-c-reason` box, new amber CSS added to `includes/patient_booking_style.php`) on any active, doctor-absent appointment, and that flag also **exempts the card's 24-hour reschedule gate** (`tooLate` no longer fires when `a.doctor_absent`) — the clinic caused this, not the patient, so it must never cost them the fee-transfer window. The reservation fee still transfers with no upcharge; nothing about billing changed.

The Reschedule modal (`reschedModal()`) additionally offers a **Dentist** `<select>` (populated from `action=dentists`, no date filter needed since `wireReschedCal()` already re-validates the target dentist's real working grid once a day is picked) whenever `appt.doctor_absent` is true — picking a different dentist calls a new `setDentist(id)` controller `wireReschedCal()` now returns, which reloads the month grid against that dentist's own schedule/absences without re-registering any DOM listeners a second time. `api/appointments.php action=reschedule` accepts an optional `dentist_id`: for a **patient**, switching dentists is only permitted when their *current* dentist is verified server-side to be marked absent on the appointment's own (pre-change) date — otherwise it 422s with "You can only choose a different dentist when your current one is unavailable," since ordinary reassignment stays the receptionist's separate `action=assign`. Staff may pass `dentist_id` freely (already trusted to reassign). Either way `assertDentistOnDuty()` still validates the (possibly new) dentist against the new date + slot before saving, and the notification/log line name the new dentist when one was picked. **A doctor-absence reschedule may land the same day too (2026-09 fix)** — ordinarily a patient reschedule needs tomorrow or later (`todayISO(1)` client-side, `+1 day` server-side), but that floor is the whole reason "same-day rescheduling to another on-duty doctor" wouldn't have worked at all, so both the client's `minDate` and the server's floor check drop to plain today (DB clock) specifically when `dentistAbsentNow` is true — staying on the same (absent) dentist still correctly 422s via `assertDentistOnDuty()`, only switching to a genuinely on-duty one for today is allowed.

**If the receptionist reassigns the affected appointment before the patient acts, the patient is now told (2026-09 fix)** — `action=assign` (the receptionist's own "Reassign" tool, same day/time, just a different dentist) previously only wrote the new `dentist_id` and logged it; it never called `notify()`, so a patient who'd already been told "your doctor is unavailable, please reschedule or cancel" would see that notice quietly disappear (the `doctor_absent` flag is a live JOIN, so it clears correctly on the next page load) with no explanation of what happened. `action=assign` now checks whether the appointment's *previous* dentist was marked absent on its own date and, only in that case, sends a follow-up notification: title **"Dentist reassigned"**, message naming the new dentist and confirming no further action is needed. An ordinary (non-absence) reassignment stays silent, unchanged from before. Both the receptionist's `assign` and the patient's own `reschedule` re-validate fresh state on every request (`assertDentistOnDuty()`, the absence lookup, the date floor), so if both somehow race, whichever loses just gets a clear rejection (e.g. the date floor reverting to "tomorrow onwards" once the absence is already resolved) rather than a corrupted appointment — there is no separate locking mechanism, none is needed at this scale.

**Clinic-wide** date blocking (closing the whole clinic for a day — distinct from a single dentist's working grid above): the `blocked_dates` table (unique `blocked_date`) is managed from **admin Settings** only (`api/settings.php` `action=block_date` / `unblock_date`, `apiAuth(['admin'])`; `action=blocked_dates` GET is any user). The **Block an Operational Date** card sits alone in the Settings right column with its **Blocked Dates** list capped (`max-h-[420px]`, `overflow-y-auto`, sticky `<th>`) + a count chip, so the list never pushes a sibling card down. A blocked date is rejected by `validateNew()`, `reschedule`, and `action=slots` just like a Sunday, and the dentist calendar hatch-shades it. Native `<input type="date">` can't grey out weekdays, so every **appointment** date field (patient booking + reschedule, receptionist walk-in + reschedule, cashier reschedule) is wired with `window.wireClinicDate(input, {msgEl?})` in `app.js` — on `change` it clears any Sunday or `blocked_dates` pick and shows a message. Do not use it on non-booking date fields (date of birth, payment date, report ranges, the admin block-a-date picker).

The bookable **time slots** are also clinic-wide, managed from the **admin Settings** page's **Clinic Time Slots** card (`api/settings.php` `add_slot` / `remove_slot`, `apiAuth(['admin'])`). `generateSlots()` (in `includes/functions.php`) reads the `time_slots` setting (CSV of `HH:MM`), falling back to hourly `clinic_open`–`clinic_close` when unset. The clinic runs **10:00 AM–5:00 PM with a 12:00 PM lunch break**, so the seeded `time_slots` are `10:00, 11:00, 13:00, 14:00, 15:00, 16:00` (migration `025_clinic_slot_config.sql`; `17:00`/5:00 PM was dropped by migration `053` — see the Calendar-View paragraph below — since a 1-hour visit starting then would run past the 5:00 PM close, so 4:00 PM is the last slot that finishes exactly on time). `api/appointments.php?action=slot_list` returns the list; every date/time picker (patient booking + reschedule, receptionist walk-in + reschedule, cashier reschedule) fetches it via `slot_list` instead of a hard-coded array.

**Slot capacity:** a date+time is *full* once it holds `slotCapacity()` = **a flat 5** live bookings (`pending`/`approved`/`rescheduled`) — the clinic runs 5 chairs an hour across both dentists, so capacity does **not** scale with the active-dentist count (it used to be active dentists × 2). `action=slots&date=` returns each slot's `available` flag; the patient booking + reschedule modals call `fillSlotOptions()` on date `change` to grey out full slots (labelled "— Full", or "— Fully booked, choose another day —" when none are free). `slotIsFull()` re-checks server-side in `action=create` (always), `action=walk_in` (**added 2026-09** — see the walk-in paragraph below; when a slot fills, the front desk now has to pick a genuinely open slot rather than stacking more onto it) and `action=reschedule` (patients only — staff may override). `action=availability&from=&to=` (≤ ~2 months per call, any auth) returns the same capacity model rolled up **per day** for a date range — `{closed,reason}` for Sundays / `blocked_dates`, else `{past, open, total, full, slots:{HH:MM:bool}, left:{HH:MM:int}}` (`left` = chairs still open that hour, `capacity − taken`) — and powers the patient **Calendar View** page (below).

### Core workflow (booking → treatment → billing)

1. **Patient** books online from the **Appointments** page (`modules/patient/appointments.php`, sidebar key `appointments`, and the patient's `roleHome()`): the **+ Request Appointment** modal is a 3-step wizard (`wizSteps()` — Appointment / Payment / Finished): **step 1 "Appointment Details"** (red title) = who-for radios (**For Myself** shows First Name / Last Name (joined into `patient_name`) + an editable Age; **For Someone Else** shows a **Person** `<select>` instead — see the *Booking "for someone else"* paragraph below), Age / Phone Number (`appointments.patient_phone`, migration 016 — prefilled from the account's `contact_number` for a self-booking; **Age is prefilled the same way for a self-booking, 2026-09** — `SELF_AGE` is computed once at page-render time from `patient_records.date_of_birth` via `TIMESTAMPDIFF(YEAR, date_of_birth, CURDATE())` (DB clock, same convention as everywhere else), blank when the record has no DOB on file (e.g. a staff-created patient, whose "+ Create Record" modal only ever collects a manual Age, not a birthdate); the "For Myself" / "For Someone Else" toggle fills/clears `b_age` alongside `b_first`/`b_last`/`b_phone`, and the field stays a normal editable `<input type="number">` — the patient can still correct it, exactly like the sibling self-fields), insurance, service, preferred date/time, concern. Required fields carry a red `*` (a page-level `REQ` const): First / Last / Age / Phone Number / Pick your Service / Preferred Date / Preferred Time, plus **Person** and **Relationship to Patient** when booking "For Someone Else" (typed by hand every time, even when re-picking someone booked before — see below); insurance + concern are optional. Step 2's **GCash / Bank Reference** fields and **Upload Payment Receipt** are also `*`-marked. **step 2 "Payment Information"** (red title) = an **Appointment Summary** card (fee / service / date / time / **Total Amount**), **Select Payment Method** radio-cards (`.pm-card` — **Bank Transfer / GCash** only; no PayMaya), an expanding `#pm_panel` (`payPanel()` — GCash: QR image + account name/number + numbered instructions + **GCash Reference No.** (exactly 13 digits); Bank: a **clinic-account block** (bank / account name / account number parsed from the `pay_bank_details` setting's 3 lines) + numbered instructions + **Bank Reference No.** only (10–20 alphanumeric) — stored into `payments.bank_reference` at `create`, `reservation_reference` still holds the plain ref number. **The "Bank / Branch" and "Account No." input fields were removed 2026-09, instructor feedback** — a bank transfer's single identifier is now just its reference number, matching GCash; see the dedicated paragraph in the cashier Billing section below for the full change across all three payment surfaces). `validatePayment()` blocks Submit on a bad field, red-borders it (`.is-invalid`) and shows an inline `.field-err`, cleared live on the next keystroke; the same rules are re-checked in `api/appointments.php action=create`. **Reference-number uniqueness:** `referenceInUse($ref, $excludePaymentId?, $excludeCounterId?)` in `api/_bootstrap.php` checks a value against `payments.reservation_reference` / `gcash_reference_number` / `bank_reference` **and** `counter_transactions.reference_no`; `api/check_reference_number.php?ref=&exclude_payment=` powers the debounced (400 ms) keyup/blur check wired by `window.wireRefCheck(input,{errEl,submitBtn,format,excludePayment})` in `app.js` — a duplicate red-borders the field, shows *"This reference number has already been used for another transaction"* and disables the submit button; a fresh well-formed value shows a green `.field-err.ok` "✓ Reference number available". It is re-enforced server-side in `action=create`, `payments.php action=settle` (excluding its own `payment_id`) and `action=counter_add`. A styled **Upload Payment Receipt** dropzone (`.rcpt-drop`, "Choose Receipt Image", live thumbnail preview) **with client-side OCR (2026-09)** — vendored Tesseract.js scans the uploaded image and auto-fills **GCash Reference No.** when it can read one, but the field always stays a normal editable input, so an unreadable scan just falls back to typing it in by hand (the pre-existing manual entry path was never removed). Four checks run on upload, in order: (1) **orientation** — a plain image-dimension comparison (no OCR involved), so unlike the three checks below it's enforced as a real gate: a landscape/sideways screenshot is rejected outright (`.text-danger`, "Error: Receipt must be in portrait orientation…", the file input is cleared) since official GCash receipts are always portrait; (2) if portrait, OCR runs and, when Tesseract's own confidence score is too low or it reads no text at all, shows "Error: Receipt image is blurry or unreadable…" as a **soft warning only** — never blocks or clears anything, since a real receipt can be valid but oddly cropped or lit; (3) if OCR found a plausible 13-digit run, it fills `#p_ref` (firing a `keyup` so the pre-existing `wireRefCheck()` duplicate-lookup below picks it up exactly as if the patient had typed it) and separately looks for a peso amount in the recognised text — if under the required reservation fee, shows "Error: Uploaded payment amount (…) is less than the required down payment (…)" as another **soft warning**, since the cashier verifies the real amount at billing regardless and a misread shouldn't block a legitimate booking; (4) **GCash-content check (2026-09)** — for the GCash payment method only (bank receipts have no equivalent — too many different Philippine banks/apps to build one reliable keyword list without false-positiving on legitimate ones), once OCR confidence clears the blur threshold above, the recognized text is checked for at least one real GCash marker (`gcash`, `g cash`, `express send`, case-insensitive substring match — genuine GCash receipts always name the app/feature somewhere on screen); no match shows "Error: This does not look like a GCash receipt screenshot. Please upload your actual GCash payment confirmation." and skips the remaining reference/amount/date checks below (pointless to run them against a document that isn't even a GCash receipt). Caught a real gap: a photo of an unrelated handwritten paper receipt had previously only surfaced as the generic "blurry or unreadable" message (low OCR confidence on handwriting), which didn't tell the patient *why* — this check gives a specific reason when the text **is** readable but simply isn't from GCash.
(5) **receipt-date recency (2026-09, tightened same day to require an exact same-day match, no grace window)** — `extractReceiptDate()` looks for a date in the OCR'd text (month-name "Sep 20, 2026", `MM/DD/YYYY`, or `YYYY-MM-DD` — GCash's own receipt format is the month-name style) and, when found, compares it to today: **any date other than today** — whether older (even by one day) or dated in the future — shows an error ("Error: This receipt is dated … not today. Please upload a screenshot of a payment made today." / a future-date mismatch warning) rather than the earlier 3-day grace window. Both are still **soft warnings**, same reasoning as the amount check, since OCR misreads (wrong digit, wrong month) are common and staff verify the payment at billing regardless; no date found in the text is silently skipped, not treated as an error. All warnings from steps (2)-(4) can stack (appended into the same `#rcptWarn` paragraph, space-separated) rather than one overwriting another. Never auto-fills over a value the patient already typed. The panel content comes from `settings.php?action=payment_info` (any auth). Back re-opens step 1 with everything preserved; **step 3 "Appointment Submitted"** = a status card — big `.submitted-icon` (amber clock → green check / red ✕ on resolution), `Ref: <payment reference>` + a **Pending Approval** badge, a Patient / Service / Date & Time / Contact `.kv`, an amber "waiting for approval from our front desk" note, and a `.submitted-poll` box ("Auto-checking status every 5 seconds" + spinner) — `poll` hits `appointments.php?action=poll` every 5 s and swaps the icon/badge/message on approve or decline. **Never says "secretary"** (front desk / receptionist only). The page shows a `.tab-row` (All / Pending / Confirmed / Rescheduled / Cancelled, each with a count) over an `.appt-grid` of `.appt-c` cards (friendly status badge only in the head — the `APT-00N` ref chip was removed at the user's request; then service, dentist, long date + time with inline-SVG icons, a "For: …" line for `booking_for = other`, the cancel/decline reason in a red box, and View Details / Reschedule / **Cancel** (`cancelModal()` → `action=cancel`, reason required — **re-added 2026-09 to match the reference doc's "Cancelation / Rescheduling module"**; a Cancel forfeits the non-refundable reservation fee, so the modal steers the patient to reschedule ≥ 24 h ahead instead). **The modal is itself a confirmation step, reworded 2026-09 at the user's explicit request** — title "Confirm Cancellation", an opening question line ("Are you sure you really want to cancel this appointment?"), then the non-refundable policy as a red `window.alertCls('error')` callout ("Please note: According to clinic policy, appointment down payments are strictly NON-REFUNDABLE upon cancellation.") folded together with the existing 24 h/reschedule guidance, the required Reason textarea, and two buttons — "Keep Appointment" (ghost, ends the flow with no API call) and "Yes, Cancel Appointment" (danger). Nothing is sent to `action=cancel` until that danger button is clicked with a non-empty reason (an empty reason just toasts an error and leaves the modal open); on success the toast reads "Your appointment slot has been cancelled." This did not change the underlying flow — the Cancel button already opened a modal rather than cancelling immediately — only the modal's copy, title, and button labels were rewritten to make the confirm-before-processing step and the non-refundable warning more explicit. Reschedule hides inside the 24 h window (`canResched`); Cancel shows whenever the booking is still `pending`/`approved`/`rescheduled` **and its date has not passed** (`canCancel = active && !past`). **Once the slot's start time is in the past both buttons disappear** (`past` in `card()`) and a "This appointment date has passed — contact the clinic, a new booking may be required" note shows instead; the server rejects a patient `reschedule`/`cancel` on a past row too (staff can still act, for cleanup). Stale `pending` requests are also aged out automatically — see the reschedule/cancel paragraph below. `statusOf()` maps `pending→Pending Approval`, `scheduled→Confirmed`, `rescheduled→Rescheduled`, else `Cancelled` (a declined request is just a cancelled card — its `decline_reason` still shows in the red box). **`loadAppts()` hides two kinds of row from the patient:** `completed` visits (they live on the Billing page) and any **`cancelled`/declined booking whose slot start is already in the past** (client-side filter only — the row + reason stay in the DB for staff and the History Log; the patient just stops seeing stale cancellations). A cancelled booking still dated today or later stays visible on the Cancelled tab. A small `N total · N active` count sits under the `<h1>` (a stat line, not a banned prose subtitle). The **Treatment & Billing** page (`modules/patient/dashboard.php`, sidebar key `billing`, `<h1>` "Treatment & Billing History") — off `appointments.php?filter=completed` — leads with three soft-tinted metric cards with icon chips (Total Visits `accent` / Total Billed `ok` / Outstanding `warn` — label + value only, the small sub-caption line was removed 2026-09), then an amber **Outstanding Balance** `.alert-warn` when anything is owed, then a plain table: Date (`YYYY-MM-DD`, **stacked with the appointment's time beneath it in smaller muted text, 2026-09** — see the stacked-date-cell paragraph below; this table only, not the other billing/treatment tables) · Patient. **Total Visits and Outstanding are still lifetime totals; Total Billed is today-only (2026-09)** — `loadBilling()`'s client-side sum only adds a completed appointment's `total_amount` into `billed` when its `scheduled_date` (sliced to `YYYY-MM-DD`) equals `todayISO()`, so the card reads "how much was billed for treatments completed today," not the account's all-time billed total; `outstanding` (and the row count feeding Total Visits) are unfiltered, unchanged (name + dentist) · Service (`serviceSummary()`, with the dentist's **Treatment notes** — the part of `appointments.notes` after the `Treatment notes:` marker, parsed by a local `dentistNotes()` mirroring the shared modal — shown as a small muted `DENTIST'S NOTES: …` line beneath when present) · Total · Balance (₱0 muted when clear, else amber amount + a `.pay-bar` installment progress bar + "N% paid") · Status (`Fully Paid` / `Partial` / `Unpaid` badge) · actions **View** (shared `appointmentModal`) + **Receipt** (`modules/shared/receipt.php`, new tab).

**Receipt image-manipulation / AI-generation flag (migration `055`, 2026-09) — server-side, advisory only, never blocks the patient's booking.** In addition to the client-side OCR checks above (orientation, blur, amount, receipt-date recency — all patient-facing soft warnings), `api/appointments.php action=create` independently re-inspects the actual saved receipt file on disk via `inspectReceiptForManipulation($filePath)` (`includes/functions.php`) — never trusting anything the client claims about the file. It checks four signals in order of reliability:
1. **C2PA content-provenance metadata** (the industry-standard "Content Credentials" manifest — a JUMBF/CBOR box — that modern AI image generators embed). The single most reliable field is `digitalSourceType`; a value containing `trainedAlgorithmicMedia` (or `compositeWithTrainedAlgorithmicMedia`) is the official IPTC vocabulary marker for "this asset was produced by a generative AI model," used industry-wide (OpenAI, Adobe Firefly, Google, Microsoft Designer, …) — not a guess or keyword heuristic. **Verified directly against a real ChatGPT-generated fake GCash receipt** the user supplied (the exact kind of fabricated "proof of payment" this check exists to catch) — its manifest contained `"softwareAgent":{"name":"ChatGPT","version":"gpt-image"}` and the `trainedAlgorithmicMedia` marker; `inspectReceiptForManipulation()` correctly returned `suspected:true` with a reason naming ChatGPT specifically.
2. A known AI-generator name (`ChatGPT`, `gpt-image`, `DALL-E`, `OpenAI`, `Midjourney`, `Stable Diffusion`, `Stability AI`, `Adobe Firefly`, `Google Imagen`, `Microsoft Designer`, `Bing Image Creator`, `Copilot Designer`, `Leonardo.Ai`, `NightCafe`) found anywhere alongside a `c2pa`/`claim_generator` marker, for a manifest that doesn't carry the `digitalSourceType` field above. (Since the manifest is CBOR-encoded binary, not JSON/XML text, tool names are recovered via a plain substring scan rather than parsing the manifest structure — the tool-name strings themselves are still stored as literal, searchable ASCII even though the surrounding structure isn't.)
3. The EXIF `Software` tag (`exif_read_data()`, when the format supports it).
4. A raw-byte scan for conventional desktop-editor signatures — `Canva`, `GIMP`, `Paint.NET`, `Affinity Photo`, `Adobe Illustrator`, `CorelDRAW`, `Pixlr` — covering PNG/WEBP too, which `exif_read_data()` doesn't support at all. **Deliberately excludes `Photoshop`/`Adobe Photoshop` and `Adobe XMP Core` from this raw scan (2026-09 fix, a real false positive caught by the user)** — those two specific strings are also generic boilerplate: `"Photoshop 3.0"` is the de-facto standard identifier for a JPEG's APP13/8BIM metadata *container format*, echoed verbatim by plenty of unrelated JPEG-writing libraries (phone OS image pipelines, messaging-app re-encoders, compression tools) with no actual Photoshop editing involved, and `"Adobe XMP Core"` is similarly just the version string of Adobe's freely-licensed XMP toolkit, which many camera/phone software links against for routine non-editing metadata. Confirmed live: a completely genuine, unedited GCash screenshot the user tested with contained a literal `"Photoshop 3.0"` marker near the start of the file and was wrongly flagged before this exclusion — inspecting the raw bytes directly confirmed it was the sole trigger (no other signature matched). A real Photoshop edit is still caught reliably via the **structured** EXIF `Software` tag (step 3), which doesn't share this false-positive problem since it's a properly-labeled field a generic encoder has no reason to set, rather than a raw substring any encoder might happen to echo.

**Deliberately does *not* treat a receipt with no metadata at all as suspicious** — these uploads are phone screenshots, not camera photos, and a genuine screenshot never carries camera EXIF (no lens/GPS/device info) to begin with, so "no metadata" is the normal case, not a red flag; only a positive match on one of the four signals above counts. (A real, unedited GCash screenshot containing an in-app promotional banner — e.g. GCash's own "claim your reward" upsell — is correctly **not** flagged by this check, since nothing about that file's metadata indicates editing or AI generation; only forensically-detectable manipulation triggers it, not merely content a human finds suspicious.) The result is still stored on the `payments` row (`receipt_flag_suspected TINYINT(1)`, `receipt_flag_reason VARCHAR(255)`) — `inspectReceiptForManipulation()` still runs on every `action=create` and the columns are still populated — but **the staff-facing display was removed (2026-09, at the user's request)**: the receptionist's **Appointments for Approval** Receipt column no longer shows the red "⚠ Warning…" line under **View**, and `receiptModal()` no longer shows the alert banner above the receipt image (its signature dropped the now-unused `flagSuspected`/`flagReason` params, and the `View` button no longer carries `data-flag`/`data-reason`). The flag is written and available for a future consumer but currently has no UI anywhere in the app. The admin **Appointment** page (`modules/admin/appointments.php`) has no receipt-viewing UI at all (it's a filterable history/audit table, not an approval queue), so there's nothing there to attach this flag to either.

**Also mirrored client-side, in the upload box itself (2026-09 follow-up) — unlike the paragraph above, this one *is* patient-visible.** The user first asked for the flag to stay staff-only (so a submitter can't learn their fake was caught and iterate); then, after testing with a real ChatGPT-generated fake receipt and seeing no inline feedback, asked for it to show the same kind of "Error: …" red text the other upload checks already show right in the dropzone. `modules/patient/appointments.php`'s `scanReceiptForManipulation(file, warnEl)` is a byte-for-byte JS port of `inspectReceiptForManipulation()`'s signature list and priority order (`trainedAlgorithmicMedia`/`compositeWithTrainedAlgorithmicMedia` → named AI tool + `c2pa`/`claim_generator` → desktop-editor signatures), reading the file via `file.arrayBuffer()` decoded with `TextDecoder('iso-8859-1')` (a byte-safe 1:1 char mapping, the JS equivalent of the PHP version's raw `stripos()` scan) — verified against the same real ChatGPT-generated file, a fake-Photoshop-signature file, and a clean zero-metadata file, all three matching the PHP version's results exactly. It's called from `#p_file`'s `change` handler alongside the existing `scanReceiptForReference()` OCR scan, both writing into the same stackable `#rcptWarn` paragraph via a shared `addReceiptWarning()` helper (factored out of `scanReceiptForReference()`'s previously-local `addWarning()` closure). **This client-side copy is a UX convenience, not the enforcement** — being visible (and thus technically discoverable/bypassable by disabling JS) is fine precisely because the server-side check in `api/appointments.php action=create` never trusts the client and re-runs the identical inspection against the actual saved file regardless of what the browser did or didn't show.

**Stacked date+time cell on the Treatment & Billing History table only (2026-09).** The user first asked for this on every similar billing/treatment table, then explicitly asked to remove it from all of those and keep it on Treatment & Billing History alone. `assets/js/app.js` has **`window.dateTimeStack(dateHtml, slot)`** right next to `fmtTime()` — takes the caller's own already-formatted date HTML and appends a `<div class="mt-0.5 text-[11px] font-normal text-steel-400 dark:text-steel-500">` sub-line with `fmtTime(slot)`, or nothing if there's no slot. Its only caller is `modules/patient/dashboard.php`'s Treatment & Billing History table's Date column. `modules/cashier/transactions.php`'s Patient Transactions "Date of Service" column, `modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s Recently Completed table, and `modules/cashier/logs.php`'s Transaction Logs Date column were all reverted back to their original, non-stacked rendering (the transactions page's inline `fmtDate(...) · fmtTime(...)`, the dentist page's date-only cell, and the logs page's `paid_at`/`scheduled_date` fallback with no time at all) — `api/payments.php`'s `action=logs`/`action=history` query no longer selects `a.time_slot` either, since nothing there needs it any more. Do not re-spread this pattern to other tables without being asked again.

**Calendar View** (`modules/patient/calendar.php`, sidebar key `calendar`, `<h1>` "Book an Appointment") is a month-grid booking calendar — `includes/patient_calendar_style.php` (`.pcal-*`, legacy palette, its own widget, *not* the staff `calendar_style.php`). Each day is driven by `appointments.php?action=availability`: Sundays / blocked dates render hatched "Closed"; genuinely past days, the 2-day lead-time window, and other-month days are greyed and inert; a bookable day shows a plain green left edge, a fully-booked day a plain red left edge + light red fill (the earlier amber "few left" ≤ 2 state was **removed** 2026-09 — any day with at least one open slot is just plain "open" green now, no intermediate tier). Clicking a day opens a panel of that day's time slots — each free slot is an `<a data-nav>` to `appointments.php`, each taken one a dashed "Occupied" chip (renamed from "Full" 2026-09 — see below). **The per-slot "N of 5 open" chair-capacity count under each time was removed 2026-09** (the user found the raw chair-count fraction confusing sitting directly under a specific time — it describes the clinic's shared 5-chairs-per-hour capacity, not anything about that one dentist or that one patient) — a free slot now shows only the time itself; `.pcal-slot` in `includes/patient_calendar_style.php` got a `min-height` so a one-line free slot and a two-line ("time" + "Full") taken slot still render the same box height. **The day-cell's own "N slots open" / "Full Schedule" text tags were also removed (2026-09, a later same-topic pass)** — a day cell now carries *no* text badge at all, just the plain colour-coded left edge (green/red) and, for a closed day, the "Closed" tag only; `cellInfo()` in all three calendar surfaces (`modules/patient/calendar.php` and the reschedule-modal copies in `modules/patient/appointments.php` / `modules/receptionist/dashboard.php`) now returns `tag: ''` for the `open` and `full` cases, so the `<span class="pcal-tag">` never renders for either — the CSS `.pcal-cell.open`/`.pcal-cell.full` border-left/background rules are untouched and still carry the colour on their own.

**Reversed again 2026-09 — the day-level and slot-level text tags are both back, per an explicit instructor rule the user pasted verbatim** ("Time Slot Status Wording (Individual Hours)" / "Day-Level Status Wording (Entire Dates)"), which distinguishes the two levels precisely: an individual hourly slot must read **"Available"** (green) or **"Occupied"** (red, replacing "Full"), while the word **"Fully Booked"** is reserved strictly for a whole calendar date where every bookable slot that day is taken — a day with at least one open slot must read **"Available"**, never a bare colour with no label. The underlying data already matched this exactly (`action=availability`'s per-day `full` flag was already `$open === 0` — "zero slots left anywhere that day" — never a soft/partial state), so this was a pure wording/labelling change, not a threshold change: `cellInfo()` in all three calendar surfaces (`modules/patient/calendar.php`, and the reschedule/walk-in-modal copies in `modules/patient/appointments.php` / `modules/receptionist/dashboard.php`) now returns `tag: 'Fully Booked'` for the `full` case and `tag: 'Available'` for the `open` case (closed/out-of-range cells are unchanged — `'Closed'` / no tag) — the existing `.pcal-cell.open`/`.pcal-cell.full` `.pcal-tag` CSS (green/red pill, already defined, previously just unused) needed no changes to pick this up.
