The last three are **always shown as a pair, universally** rather than the app's usual either/or framing (a full payment shows "Change", an installment shows "Balance after downpayment") — computed fresh as `amountDue = max(bal − paidNow, 0)` and `change = max(paidNow − bal, 0)`, which are naturally mutually exclusive (only one is ever non-zero) and need no payment-type branch to pick a label. `billing.php`'s `totalPaidNow()` (replacing the first pass's per-method `paymentBreakdownRows()`) sums the live form's currently-entered payment amount(s); `receipt.php`'s PHP side uses `$collected` (falling back to `$received` when the per-method split columns were never populated — the same real gap the first pass's version had already found and fixed, carried forward here since the fallback is still needed). The "Amount in words" line (kept from the first pass, a real Philippine official-receipt convention, not decoration) stays positioned right under the totals block.
   - **Notes / Remarks — removed outright (2026-09)**, at the user's request ("do not add notes/remarks on receipt on cashier"). The block (the dentist's own treatment notes, parsed off `appointments.notes`'s `Treatment notes:` marker) had been kept from the first A5 pass through the monochrome redesign above; it's now deleted from both `modules/shared/receipt.php` (the `$rawNotes`/`$remarksText` PHP computation and the conditional `<div class="inv-remarks">` block) and `modules/cashier/billing.php`'s `buildPrintDoc()` (the matching JS `rawNotes`/`remarksText` + its conditional markup), plus the now-dead `.inv-remarks` CSS (base rule, its print-narrower override, and its entry in the print `page-break-inside: avoid` selector list) in `includes/invoice_style.php`. The dentist's treatment notes are still visible elsewhere (the shared `appointmentModal()` View modal, the patient Treatment & Billing table's "DENTIST'S NOTES" line) — only the printed/saved invoice document no longer carries them. Verified against a real payment with genuine treatment notes on file (appointment #328) — the rendered receipt has no `.inv-remarks` element and no "Notes / Remarks" text anywhere, ending cleanly at "Thanks for visiting us!".
   - **Signature and footer wording reverted to the plain original phrasing this spec asked for** — the single centered `.inv-sign2` block (kept from the first pass, replacing `#invRoot`'s own two-sided `.inv-sign`) still reads "Authorized Cashier / Staff"; the footer line reverted from the first pass's "Thank you for choosing DGT One Dental Center!" back to the literal **"Thanks for visiting us!"** the new spec quoted; the disclaimer is unchanged.
   - **The signature block was pushed lower on the page, closer to "Thanks for visiting us!" (2026-09, a later pasted spec — same fix applied to both the patient receipt and the cashier invoice, since both read this one shared `includes/invoice_style.php`).** The pasted spec's own literal CSS (`.signature-table { margin-top: 55px/60px }`) was **not** used as-is — a fixed pixel push is exactly what an earlier pass in this same section already fixed once ("Compacted (2026-09, fixing a real 2-page overflow with 4-6 service rows)"): it adds a flat gap regardless of how much room is actually left on the page, so it's either too small on a short bill or, on a fuller 4-6-item one, tips the signature/footer onto a second page — precisely the overflow the spec's own requirement 2 asked to avoid. Instead, `.inv-sign`'s print-mode margin became `margin-top: auto !important` (moved off `.inv-footer-thanks`, which used to own this trick alone) — a flex-column `margin-top:auto` claims only whatever vertical space is genuinely still free on that one page: a short invoice has lots of it, so the block visibly drops low, near "Thanks"; a fuller 4-6-item invoice has little to none, so the push shrinks toward zero and the block just follows the content — it can never by itself force a second page. `.inv-footer-thanks` dropped its own `margin-top: auto` in favor of a small fixed `margin-top: 10px`, so it trails immediately below the (now independently pushed-down) signature block rather than each claiming a separate share of the leftover space and visibly drifting apart from each other. `page-break-inside: avoid` on `.inv-sign`/`.inv-footer-thanks` (the spec's requirement 2/3) was already in place from the earlier pass — no change needed there. Verified with a real headless-Chrome CDP session (not visual inspection): against a real 1-item settled invoice, the signature sits at 86% of the page height with an 18px gap to "Thanks" and 1 PDF page; against a throwaway 6-line-item invoice (4 services + 2 add-ons, seeded and deleted afterward) on **both** `modules/shared/receipt.php` and the cashier `modules/cashier/billing.php`'s Print Preview (`#invPrintRoot`, triggered via a real click on `#printBtn` so `buildPrintDoc()` actually ran), the signature sits at 88% of the page height with the same 18px gap — still exactly 1 PDF page on both. The local `cashier.demo` demo account (deactivated by default) was temporarily reactivated to run this verification and restored to deactivated immediately after.
   - Verified end-to-end with headless Chrome against the same real seeded payment (`payment_id 85`) as the first pass: a plain fully-paid case rendered correctly monochrome (a full-DOM background-color sweep found zero color anywhere); a second pass with `sc_pwd_discount`, a promo, and a GCash reservation deposit temporarily written onto the same row (restored byte-for-byte afterward) rendered every conditional row correctly with consistent arithmetic (`Total` → `Less: Amount Paid` → `Amount Due`/`Change` balancing exactly); `billing.php`'s own live Print Preview click path was independently re-checked (not just `receipt.php`) and confirmed byte-for-byte consistent with it; a real `Page.printToPDF` capture confirmed the A5 page size survived the `@page margin:0` + content-padding change.
5. **Admin** gets the Dashboard (`<h1>` "Dashboard Overview"), Revenue Reports (Day/Week/Month/range + printable), the **Appointment** page, the **Dentist Schedules** page (per-dentist weekly working grid — see the per-dentist-working-schedule paragraph in *Domain vocabulary*) and the **History Log** page. Admin sidebar order: Dashboard · Appointment · Dentist Schedules · Services · User Accounts · Revenue Reports · History Log · Settings.
   - **Appointment** (`modules/admin/appointments.php`, sidebar key `appointments`, `<h1>` "Appointment") — a filterable/sortable table of every appointment (status dropdown + date range), with a **Type** column (Online / Walk-in badge from `appointments.booking_type`), **Export CSV** (`reports.php?action=logs_csv`), **Remove Selected** bulk-delete (`reports.php?action=delete_logs`, cascades to that appointment's payment + services rows), and a 30-second auto-refresh toggle (a sliding switch, off by default, toasts "Data refreshed" on each auto reload). The admin **Dashboard** now uses the same sliding-switch style for its **Auto-refresh (30s)** toggle (default on, silent), and its `· updated <time>` timestamp text was removed. It still uses `api/reports.php` `action=logs` / `logs_csv` / `delete_logs` (function `historyLogRows()`). *(This page was `modules/admin/logs.php` — the old "History Logs" — until 2026-09, when that file was repurposed as the Activity Log and the appointment table moved here.)*
   - **Audit Logs** (`modules/admin/logs.php`, sidebar key `logs`, title "Audit Logs" since 2026-10-16; was "History Log", and "Activity Log" for one iteration in 2026-09) — the **staff system audit trail**, admin-only, backed by `api/logs.php` (`apiAuth(['admin'])`, `action=list`). Columns: **Timestamp** (`Sep 3, 2026 10:15 AM`), **User** (`first.last`), **Role**, **Category** (**Authentication** / Appointments / Records / Billing badge — the `auth` slug displays as "Authentication" via `CAT_LABEL` in the page JS and the Category `<option>`; the stored value stays `auth`), **Action Description**. Filters: Role `<select>` — `api/logs.php` always returns the full role set (**Admin, Dentist, Receptionist, Cashier, Patient**) merged with any other role present in the log (so an older `dental_assistant` row still surfaces its option) — Category `<select>`, From / To date inputs, a debounced Search box (matches `username` or `action`), Clear, and the 30 s auto-refresh toggle. Rows come from the `activity_log` table, written by **`logActivity($category, $action, $actor = currentUser())`** in `includes/functions.php` — a best-effort insert (swallows every error so auditing never breaks the request; `username` / `role` are snapshot at write time). The `$action` text **starts with a capitalised verb and never repeats the actor's username** ("Checked in Rosa Lim", "Signed in", "Processed a ₱1,500.00 GCash payment …") — the table's User column already shows who did it; `logActivity()` strips a stray leading `<username> ` and capitalises the first letter as a backstop. Migration `030_activity_log_strip_username_prefix.sql` retro-cleans older rows (idempotent). Instrumented events: **auth** — sign-in (every role, incl. patient), sign-out, failed sign-in attempt, blocked deactivated sign-in, admin account creation, admin forced password reset, self-service password change (flags when it clears a temp password from a forced reset), **denied-access attempt** (an authenticated user whose role is refused by `checkAuth` / `apiAuth` — logged by `auditAccessDenied()` in `auth_middleware.php`) (`login.php`, `logout.php`, `api/users.php`, `api/profile.php`); **appointments** — **patient online booking request** (`Submitted an online booking request (Appointment #N) …`), approve, decline, check-in / undo, walk-in booked, reschedule (with reason), dentist reassigned, visit completed + N procedures logged — all keyed `Appointment #<id>`; `reschedule` and `cancel` are callable by the patient on their own booking too, and the row's Role then reads **Patient** (`api/appointments.php`, `api/treatments.php`); **cancel** (patient or staff, reason required — re-added 2026-09) logs `Cancelled Appointment #N …`; **records** — patient-record create, clinical-chart / medical-history edit, dental procedure notes added, contact-detail update (`api/records.php`, `api/treatments.php`); **billing** — payment processed (method — Cash / GCash / Bank Transfer / Split with per-method amounts — plus `Invoice #<payment_id>`, SC/PWD + promo discounts, "invoice charges edited" when line items change, "official receipt issued" on full payment), counter transaction recorded (`api/payments.php`). No appointment listings live in this table — those are strictly on the Appointment page. (The `seed_demo_activity_log.sql` demo seed was deleted 2026-09 with the rest of the demo-patient fixtures; the log fills from real signed-in activity.)

**Admin Dashboard — the operational cards** (`modules/admin/dashboard.php`, `<h1>` "Dashboard Overview", the top row directly under the title; backed by `api/dashboard.php?action=operational`, admin-only, polled every 30 s via the **Auto-refresh (30s)** toggle beside the `<h1>` — default on, matching the Appointment page; the analytics section below does *not* poll). There is **no "Clinic Operations" heading** and no card-group dividers (a grouping pass was reverted 2026-09). Six floating metric cards in one `#opsCards` grid (`grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4` for the first four, `grid-cols-2` for the trailing pair; Today's Schedule the double-wide featured navy card), **every one clickable** (`role="button"` + Enter/Space, a faint `›` affordance) → a `{ wide: true }` drill-down modal. **"Total Active Accounts" (the 7th card, a login-presence tile with a `staff_list&key=online` drill-down and the app's one surviving "Online now" pill) was removed at the user's request (2026-09)** — `api/dashboard.php?action=operational` no longer computes or returns `online`/`online_total`, and `action=staff_list`'s `key=online` branch is gone along with it:
- **Today's Schedule** — the **expected queue**: today's `approved`/`rescheduled` bookings the patient has **not arrived for yet** (`checked_in_at IS NULL`). The front desk's **Check In** moves a row straight out of this card and into **Ongoing Patients** — the two never overlap. (A `pending` one isn't confirmed yet, so it's on neither.) **Ongoing Patients** — today's checked-in visits the cashier **hasn't billed yet**: a patient enters at check-in and stays through the dentist visit **and** the trip to the cashier, dropping off once the cashier processes **any** payment — a full settlement *or* an installment downpayment (`payment_status` becomes `fully_paid` **or** `partially_paid`) — or the visit is cancelled. A `completed` visit whose bill is still `unpaid` / has no payment row stays on the card. **Pending Schedule** (labelled "Pending Requests" until 2026-09; `status='pending'`, split `Online N · Walk-in N` by `booking_type` — both figures are live now that walk-ins also enter as `pending`), **Rescheduled** (`status='rescheduled'` running count) → an **appointment table** (`?action=list&key=<today_schedule|ongoing|pending|rescheduled>`, `apptTable(rows, key)`: Date · Time · Patient · Source badge · Dentist · Service · Status badge). Every drill-down renders the **plain appointment status** via `window.badge(r.status)` (there is no "Ongoing" badge). Each row is `data-view` → the shared global `appointmentModal()` drill-down.
- **Doctors on Duty** (`N / M` — active dentists whose **weekly working schedule** (`dentist_schedules`) puts them in the clinic today, over all active dentists. This is the physical-presence roster: **not** login-based and **not** "has an appointment booked today" (the old rule — the user changed it because the card means "who is in the clinic"). `dentistWorksOn()` in `functions.php`; **a dentist whose schedule grid is empty (or has nothing active today) counts as OFF duty** — an empty grid means "not working", changed 2026-09; only a DB error falls back to on-duty. **A dentist scheduled to work today can also be marked absent for just today** (`dentist_absences`, migration 040 — a sick day / emergency leave; see the one-off dentist absence paragraph in *Domain vocabulary*) without touching their recurring grid — this card, its drill-down, and every duty check elsewhere pick that up automatically), **Assistant Dentists on Duty** (`N / M` — dental-assistant roster rows with an `assistant_shifts` row for today, over all active roster rows) → a **people table** (`?action=staff_list&key=<doctors_on_duty|assistants_on_duty>`, `staffTable()`: Doctors → every active dentist, Type (In-House / Roaming badge from `users.doctor_type`) / **On Duty Today** (schedule-based badge, or a red "Absent today" badge when `dentist_absences` has a row for today) / Booked Today / Completed / Waiting / Last Active / a trailing action cell — **Mark absent** / **Mark present** for a dentist scheduled today (calls `schedules.php?action=set_absence`, optional reason on marking absent, then re-renders the drill + refreshes the card), or a muted "Not scheduled" label for one who isn't on the grid today at all; **Assistants → a read-only snapshot, not a control (2026-09, reworked)** — the drill now lists **only** assistants currently on duty (the query filters to `EXISTS (assistant_shifts row for today)`, rather than every active roster row with a badge), since an assistant manages their own duty status from their own dashboard's "My Duty Today" toggle; the admin's old **Mark on/off duty** button (`wireShiftToggles()`, `assistants.php?action=set_shift`) was removed outright from this drill. Columns: **Assistant** / **Status** (always a green "On Duty" badge, since every listed row is by definition on duty) / **Active Patients** (`COUNT` of today's visits this assistant has personally checked in — `checkin_assistant_id` — that aren't `completed`/`cancelled` yet; the same "still open" definition the dashboard's own **Ongoing Patients** card uses, just scoped to one assistant) / **Availability** (a green "Available" badge when Active Patients is 0, an amber "Busy" badge otherwise — new `available`/`busy` `BADGE_C` entries in `app.js`). `seen_ago` seconds come from `TIMESTAMPDIFF(SECOND, last_seen_at, NOW())` so a doctor's "N min ago" Last Active label is DB-clock-correct; null → "—").

The page script is a single IIFE (nav.js re-runs it) that clears its own `window.__opsTimer` on re-entry and on navigating away. Below the operational cards, **Revenue Summary** and **Service Usage & Demand** (`#svcCard`) are **two full-width, stacked rows** (`col-span-12` each, no `lg:col-span-6` split) — **not** side-by-side. This replaced an earlier side-by-side two-column layout (`lg:grid-cols-2 lg:items-stretch`, revenue tiles on `auto-rows-fr`, a `min-h-[calc(100vh-150px)] flex-1` viewport-stretch) at the user's explicit request (2026-09) — squeezed into half the row width, the grouped bar chart's `groupedBarChart()` SVG (7 or 6 date/month groups × up to 10 clustered service bars, ~840px wide) triggered its own `overflow-x-auto` horizontal scrollbar, which the user wanted gone. Stacking the two cards full-width gives the chart the whole row's width (~1000px+ on a normal desktop), comfortably fitting the SVG with no scrollbar. Per the advisor, the dashboard has **only** these panels; fill space by expanding them, never by adding a new section. (Before the donut, `#svcCard`'s slot used to hold a read-only Upcoming Appointments feed — `action=upcoming`, still live but unused.) **The metric cards carry no decorative pills** as of 2026-09 — the user had every micro-badge removed (the `+/-%` prior-period deltas, the `Live` / `Needs approval` status pills, and — with the whole card's 2026-09 removal — the one surviving `Online now` pill on Total Active Accounts).

**Revenue Trend line chart (2026-09, advisor feedback; cards removed and windows aligned to Service Usage 2026-09) — a "stock market trajectory"-styled line, now the Revenue Summary card's only content.** The card originally sat three fixed today/week/month revenue tiles (`#revenueCards`, backed by `api/reports.php?action=analytics`) above this chart; the user had those tiles removed outright (along with the now-dead `analytics` action and its own `action=revenue_list` drill-down — see the reports-page paragraph above), leaving the card as just a header row (`<h2>Revenue Summary</h2>` + the Daily/Monthly toggle, the same `flex-wrap gap-y-2` layout Service Usage & Demand's own header uses) and the chart. **The chart's Daily/Monthly windows were also changed to match Service Usage & Demand's exactly, and its size to match too** — Daily is always **the current calendar month, day 1 through its real last day** (no "Last 7 Days" default, no calendar-month `<select>`; the `#revTrendMonthPicker` dropdown and its `fillRevTrendMonthOptions()` were deleted outright) and Monthly is the rolling 12 calendar months, identical to `action=service_usage`'s own two views; `#chartRevTrend` carries the same `flex flex-1 min-h-[300px]` sizing as `#chartSvcUsage`. **`window.lineChart()` gained the same natural-width-vs-stretch split `groupedBarChart()` already had (2026-09, second pass)** — sizing the chart's box to match `#chartSvcUsage` wasn't enough on its own: the chart's old always-`width:100%` rendering squeezed all 30 Daily points into one fixed-width card, and the date labels overlapped into unreadable mush (screenshotted and flagged directly — "its still not like service usage"). `lineChart()` now takes an `opts.fill` flag, defaulting to `false`: Daily renders inside a `w-full overflow-x-auto` wrapper — exactly `groupedBarChart()`'s own non-fill behaviour — so a full calendar month of dates gets real, unsquished spacing and scrolls horizontally just like Service Usage & Demand's own Daily view; Monthly passes `{ fill: true, height: 420 }`, stretching full-width at the same taller height as Service Usage's own Monthly.
**A follow-up pass (2026-09, third round) fixed a deeper root cause the first fix only partly addressed** — the user reported the chart "still not like service usage," pointing at visibly larger left/right gutters than the bar chart's. The real bug: `lineChart()`'s viewBox width (`W`) was hardcoded to a flat `520` whenever `opts.fill` was set, then stretched to the container's real width (~1000-1100px) via `preserveAspectRatio="none"` — a ~2x horizontal-only scale factor that inflates *everything* inside proportionally, including the padding gutters **and the glyph shapes of every `<text>` element**, which measurably fattened the axis labels (confirmed by comparing rendered `getBoundingClientRect().width` of the same "Sep 2026" label in both charts — the line chart's came out ~1.7x wider than the bar chart's for identical text). `groupedBarChart()` never hits this because its own natural-width formula, driven by up to 10 clustered bars per category, already computes a `W` close to a typical card's real pixel width regardless of `opts.fill` — the stretch ratio it applies is negligible (~1.06x). `lineChart()` now follows the identical rule: `W` is **always** the natural, content-driven width (`Math.max(520, (n-1)*pointW + pad.l + pad.r)`, `opts.fill` never special-cased in that formula) — only the default `pointW` changed, from `48` to `90`, so that Monthly's fixed 12-point natural width (~1050px) lands close enough to a real full-width card that `opts.fill`'s stretch is nearly a no-op (verified: viewBox landed at `1054`px against a measured `1067`px container — a ~1.01x ratio, no visible distortion). Daily's natural width grows accordingly (~2600-2700px for a full month) but stays comfortably in the same order of magnitude as `groupedBarChart()`'s own Daily natural width (~2460px), so the two charts still feel like a matched pair when scrolled. `pad.l`/`pad.r` were also trimmed from their original `58`/`28` down to `46`/`32` (money/non-money) and `20` respectively — closer to `groupedBarChart()`'s own `32`/`14` (a peso label is wider than a plain integer count, so `pad.l` can't drop all the way to `32`), verified with `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements that the last point's own centered axis-label text ("Sep 2026") no longer clips past the card's right edge even at the new, tighter margin. `loadRevTrend()` calls **`api/dashboard.php?action=revenue_summary&view=day|month`** (no `month` query param any more) and renders the response with **`window.lineChart(points, opts)`** (extended, not replaced — it existed in `charts.js` since the Tailwind migration but had zero callers until this feature) into `#chartRevTrend`, then calls **`window.playLineChartIntro()`** for the entrance (see below). **Update 2026-10-02: `config/db.php` now calls `date_default_timezone_set('Asia/Manila')` (local + live), so PHP's clock matches the DB session's `+08:00`.** Live PHP had been UTC (local XAMPP Europe/Berlin), which stamped a morning payment's `paid_at` on the previous day and put it on the wrong Revenue Summary date; the two affected live payments were shifted +8 h. The note that follows predates this. **Revenue windows are still computed on the PHP clock (`date()`), not `SELECT CURDATE()`** — a deliberate exception to this app's usual DB-clock rule, because it must stay self-consistent with `payments.paid_at`, which `recomputePayment()` itself stamps via PHP's `date()` (see the clock-skew note elsewhere in this file); this is unlike the DB-clock convention `action=service_usage` uses for appointment-scheduling dates, even though the two endpoints' window shapes are now otherwise identical. Revenue is recognised the same way as every other revenue figure in the app: `COALESCE(DATE(paid_at), payment_date)`, `payment_status = 'fully_paid'`, summed as `total_amount - discount`; a bucket with no matching revenue is a real zero, not a gap, same zero-fill convention as Service Usage & Demand. Each point carries `key` (the bucket, `YYYY-MM-DD` or `YYYY-MM`), `label` (short axis tick, e.g. `"Sep 21"` / `"Sep 2026"`), `tip_label` (the fuller tooltip date, e.g. `"Sep 21, 2026"` / `"September 2026"`) and `value`.
`window.lineChart()` was extended (its markup, not its call signature) to: draw a **line** through every point (a straight-segment polyline by default — see below for why it isn't the smoothed curve this paragraph originally shipped with); give every point a **`.line-node`** circle (added to the shared `.donut-arc, .grouped-bar, .line-node` floating-tooltip delegate in `charts.js`, `tabindex="0"`, `data-tip`) so hovering or focusing a node shows exactly `"<date>: <peso amount>"` (e.g. `"Sep 21, 2026: ₱5,800"`) — this is deliberately the **only** place the exact figure is shown; there is no on-chart value label and no legend box (a single series needs neither — the card's own "Revenue Summary" heading already names it, matching the `dataviz` skill's single-series rule); and use a **unique `<linearGradient>` id per render** (`lg-line-N`, module-scope counter) since this chart can now render more than once on a page. **The tooltip/axis money formatting is deliberately whole-peso** (`pesoWhole()`, a small local helper — `'₱' + Math.round(v).toLocaleString('en-PH')`), not the shared `window.peso()`'s always-2-decimal convention used everywhere else (invoices, tiles, drill-down tables) — a trend line's job is the shape/direction at a glance, and centavos are clutter on an axis tick. `.line-node:focus-visible` in `tailwind.php` grows (`r`) and brightens (`filter: brightness(1.25)`) a tabbed-to node via plain CSS (see below for why the mouse-hover half of that rule is effectively dead code now). **Entrance animation (`window.playLineChartIntro()`, the line-chart counterpart of `playDonutIntro()`/`playGroupedBarChartIntro()`)** — the path draws itself in via a `stroke-dashoffset` sweep using the path's own real `getTotalLength()` (700ms), and the area fill fades in alongside it; skips entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Called on every render (page entry, refresh, or a Daily/Monthly toggle change) — this chart has no polling to guard against replaying on.

**`smoothPath()` switched from a plain Catmull-Rom spline to monotone cubic Hermite interpolation (Fritsch-Carlson), to fix a real rendering-artifact class of bug** — Catmull-Rom can bulge a curve past a local peak or dip it below a local valley when neighbouring values swing sharply in alternating directions (e.g. a low day sitting right next to a spike), which on a revenue line reads as the curve looping back on itself or dipping below the axis between two points. Monotone-cubic (the same technique behind D3's `curveMonotoneX` / Chart.js's "monotone" tension mode) is mathematically guaranteed to never over/undershoot past a segment's own two endpoints — implemented by forcing the tangent to `0` at any point where the neighbouring slopes flip sign (a local peak/valley), and a weighted-harmonic-mean tangent everywhere else.

**The Revenue Summary chart itself no longer uses that curve at all (2026-09, same-day follow-up)** — the user asked for a sharp, straight-segment ("jagged") line instead of a smoothed one, plus markers hidden until hovered. `lineChart()` now defaults to a new `straightPath()` helper (plain `M`/`L` commands — trivially immune to the overshoot class of bug above, since a straight segment between two points can't bulge past either endpoint); `smoothPath()`'s monotone-cubic curve is kept as an opt-in (`opts.smooth`) for a hypothetical future caller that wants the eased look, but has no current caller. Every `.line-node` renders at **`r="0"`** (invisible, no area of its own for a real mouse `:hover` to ever land on) instead of a small always-visible dot; `window.wireLineChartHover()` (the crosshair companion function documented above) is what actually reveals one — on `pointermove` it sets the previously-nearest node back to `r="0"` and the newly-nearest one to `r="6"`, so exactly one marker is ever visible at a time, tracking the crosshair. `.line-node:focus-visible` in `tailwind.php` still grows a tabbed-to node to `r: 6` via plain CSS regardless of this JS-driven state, since `:focus-visible` matches the actual focused element no matter its geometry — keyboard users still get a visible marker + tooltip on their own focused point. `window.playLineChartIntro()`'s old per-node "pop in" entrance step was deleted outright (not left dead) — animating the scale of a permanently zero-radius circle has nothing to show now that markers are hover-only; the path-draw and area-fade steps are unaffected.

**Full-width interactive crosshair (2026-09), `window.wireLineChartHover(container)`** — a companion function to `lineChart()`, the one piece that genuinely needs live JS rather than returned markup. `lineChart()`'s SVG now also includes a `.line-crosshair` — a single vertical dashed `<line>` (`stroke-dasharray="4 4"`, spanning the plot area's own top/baseline, `opacity="0"` at rest, `pointer-events: none` in `tailwind.php` so it never itself blocks hover) rendered right after the area/line but before the nodes. `wireLineChartHover()` (called once, right after `playLineChartIntro()`, from `loadRevTrend()`) wires a `pointermove` listener on the whole `<svg>` — not just each ~4px `.line-node`, a fiddly target on its own — that finds the nearest node by comparing the pointer's `clientX` to each node's own `getBoundingClientRect()` centre, snaps the crosshair's `x1`/`x2` to that node's `cx`, and calls the same shared `showTip()`/`hideTip()` the document-level `TIP_SEL` delegate already uses (both live in the same `charts.js` closure) so the floating tooltip tracks the crosshair — hovering anywhere across the chart's width shows the nearest date's exact figure, not just a precise hover directly over a dot. `pointerleave` on the SVG hides both. This is pointer-only by design (matches the request's own "on hover" / "cursor movement" wording) — keyboard users still get per-node tooltips via the existing `focusin`/`focusout` delegate, just without the crosshair line moving.

**Revenue Summary is click-to-drill too (2026-09)** — the user asked for it directly ("just like all in dashboard add a interactable when i click revenue summary"), matching the rest of the page's "every panel drills down" convention. `window.wireLineChartHover(container, onPick)` gained an optional second parameter: when given, a click anywhere on the `<svg>` resolves to the *nearest* point (the exact same `nearest()` lookup hover already uses — a real click landing precisely on a node is effectively impossible since every `.line-node` renders at `r="0"`) and calls `onPick(key)`; each node is also individually focusable regardless of its zero visible radius, so Tab + Enter/Space reads `e.target.dataset.key` straight off the focused node with no nearest-point search needed there. `lineChart()`'s `.line-node` markup now carries a `data-key` (the point's own bucket key, e.g. `"2026-09-21"`/`"2026-09"`) whenever the caller's `points` entries have one — a generic passthrough, same convention as `groupedBarChart()`'s `data-service-id`/`data-bucket`. `renderRevTrend()` passes `openRevTrendDrill` as `onPick` (only when the chart actually rendered — an empty-revenue period has no chart to click, so `onPick` is `undefined` then and `wireLineChartHover()` skips wiring the click/keydown listeners entirely), and a static caption below the chart (`#revTrendCaption`, "Click a point on the chart to see the transactions behind it.") — hidden via `renderRevTrend()` toggling its `hidden` Tailwind class whenever there's no chart to click — gives the same affordance hint Service Usage's own caption does. `openRevTrendDrill(key)` looks up the point's own label from the cached `lastRevTrend.points` for the modal title ("Revenue — September 2026" / "Revenue — Sep 21, 2026"), opens a `{wide:true}` modal, and fetches the new **`api/dashboard.php?action=revenue_detail&bucket=&view=`** — the line-chart counterpart of `service_usage_detail`, built the same way (validates the bucket shape, resolves it to a `from`/`to` date range for a day or a whole calendar month) but querying `payments` (not `appointment_services`) with the *identical* revenue-recognition rule the summary aggregate itself uses (`payment_status = 'fully_paid'`, `COALESCE(DATE(paid_at), payment_date)` between the window, summed as `total_amount - discount`) so the drill-down's rows always add up to exactly the figure the point showed. Rows render via a new `revenueTable(rows)` (Date · Time · Patient · Dentist · Service · Amount, each `data-view` → the shared global `appointmentModal()` like every other dashboard drill-down table) — `Service` falls back to a `GROUP_CONCAT` of the visit's availed procedures for a legacy walk-in with no `appointments.service_id`, the same fallback `historyLogRows()`/the revenue reports already use. Verified end-to-end with headless Chrome: clicking the Sep 2026 point (₱27,300) opened a 7-row modal whose rows are the real underlying transactions; clicking one of those rows opened the shared appointment modal ("Appointment #246"); tabbing to a Daily point and pressing Enter opened its own drill-down ("Revenue — Sep 6, 2026") with no mouse involved.

**A real bug in the very first version of this feature, caught immediately by the user testing Daily vs. Monthly clicks directly.** The modal's Date column showed `a.scheduled_date`/`a.time_slot` (the appointment's own visit date) instead of the transaction's actual revenue-recognition date — the two are not always the same day: a visit can be scheduled one day and its bill settled another (an installment paid off later, or a same-day visit billed just after midnight). Confirmed against a real row in the DB: `payment_id 74` (`appointment_id 274`) has `scheduled_date = 2026-09-20` but `paid_at = 2026-09-21 09:30:00` — clicking the **Sep 21** point on the chart correctly opened "Revenue — Sep 21, 2026" and the query correctly filtered to exactly that payment (`COALESCE(DATE(paid_at), payment_date) BETWEEN '2026-09-21' AND '2026-09-21'` matched it), but the row's own Date cell then displayed **"Sep 20, 2026"** — reading as if the modal had pulled the wrong day's transactions, when the underlying query had actually filtered correctly all along; only the *displayed* column was wrong. **Fixed by having `action=revenue_detail` select and return `COALESCE(DATE(p.paid_at), p.payment_date) AS revenue_date` and a pre-formatted `revenue_time` (`DATE_FORMAT(p.paid_at, '%h:%i %p')`, `NULL`/"—" when the row is on record only via the date-only `payment_date` fallback) instead of `a.scheduled_date`/`a.time_slot`**, and having `revenueTable()` render those two fields — so the Date/Time columns now always agree with the bucket the row was actually counted under, for both Daily (exactly one date) and Monthly (every date within that calendar month) alike. Re-verified after the fix: the same row now displays "Sep 21, 2026 · 09:30 AM", matching its clicked bucket exactly; a fresh Monthly-view September click shows all 7 transactions with distinct dates Sep 15 through Sep 21 summing to exactly ₱27,300, the same figure the point itself showed. (A first verification attempt of the Daily click produced a false negative — clicking at a node's raw, unscrolled `getBoundingClientRect()` position landed off the actual 1440px-wide viewport, since Daily's natural chart width is ~2600px and needs horizontal scroll; the fix was to scroll `#chartRevTrend`'s own `.overflow-x-auto` wrapper so the target node was genuinely on-screen before computing click coordinates — the same "measure the clipping wrapper, not the oversized element" methodology already documented elsewhere in this file for a transform/scroll-positioned track.)

**A second real bug, reported directly right after the first fix** ("when i click on daily and goes back on sep 1... for monthly a horizontal bar will pop up") **— the click-to-drill feature was fighting the resize-redraw feature (both 2026-09, added the same day) on every single click.** `openModal()`/`closeModal()` (`app.js`) toggle `document.body.style.overflow` to lock/restore page scroll while a modal is open — hiding/revealing the browser's own vertical scrollbar, which shifts every full-width element's real pixel width by that scrollbar's ~15-17px, `#chartRevTrend`/`#chartSvcUsage` included. `wireChartResize()`'s `ResizeObserver` (see its own paragraph above) had no way to tell this page-chrome noise apart from a genuine resize, so clicking a point to open its drill-down modal *also* tripped a mid-interaction chart redraw: Daily's chart rebuilt from scratch (a fresh `.overflow-x-auto` div always starts at `scrollLeft: 0`), visibly snapping the user's scrolled-over view back to day 1 out from under them; Monthly could render at the transiently-widened width while the modal was open, and since the container reverts once the modal closes, the two could mismatch and show a real, unwanted horizontal scrollbar for the brief window in between. **Fixed with one guard clause** — `wireChartResize()`'s `ResizeObserver` callback now returns immediately whenever `document.body.style.overflow === 'hidden'` (a modal is open), never touching the `widths` tracking map or scheduling a redraw during that window; once the modal closes and the scrollbar returns, the container's width is back to exactly what it was before the modal opened, which correctly compares as "unchanged" against what's already on record — so no catch-up redraw is needed on close either. Verified with headless Chrome against a page with a genuine vertical scrollbar (`document.documentElement.scrollHeight > window.innerHeight` confirmed true, and `#chartRevTrend`'s own `clientWidth` measurably grew from 1083 to 1098 the instant a modal opened, proving the scrollbar-removal mechanism was really exercised, not just theorized): a Daily chart scrolled to `scrollLeft: 1309` stayed at exactly `1309` through modal-open, past the 120ms debounce window, and after modal-close; a Monthly chart that needed no scroll (`needsScroll: false`) stayed scroll-free throughout the same sequence instead of growing one mid-open.

**A third issue from the same report, visible in a screenshot** — the shared floating tooltip (`charts.js`'s `tip()`/`showTip()`, used by every chart's hover/focus delegate, not just this one) had no viewport-edge awareness: centred horizontally and positioned entirely above the hovered point by fixed CSS classes alone, it could overflow past the browser's right edge whenever the hovered point sat near a chart's right edge — exactly the case for a trend line's *most recent* point, which is always the rightmost one, shown clipped in the reported screenshot ("September 2026: ₱27,300" pinned at the corner of the Revenue Summary card). **Fixed by clamping `showTip()`'s position after measuring the tooltip's own real `getBoundingClientRect()`** — if its right edge would exceed `window.innerWidth` (minus an 8px pad), or its left/top edge would go below `8px`, an inline `transform` nudges it back in bounds (`translate(calc(-50% + dx px), calc(-100% - 10px + dy px))`, only ever applied when actually needed — an ordinary, non-edge point's tooltip is untouched, still positioned purely by the base Tailwind classes). This is a shared fix: it benefits the donut/grouped-bar tooltips too, not just the line chart, since all three share the same `tip()`/`showTip()` pair. Re-verified with headless Chrome: hovering the same rightmost/topmost point that used to overflow now measures `overflowsRight: false` with its right edge landing exactly at the clamped boundary (`1432px` on a `1440px` viewport, an 8px pad), confirmed visually via screenshot.

**A follow-up, phrased in ApexCharts terms** (`chart.updateOptions()`, translated silently per the standing `requests-framed-as-react` convention — this is still the vanilla `lineChart()` above, not a real ApexCharts instance) **asked the two symptoms be closed more explicitly rather than relying only on the modal-open guard.** For Daily, the guard clause above is in fact the whole fix — there is no other code path that ever rebuilds `#chartRevTrend`'s markup on a click (`openRevTrendDrill()` only opens a modal and fetches its own row data; it never touches the chart), so scroll preservation was already correct — re-verified once more directly (dispatching a real `click` at a node scrolled to `scrollLeft: 1073`, confirming the drill-down modal actually opened — `modalTitle: "Revenue — Sep 13, 2026"`, `bodyOverflow: "hidden"` — and that `scrollLeft` stayed at exactly `1073` through the open, past the debounce window, and after close). Monthly's scrollbar had a real, separate root cause the guard didn't touch, though: `lineChart()`'s width was always `Math.max(minW, containerWidth)` — a *floor*, never a ceiling — and Monthly's natural 12-point `minW` (~1050-1060px with the money-axis padding) can land a handful of pixels wider than the card's real measured width on a narrower window or with the sidebar expanded, which pops a genuine, if small, horizontal scrollbar independent of any click or modal. **Fixed with a new `opts.fill` flag on `lineChart()`** (distinct from `groupedBarChart()`'s own same-named, differently-behaved flag — the bar chart's still only ever widens past its own minimum; this one is a hard ceiling): when set, `W` is capped to exactly `opts.containerWidth` — even below the natural minimum, trading a little per-point spacing for a guarantee — and the wrapper renders `overflow-hidden` instead of `overflow-x-auto`, so there is no scroll track to render even from a 1px rounding difference. `renderRevTrend()` passes `fill: true` only for the Monthly branch of `lineOpts`; Daily is untouched and keeps its natural-width, genuinely-scrollable rendering. Verified with headless Chrome across three emulated viewport widths (1440/1100/900px, the last two narrow enough that the un-capped formula would have produced `minW > containerWidth`): at every width the wrapper's class was `overflow-hidden`, `needsScroll` (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`) was `false`, and the rendered `<svg width>` matched the wrapper's `clientWidth` exactly (`1083`/`743`/`543`).

**The static "Click a point…" / "N patients seen…" captions under both charts were removed outright (2026-09, at the user's request)** — `#revTrendCaption` and `#svcUsageCaption` (the `<p>` elements below `#chartRevTrend`/`#chartSvcUsage`) and every line of JS that populated or toggled them (`renderRevTrend()`'s `cap.classList.toggle('hidden', …)`, `renderSvcUsage()`'s dynamic `cap.textContent = …` sentence, `loadSvcUsage()`'s error-path `cap.textContent = ''`) are gone; neither chart shows any caption text now, just the heading + toggle + chart. The per-point/per-bar tooltip (hover or focus) is unaffected — it's a different piece of UI entirely (the shared `charts.js` floating tooltip), only the always-visible static sentence beneath each card was cut.

**Service Usage & Demand gained the same full-width hover crosshair Revenue Summary's line chart already had (2026-09, phrased in ApexCharts terms — `xaxis.crosshairs` — and translated to this app's own vanilla-SVG `charts.js`, per the standing `requests-framed-as-react` convention; no ApexCharts here).** `window.groupedBarChart()`'s markup gained a `.grouped-bar-crosshair` (a single dashed vertical `<line>`, `stroke-dasharray="4 4"`, initially `opacity="0"`, spanning the same `pad.t..baseY` plot bounds the grid uses — 1:1 the same shape as `lineChart()`'s own `.line-crosshair`) and a `.grp-anchor` class + `data-tip` on each category's existing bottom axis-label `<text>` (one per date/month, already centred on its own bar cluster via `text-anchor="middle"`, so no new invisible markup was needed — the label itself doubles as the hit-test anchor). The new companion `window.wireGroupedBarChartHover(container)` mirrors `wireLineChartHover()`'s technique exactly: on `pointermove` anywhere over the `<svg>`, it finds the nearest `.grp-anchor` by comparing the pointer's `clientX` to each anchor's own `getBoundingClientRect()` centre (not the nearest individual `.grouped-bar`, since the crosshair should track the whole cluster, not any one series' bar within it), snaps the crosshair's `x1`/`x2` to that anchor's `x`, and shows the shared floating tooltip naming just that date/month; `pointerleave` hides both. The **existing per-bar hover tooltip is completely untouched** — it's the separate, pre-existing document-level `TIP_SEL` delegate keyed to `.grouped-bar` itself (exact service + count on that one bar), unaffected since `.grp-anchor` was deliberately left out of `TIP_SEL`. `modules/admin/dashboard.php`'s `renderSvcUsage()` calls `window.wireGroupedBarChartHover(box)` right after setting `#chartSvcUsage`'s `innerHTML` (unconditionally, unlike the animate-gated `playGroupedBarChartIntro()` call beside it — every render, including a resize-redraw and a Daily/Monthly toggle, replaces the whole `<svg>` and needs the listener re-attached, the same reason `loadRevTrend()` re-wires `wireLineChartHover()` on every one of its own renders). `tailwind.php` added `.grouped-bar-crosshair, .grp-anchor { pointer-events: none; }` (mirroring `.line-crosshair`'s own rule) so neither ever blocks a bar's own hover/click underneath. Verified with headless Chrome on both views: Daily (30 categories) correctly snapped to the last cluster ("Sep 30") and back to the first, tooltip text matching each; Monthly (12 categories) correctly snapped to a middle cluster ("Apr 2026") with a screenshot confirming the dashed line visually centred through that exact bar group; `pointerleave` correctly hid the crosshair again in both cases.

**Real bug found and fixed the same day, from a screenshot: the crosshair sat over blank space next to the actual bars on a sparse day, not through them ("it needs to be align to the bar").** Root cause: every category always reserves the full `groupW` width for up to 10 fixed series slots (one per active service), most of which render at zero height on a typical low-volume day — the crosshair was snapping to that abstract *reserved* group centre (the same x as the axis label beneath it), which only visually lines up with the real bars when roughly half the services are non-zero that day; with just 1–3 services actually having a visit, the genuinely colored bars can sit off to one side of that reserved span entirely. Fixed in `groupedBarChart()` by tracking, per category, the real left/right extent of only the bars with `val > 0` (`clusterMinX`/`clusterMaxX`), and computing a `clusterCx` = the midpoint of that actual bounding box — stored as `data-cluster-cx` on the `.grp-anchor` label (falling back to the old reserved-group centre only when a category has zero visible bars at all, since there's nothing else to compute from). `wireGroupedBarChartHover()`'s `move()` now reads `a.dataset.clusterCx` instead of the anchor's own `x`. Verified against live data: dumping every category's `clusterCx` vs. its reserved-centre `x` showed `diff: 0.0` for every symmetric/empty day and a real 3.5–10.5px shift for days with sparse, off-centre data (e.g. Sep 15: 3 visible bars spanning SVG x 1196–1230, `clusterCx` computed as exactly `1213.0`, their true midpoint — confirmed both by direct attribute inspection and a re-hover-and-check on the actual middle bar's own screen position) — and a follow-up screenshot shows the dashed line now threading directly through that day's small bar cluster instead of the empty space beside it.

**Follow-up (2026-09, same day, phrased in ApexCharts terms again — `tooltip.shared`/`intersect`/`followCursor`, `xaxis.crosshairs.position`)** — the crosshair-centering fix above didn't touch the tooltip's own position, which still tracked the raw pointer (`showTip(a, e.clientX, e.clientY)`), so it could visibly drift to whichever side of the bar cluster the cursor happened to be on even once the crosshair itself was correctly centred. Fixed by anchoring the tooltip's x to the crosshair's own just-computed screen position instead: `wireGroupedBarChartHover()`'s `move()` now converts the snapped `x` (the same `clusterCx`/reserved-centre value driving the crosshair) to a real screen coordinate via `svg.getBoundingClientRect().left + parseFloat(x)` — valid because `groupedBarChart()`'s SVG viewBox and rendered width are always identical (never `width="100%"`/`preserveAspectRatio`, see its own `W` comment), so one SVG user unit is exactly one CSS pixel — and passes that instead of `e.clientX` to `showTip()`; `e.clientY` is kept as-is (only the horizontal drift was the reported problem). Verified with headless Chrome: hovering deliberately off-centre within a category (at the anchor's own right edge, not its middle) still produced a tooltip whose `style.left` (`1171.5px`) matched the crosshair's real screen x (`1171.5`) exactly, rather than the raw hover point (`1187.5`) as it would have before.

**A real clipping bug, caught from a screenshot: the Monthly view's last axis label ("Sep 2026") had its final character cut off at the card's right edge.** Every axis label in `lineChart()` was rendered `text-anchor="middle"`, centring the text on its own point's x-coordinate — for an interior point that's fine (neighbours on both sides absorb the overflow), but the *last* point sits exactly at the plot's outer edge (`xAt(n-1) = W - pad.r`), so half of a centred label's width extends past that edge into `pad.r`'s margin; `pad.r` (22px) was sized for an ordinary label, not enough for "Sep 2026" at 12px font (~56-60px wide, ~28-30px half-width). This is independent of the `opts.fill` fix above — it's a labeling-geometry bug, not a width/scrollbar one, and existed on the un-capped natural-width rendering too (SVG's default `overflow: hidden` on the root element was already silently clipping it there; `opts.fill`'s tighter width just made it more consistently visible). **Fixed by anchoring only the first and last labels toward the inside of the plot** — `text-anchor="start"` for point 0, `"end"` for the last point, `"middle"` unchanged for every interior point — so the two edge labels grow inward from their point's x-coordinate instead of centring outward past it; no `pad.l`/`pad.r` change needed. Verified with headless Chrome: the Monthly view's last label (`"Sep 2026"`, `anchor: "end"`) now measures `right: 1338.5` against the SVG's own `right: 1360` — fully inside, confirmed both by direct `getBoundingClientRect()` comparison and a screenshot.

**Service Usage & Demand (2026-09, replaced the Revenue-by-Service donut)** — the donut only ever plotted the top handful of services by all-time revenue, which doesn't answer "which services are in demand right now, and on which days" the way the clinic owner needed; a **clustered/grouped bar chart** over the **whole active catalogue** (all ~10 services, always, even at a 0 count) does. **First built as a single-series chart with services on the X-axis and a one-day/one-month picker, then corrected the same day** — the user pointed out that layout couldn't compare volume across time, and asked for dates (or months) on the X-axis with one bar per service clustered under each. `#svcCard`'s header carries just a **Daily / Monthly** `role="tablist"` toggle (`.tab`, so it opts out of the app-wide button hover-lift meant for ghost buttons) — **no other control**. `loadSvcUsage()` calls **`api/dashboard.php?action=service_usage&view=day`** (**always the current calendar month, day 1 through its real last day** — e.g. Sep 1–30 in September, 28–31 categories depending on the month, server-resolved from the DB clock) or **`&view=month`** (**the last 12 calendar months**, ending with the current month) and renders the response with **`window.groupedBarChart(categories, series, opts)`** (replaced `window.vBarChart()` in `charts.js` outright, same day — a clustered vertical bar chart: one X-axis group per date/month, `series.length` thin bars side by side within each group, a numeric Y-axis gridded in fixed 5-count increments, and a colour-coded `<ul>` legend rendered **above** the chart naming every service; shares the donut's floating-tooltip delegate via a combined `.donut-arc, .grouped-bar` selector, so hovering or focusing a bar shows "`<Service>` · `<date>`: `N` Patient(s)"). Both views render with the same tighter `groupedBarChart()` bar/gap sizing (`barWidth:6, gapBar:1, gapGroup:12`, vs. the library's 8/2/18 defaults). **Monthly additionally passes `{ fill: true, height: 420 }`** so its chart stretches to the full width of the card and uses a taller 420px canvas — settled here after churning back and forth the same day (2026-09): tried (flagged as "too compact" from a screenshot) → stretched via a new `opts.fill` on `groupedBarChart()` (`width="100%" preserveAspectRatio="none"` on the `<svg>`, viewBox unchanged, mapping the coordinate system directly onto the container's real box) → reverted (`opts.fill` removed) after the independent X/Y rescaling was reported as visibly fattening and unevenly gapping the bars ("the months too stretch") → the taller-but-unstretched middle ground reverted too once the user saw it live ("change it back") → **`opts.fill` reinstated and Monthly's stretch restored** at the user's explicit final request ("make it back where the months are stretch"). `opts.fill` is opt-in on `groupedBarChart()` (a caller that wants undistorted natural sizing still can), not the only rendering path, so Daily (up to 31 categories for a full calendar month, still needing real horizontal scroll to stay legible) is unaffected and keeps its natural, non-stretched width. The endpoint (`api/dashboard.php`) returns a matrix, not flat rows: `categories` (`[{key, label}, …]`, one per bucket — `label` is `"Sep 21"` per day or `"Sep 2026"` per month) and `series` (`[{service_id, label, data: [count per category, same order]}, …]`, one entry per active service, `ORDER BY service_name` so colour assignment stays entity-stable), plus (day view only) `range_label` (e.g. `"September 2026"`, used in the empty-state text). Counts are **`COUNT(DISTINCT appointment_services.appointment_id)`** per `(service_id, bucket)`, joined through appointments with `status = 'completed'` (a dentist logged it as performed — the same "availed" semantics `service_revenue_list` used to use, just gated on completion rather than payment); the window's start/end dates are anchored to **`SELECT CURDATE()`** (the DB clock), never PHP's `date()`, the same convention as `dentalAssistantsOnDuty()`/`touchPresence()`/every other "what is today" calculation in this app. A service with zero matching visits anywhere in the window still gets a full `data` array of zeros (computed in PHP by iterating every requested bucket key against a `[service_id][bucket] → count` map built from the query, defaulting missing entries to 0), so every service keeps its own coloured series and its own legend entry regardless of demand. **Colours: the shared, CVD-validated `window.CHART_PALETTE` only has 8 hues, one short of the ~10-service catalogue** — reusing a colour for two services in the same clustered chart would make them indistinguishable, so this card extends it with two more hues **locally, not in the shared array** (`SVC_COLORS` in the page script: `CHART_PALETTE` plus a cyan `#0aa3b5` and a slate-grey for the 10th, last-alphabetically service, Restorative Dentistry) — chosen by manual hue-gap inspection, not run through the `dataviz` skill's `validate_palette.js`, since that script wasn't reachable in the session that added this; re-validate before relying on it for anything beyond this one dashboard card. **The slate started at `#475569` and was lightened to `#94a3b8` (2026-09)** — the user reported it was hard to see in dark mode, where the dark card background (`dgt-ink-800`/`900`) and the original dark slate had too little contrast for the bar/legend swatch to read clearly; the lighter slate-400 step stays legible against both the white light-mode card and the dark navy dark-mode one, confirmed via a real dark-mode screenshot. The card's caption line (`#svcUsageCaption`) reads e.g. "41 patients seen across all services, Sep 1 – Sep 30. Hover a bar for the exact count." **Daily-range history (2026-09, all same day):** the window churned through several shapes before landing here — 7-day rolling default → widened outright to 31 rolling days (Monthly to 12 months) → replaced with a "Last 7 Days / pick a calendar month" `<select>` dropdown → the default changed to the *current* month instead of 7 days → **the dropdown removed entirely**, leaving the single fixed rule described above with nothing to operate. Each intermediate shape (the `$_GET['range']`/`month`/`year` params, `month_options`, `fillSvcMonthOptions()`, `#svcDayRange`) was deleted outright rather than left dead once superseded. The whole card is no longer itself a clickable drill-down (it now hosts the Daily/Monthly toggle, so a card-wide click handler would fight with it); `svcRevTable()`, `foldSegments()`, `distro()`, and `openRevDrill()`'s `services` branch were deleted along with the donut. `api/dashboard.php action=service_revenue_list` and `api/reports.php action=analytics`'s `revenue_by_service` query were removed too — nothing else called them.

**Individual bars are clickable instead (2026-09)** — the user asked for this chart to be "clickable like other [cards] in dashboard," matching the rest of the page's "every panel drills down" convention even though the card as a whole can't (see above). `groupedBarChart()` in `charts.js` now stamps each `<rect class="grouped-bar">` with optional `data-service-id`/`data-bucket` attributes whenever the caller's `series`/`categories` entries carry `service_id`/`key` (a generic passthrough, not `#svcCard`-specific — any future caller gets the same hook for free by including those fields). `loadSvcUsage()` passes `service_id` through into each series and caches the last-rendered `categories`/series labels in `svcUsageCategories`/`svcUsageSeriesMeta` so a click can build a modal title synchronously, with no round-trip needed just to know which service/date was clicked. `wireSvcUsageBarClicks()` delegates click/Enter/Space on `#chartSvcUsage` to `openSvcUsageDrill(serviceId, bucket)`, which opens a `{wide:true}` modal titled `"<Service> — <bucket label>"` and fetches **`api/dashboard.php?action=service_usage_detail&service_id=&bucket=&view=`** — the same `(service_id, bucket)` join the chart's own aggregate query uses (`appointment_services` × `appointments` on `status='completed'`, `scheduled_date` between the bucket's day or calendar-month bounds), returning the individual completed visits behind that one bar's count (Date · Time · Patient · Dentist), each row `data-view` → the shared global `appointmentModal()` exactly like every other dashboard drill-down table. A bucket with zero visits (an empty bar, still clickable since every bar carries the attributes regardless of value) shows the same "No completed visits…" empty-state text used elsewhere on the page.

**Entrance animation (2026-09) — the bars themselves draw in, not just the card.** A first pass called the page's shared `staggerIn(box)` helper (the plain fade+rise used by the operational cards/revenue tiles) on the whole chart wrapper; the user clarified that wasn't what they meant ("no like the bars have animations") — they wanted the individual bars to animate, the bar-chart equivalent of the donut's `playDonutIntro()`. `charts.js` gained **`window.playGroupedBarChartIntro(container)`**, called by `loadSvcUsage()` right after setting `#chartSvcUsage`'s `innerHTML` (replacing the `staggerIn()` call): every `.grouped-bar` rect grows from `scaleY(0)` to `scaleY(1)` via the Web Animations API, anchored at the shared baseline by `transform-box: fill-box; transform-origin: bottom` (set permanently, inertly, on `.grouped-bar` in `tailwind.php` — every bar's own bounding box already touches the baseline at its bottom edge, `y + height === baseY`, zero-value bars included, so this needs no per-bar coordinate math). Staggering is **by category, not by individual bar** — every bar sharing a `data-bucket` (the attribute added for the click-drill-down above) animates together, with each bucket's own start offset by a small delay (`min(45, max(10, floor(420 / bucket-count)))` ms, so a 30-bucket Daily view and a 12-bucket Monthly view both finish drawing in within roughly the same ~0.4-0.8s regardless of how many categories there are — staggering ~300 individual bars one at a time, at up to 10 services × 31 days, would take far too long). The legend `<li>` items fade/rise in afterward, same as the donut's legend. Skips entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`. This chart has no 30s poll to guard against replaying on, so every render (page entry, browser refresh, or a Daily/Monthly toggle click) gets the entrance unconditionally. Verified with headless Chrome by patching `Element.prototype.animate` to pause every `.grouped-bar` animation on creation and scrubbing its `currentTime`: a bar with a real SVG `height` of `30.7` measured `0` at t=0, `~29.8` at 50% through its own (staggered) animation, and `30.7` once finished, with no lateral shift.

**Daily defaults to showing the latest date, not day 1 (2026-09) — Service Usage & Demand and Revenue Summary both.** Daily's whole-current-calendar-month view needs real horizontal scroll (see above), and the chart's `overflow-x-auto` wrapper defaulted to `scrollLeft: 0` on every load — putting the oldest day first and today's data, the rightmost category/point, off-screen until the admin manually scrolled all the way right. `renderSvcUsage()` (Service Usage & Demand) and `renderRevTrend()` (Revenue Summary) both now set `scroller.scrollLeft = scroller.scrollWidth` (the browser clamps this to the true max scroll offset, so it's a safe one-liner) right after building the chart's markup, for the Daily view only — Monthly renders with `opts.fill` and has no scrollbar to begin with, so this is a no-op there. **The assignment is deferred one `requestAnimationFrame`, not applied synchronously right after `innerHTML=` — a real race, not a hypothetical one.** `loadRevTrend()` and `loadSvcUsage()` fire independently at the bottom of the page script with no ordering guarantee between their two separate `api()` fetches; a first synchronous-assignment version worked in isolated testing but was confirmed, via repeated headless-Chrome runs against the live page, to intermittently leave *one* of the two charts back at `scrollLeft: 0` — consistently the same one within a given warm-cache run, but which one flipped between cold-start runs, matching a load-order race rather than a fixed bug in either function alone. Wrapping the scroll assignment in `requestAnimationFrame(() => {...})` — which runs after the browser has committed layout for the frame, regardless of which of the two charts' fetches resolved first — was verified to fix it reliably across 6 consecutive fresh-profile runs with no failures, after the synchronous version had already been confirmed to fail non-deterministically. Runs on every Daily render (initial page load, a Daily/Monthly toggle back to Daily, and a resize-triggered redraw), since a redraw fully replaces the chart's DOM anyway and any prior manual scroll position is already lost with it — always re-landing on "latest date visible" is the more useful default than leaving it wherever the rebuild happens to reset to.

**Both charts' `opts.fill` (the `preserveAspectRatio="none"` stretch) was removed outright and replaced with true container-width-aware coordinate sizing (2026-09, "optimize for presentation scaling and high-resolution displays")** — the user's request was phrased in ApexCharts terms (`chart: { width: '100%', redrawOnWindowResize: true, redrawOnParentResize: true }`) and translated silently into this vanilla-SVG library, per the standing `requests-framed-as-react` convention. The old `opts.fill` mechanism (documented in the two paragraphs above) rendered at a flat natural width and stretched it to 100% via `preserveAspectRatio="none"` — functionally fine on an ordinary desktop, where the natural width and the card's real width happen to be close, but wrong in spirit for "high-resolution displays": on a genuinely wide container (an ultra-wide monitor, a 4K screen, a projector) the stretch ratio grows well past 1.0x and the old axis-text-fattening bug (see the "third round" paragraph above) would reappear. `lineChart()` and `groupedBarChart()` now both accept **`opts.containerWidth`** (a measured real pixel width the caller passes in) and compute `W = Math.max(minNaturalWidth, opts.containerWidth || 0)` — every coordinate (point positions, bar/group positions, grid lines, axis-label anchors) is generated directly against that final `W`, and the SVG's `width` attribute is always that same literal pixel number (never `"100%"`, never `preserveAspectRatio`). Since the viewBox and the rendered box are therefore always identical, there is no stretch and no distortion *at any width* — a chart on a 4K/projected display genuinely spreads its points/bars out to fill the space (via real coordinate placement, not a post-hoc scale), rather than being stretched or sitting undersized with dead space to the right. `groupedBarChart()` spends the extra room by widening `gapGroup` (the space between category clusters) proportionally; `lineChart()` needs no extra math since its point-spacing (`xAt()`) was already computed proportionally against `W`. Both keep a legibility floor: `W` never drops below the natural minimum needed for readable per-point/per-category spacing, so Daily's ~31-category view still scrolls horizontally on an ordinary desktop (there's rarely spare width to give it there) and only genuinely benefits from `containerWidth` on a much wider display — exactly the "presentation / high-resolution" case the request named. Monthly's natural minimum (12 points, or 10 services × 12 months) is already close to a typical card's real width, so in practice it now fills the card exactly (`W === containerWidth`, zero scroll) on any normal desktop too.

**`redrawOnWindowResize`/`redrawOnParentResize` translated to a `ResizeObserver`, not a `window.resize` listener** — a window resize is only one of the two ways these cards' width actually changes in this app; the sidebar-collapse toggle (see the app-shell section above) shrinks/grows `#appShellContent`'s margin with no `resize` event of its own, which is exactly what "redraw on parent resize" means. `modules/admin/dashboard.php`'s page IIFE observes `#chartRevTrend`/`#chartSvcUsage` directly with one shared, debounced (120ms) `ResizeObserver`, stored on `window.__dashChartResizeObserver` (same nav.js-re-entry-safe convention as `window.__opsTimer` — `stopChartResize()` disconnects any prior observer before `wireChartResize()` creates a fresh one, so navigating away and back never leaves a stale observer watching detached elements). A redraw re-renders from a small **local cache** of the last successful fetch (`lastRevTrend`/`lastSvcUsage`, plus their own `renderRevTrend(animate)`/`renderSvcUsage(animate)` functions) rather than re-hitting the API — a resize is a re-layout of data already in hand, not a reason to refetch it. The `animate` flag is `true` only for a genuine data load (page entry, a Daily/Monthly toggle click) and `false` for a resize-triggered redraw, so resizing the window never replays the draw-in entrance animation; `wireLineChartHover()` is re-wired on every render regardless (each render replaces the `<svg>`, so any listeners on the previous one are gone with it), while the click-drill-down listener on `#chartSvcUsage` is unaffected since it's attached once to the persistent container and delegates dynamically.

**A real bug from this same change, caught by the user directly ("why did u remove the animation?") the very first time they reloaded the dashboard after it shipped.** `ResizeObserver` fires on *any* change to an observed element's box, not just ones caused by something external — and each chart box starts at its CSS `min-h-[300px]` showing a one-line "Loading…" placeholder, then grows taller once the real chart (SVG + legend) is inserted, since the box's height is otherwise `auto`. That self-inflicted height change fired the observer again ~150ms after every single genuine load, and the debounced callback ran `renderRevTrend(false)`/`renderSvcUsage(false)` — silently replacing the just-drawn, animated chart with a byte-for-byte identical, non-animated one. The entrance animation *did* play; it was simply overwritten a frame or two later by its own resize handler before anyone could see it. Confirmed by instrumenting `Element.prototype.animate` and `Element.prototype.innerHTML`'s setter via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` (patched in before any app script runs) and tracing a real page load: `#chartRevTrend`'s `innerHTML` was written twice, ~145ms apart, both times with the identical `9977`-byte string. **Fixed by only reacting to a genuine *width* change** — `wireChartResize()` now tracks each box's last-seen width in a `WeakMap`, seeded with its real `clientWidth` *before* `observe()` is called (so the callback's always-fires-once initial invocation also compares equal instead of registering as a false resize), and only clears/restarts the debounce timer when an entry's rounded `contentRect.width` actually differs from what's stored. A content-driven height change no longer trips it at all. Re-verified with the same instrumentation: each chart's `innerHTML` is now written exactly once per load, and a genuine viewport resize (tested by emulating 1440px → 2200px mid-session) still correctly redraws Monthly's chart to the new container width (`1083px` → `1843px`).

**Axis/grid text bumped from `font-size="9"`/`"10"` to a flat `font-size="12"`** on both charts (grid value labels, category/date axis labels) as part of the same request ("so text and vector paths scale cleanly when projected or zoomed") — since there is no longer any `preserveAspectRatio` stretch to distort it, a `12` in the SVG now renders as a true, literal 12px at any resolution, permanently. `lineChart()`'s padding grew slightly to fit the larger glyphs without clipping (`pad.l` 46/32 → 50/36 money/non-money, `pad.r` 20 → 22, `pad.b` 26 → 30); `groupedBarChart()`'s similarly (`pad.l` 32 → 34, `pad.b` 30 → 34). Verified with headless Chrome at 1440px (both Monthly charts render at exactly the card's `clientWidth` with zero horizontal scroll, axis text measuring a true `12px` via `getComputedStyle`) and again after resizing the emulated viewport to 2200px with no navigation (`ResizeObserver` redraw fired, both charts' `<svg width>` attribute updated to match the new, larger card width exactly, still zero scroll) — confirming the fluid-expansion and redraw-on-resize behaviour both work as intended.

`appointments.status`: `pending` → `approved` → `completed`, plus `cancelled` / `rescheduled` (5-value ENUM; the admin Appointment page filters and counts each one by its plain name). **`scheduled` was renamed to `approved` end-to-end (migration 027, 2026-09)** to give every role one status vocabulary — Pending / Approved / Completed / Rescheduled / Cancelled. There is no "no-show" status. The `cancelled` status is reached by the **`cancel` action** (patient cancelling their own booking, or staff on a patient's behalf — reason required, re-added 2026-09 to match the reference doc) and by the receptionist's `decline` (rejecting a still-pending request).

**Front-desk check-in (migration 027):** `appointments.checked_in_at DATETIME` is stamped **only** by the receptionist's **Check In** button on the Front Desk *Approved Appointments* table (`api/appointments.php?action=check_in` / `undo_check_in`) — approving a request never auto-checks-in, walk-ins included (the user's rule: approve and check-in are distinct front-desk steps). The **Check In** button opens a small modal with an optional **Assisting Staff** picker (today's on-duty dental assistants) → stored on `appointments.checkin_assistant_id` (migration 038); `undo_check_in` clears both columns. `check_in` sets the assistant tag whenever one is passed (`COALESCE`) without re-stamping an already-set arrival time. Check-in does **two** things: it **gates the dentist's Log Procedure** button (`api/treatments.php` also backstops it — re-confirmed still enforced both client- and server-side in a 2026-09 pass, see below), and it keeps the patient on the admin dashboard's **Ongoing Patients** card until the cashier bills them (`checked_in_at IS NOT NULL AND status <> 'cancelled' AND NOT EXISTS (payment with payment_status IN ('fully_paid','partially_paid'))`, today — so an installment downpayment also clears them). It does **not** change the appointment status or its badge — there is **no "Ongoing" status/badge** (removed 2026-09). `window.apptStatusBadge(row)` / `window.opStatus(row)` are now thin aliases of `window.badge(status)`. `api/appointments.php?filter=checked_in` returns the dentist's live chair queue (unused).

**Perform Procedure gate re-confirmed, and NULL-assistant check-ins now auto-assign the least-loaded on-duty assistant (2026-09).** A pasted spec asked to (1) "re-enforce" the check-in gate on Perform Procedure and (2) auto-resolve a blank Assisting Staff pick at check-in instead of leaving it `NULL`. Point (1) needed **no code change** — both halves of the gate were already in place and working (`modules/dentist/dashboard.php`'s Queue row disables the **Perform Procedure** button with `title="Check in the patient first"` whenever `a.checked_in_at` is empty, and `api/treatments.php action=log` independently 422s server-side with *"The front desk has not checked this patient in yet."* when `empty($a['checked_in_at'])`) — this reverses this file's own earlier claim, from the "Ready for Treatment" simplification pass, that the checked-in gate on Perform Procedure had been dropped; it had since been put back (not reflected here until now) and this session just re-verified it, it did not re-add it. Point (2) is new: `check_in` (`api/appointments.php`) now branches on whether the front desk left `assistant_id` blank **and** the appointment doesn't already carry a `checkin_assistant_id` (so a repeat `check_in` call on an already-tagged visit never silently reassigns it) — when both are true it calls the new **`leastLoadedOnDutyAssistant()`** helper (`includes/functions.php`), which joins `dental_assistants` to today's `assistant_shifts` and orders by the same "checked-in-and-not-yet-completed/cancelled today" `active_patients` subquery the admin dashboard's own Assistant-Dentists-on-Duty drill-down already uses, tiebreaking on the lowest `assistant_id` (same convention as `assignAppointmentDentist()`'s own least-loaded tiebreak). If nobody is on duty it returns `null` and `checkin_assistant_id` simply stays `NULL` (direct-to-doctor) — no error. The activity-log line names the auto-picked assistant with an explicit "(auto-assigned, least loaded)" suffix so an admin reviewing the History Log can tell an auto-pick apart from a receptionist's own manual choice. **The auto-assigned assistant is alerted directly, not left to notice on their own next page load** — if that assistant's `dental_assistants` row has a linked `user_id` (migration 058; a legacy login-less roster row simply gets no notification, since there is no account to notify), `check_in` calls `notify()` with a new, dedicated title, **"New Patient Assigned"**, naming the patient, the time, and the service. This reuses the existing generic `notify()`/`notifications` table — no schema change — but needed a **third, narrow exception to the patient-only notification bell** (the same kind already carved out for the roaming-dentist on-call bell): `includes/header.php` now also renders `#asstBellBtn`/`#asstBellPanel` for the `dental_assistant` role, unread-scoped to `title = 'New Patient Assigned'`; `assets/js/app.js` mirrors the on-call bell's `loadOnCallBell()`/`refreshOnCallBellCount()` pair as `loadAsstBell()`/`refreshAsstBellCount()`, and clicking a dropdown item marks it read then scrolls to (or navigates to + scrolls to) `modules/dental_assistant/dashboard.php`'s **Today's Schedule** card (`#schedSection`, new id) with the same `oc-flash`-style pulse (`.asst-flash`) the dentist dashboard's `#queueSection` already uses for its own bell handoff, via the same `sessionStorage` flag pattern (`dgt_scroll_asst`). There is no dedicated "see all" notifications page for this role (out of scope for this pass) — the panel's footer link just goes straight to the dashboard. Verified end-to-end against the real DB and a real HTTP call (not just a code read): seeded two on-duty assistants where one already had an active checked-in patient, called the live `action=check_in` endpoint with no `assistant_id` as a real receptionist session, and confirmed the *other*, genuinely less-loaded assistant was picked, `checkin_assistant_id` was set, the activity-log line read correctly, a repeat call on an already-tagged visit does not reassign it, and a linked-login assistant's pick correctly inserts a `notifications` row (an unlinked legacy roster pick correctly inserts none). All seeded rows deleted afterward.

**Same-day follow-up: manual assistant picks now notify too, a "Total On Duty" summary line was added to the admin drill-down, and the assistant bell got a near-real-time toast.** A second pasted spec refined the first pass: (1) *"When reception checks in a patient and manually selects an Assistant Dentist, send a real-time toast/banner notification... to that assistant's dashboard"* — previously `notify()` only fired on the auto-assign path; a manual pick just silently tagged the visit. `check_in` now captures `$prevAssistantId` (the appointment's `checkin_assistant_id` *before* this call) and, for both the manual-pick and auto-assign branches alike, notifies whenever the resolved `$asstId` differs from `$prevAssistantId` **and** the caller isn't the assistant checking themself in — this covers a fresh manual pick, a deliberate reassignment to someone new, and the least-loaded auto-assign, while a redundant repeat `check_in` call re-confirming the same already-tagged assistant correctly notifies nothing a second time (verified directly: simulating the identical repeat call computed `$asstId === $prevAssistantId` and skipped the `notify()` branch). `dentalAssistantsOnDuty()` (`includes/functions.php`) now also selects `da.user_id` (additive — every existing caller that only reads `assistant_id`/`full_name` is unaffected) so the manual-pick validation loop can resolve the notification target without a second query. (2) **"Real-time" toast, not just an unread badge** — `assets/js/app.js`'s `refreshAsstBellCount()` (already polling every 30s) now tracks the highest `notification_id` seen so far in a module-scope `asstSeenMaxId`; the very first poll after a page load only establishes that baseline (so opening the app never toasts for assignments that already existed), but any poll after that whose max id has grown fires `window.toast()` for each newly-arrived "New Patient Assigned" notification, oldest-first. This is genuinely a page-agnostic toast (fires on whatever page the assistant happens to be on, not only their dashboard), which is a strictly wider reach than the spec's literal "to that assistant's dashboard" wording — treated as the better reading, matching how the app's other near-live surfaces (the operational dashboard cards, the on-call bell's own badge) all define "real-time" as "as fresh as the next poll," since this app has no websocket/push layer.
